**Snapshot:** Post-Session 17 · Campaign Day 56 (Saturday; in-world 2026-12-12), plus about a day in a pocket dimension that the hosts say Earth will not feel · Rank 3.5

## **Reading Notes**

**This is an out-of-character analytical document.** It uses mechanics, GM-side lore and player knowledge freely. Where a judgement depends on information no PC possesses, it is marked **⟨OOC⟩**. Where a judgement depends on information only *some* characters hold, the holder is named.

**How to read an entry.** All 510 placements now have the same shape:

1\. **Name** \`score\`

- **All-time.** The standing, campaign-wide case for the score.
- **Session 17: 7.0 → 7.5 (+0.5).** The shift since the last edition, and why.
  * **All-time** is the character's standing file on that axis across Sessions 1–16: the sheet, the traits and backstory, the repeated behaviour, and the incidents that set the level. **Session 16's detail has been folded into it**, and earlier sessions compressed, so that the file reads as one case rather than a stack of revisions.
  * **Session 17** states the shift as numbers, measured from the Session 16 edition's score (for the new 1.15 Heroism, from its retroactive Session 16 baseline): `S16 → S17 (change)`, or *holds at* when the score did not move. It then gives the Session 17 evidence and the reasoning for the size and direction of the move, or for the hold.
* **On the §2 risk axes** a more negative number means more exposure, so the label adds *exposure up* or *exposure down*.
* **Edge is a returning member this revision, with one session of file.** His *All-time* item is that file: his backstory and his first fight, which he fought on a sheet he has since rebuilt. Where his Session 17 number moved because of the rebuild rather than play, the shift carries **⟨Re-spec⟩**, and the item says what changed on the sheet. Read every Edge score as still provisional; two sessions is a short file.

**Ranking convention:** Position 1 = most of the named quality. Where a low score is desirable (burnout risk, enemy targeting, legal exposure), position 1 = highest risk, and the axis header says so explicitly. Positions run 1–5. Where two characters share a score, the printed order is the tie-break and the axis carries a note: on the Logic-driven non-combat axes, ties between Intrepid and Edge are broken on Logic, and other ties are broken on the record, with the reason stated.

**Confidence markers:**

* Orderings drawn from repeated logged behaviour are stated plainly.
* *Closest call* explains the split where the logs support two defensible orderings.
* *Volatile* marks a ranking that would move on a single die roll, GM ruling or piece of unconfirmed lore.
* **⟨Flag⟩** marks a score that depends on something the document cannot see.
* **⟨Ruling⟩** cites a house rule or roster ruling.
* **⟨Mechanical flag⟩** marks a sheet-level caveat.
* **⟨Re-spec⟩** marks a movement caused by Edge's rebuild rather than by play.
* **⟨Advance⟩** marks a movement caused by Oracle's newly declared Rank 3.5 advances.
* **⟨Retcon⟩** marks a correction to something the Session 16 edition reported from the end of Session 16 that the log has since withdrawn.
* **⟨New axis⟩** marks 1.15 Heroism, added this revision. Its Session 16 figures are a retroactive baseline, scored on the record through Session 16 with the retcon applied.

**Damage notation.** Damage multipliers are written *multiplier + add* per the roster convention: `7+7` is a ×7 multiplier with +7 applied after the multiplication, not a flat bonus of 14. Expected values use a Marvel-die mean of 4.33 (a roll of 1 counts as 6) and exclude Fantastic doubling, edges, assists and Focus investment. Edge's Elemental Blast adds +1 to his damage bonus for every 2 Focus spent, like Intrepid's Smash; the table in §4.10 shows the base line.

**The roster did not change. Two sheets did.**

* **Edge rebuilt himself.** Between sessions his player replaced the marksman's kit — Agility 7, Accuracy 4, Stopping Power, Double Tap, Suppressive Fire, Slow-Motion Dodge, Situational Awareness, the Cutting Edge and his Healing Factor — with Force elemental control run off Ego. He now has **Ego 7 with Discipline 4 (×7+7), Elemental Blast (Force) as his Signature Attack, Elemental Burst (Force), Supernova (Force) and Lightning Actions**, on Melee 6 with Brawling, Sturdy 3, Uncanny 1, Wisdom and Heightened Senses 2. His abilities read Melee 6, Agility 0, Resilience 3, Vigilance 2, Ego 7, Logic −1; his defences 19/19/16/13/18/18; H-DR 3, F-DR 1. His role is now *Kinetic Force Artillery & Disciplined Vanguard*, and his power summary describes *"Catastrophic Invisible slashing force producing precise unseen cuts at range and sweeping storms of blades that carve through everything around him at once."*
  * **What it buys:** the team's second area attack, the heaviest single-target output per round on the roster, a guaranteed Marvel on initiative, and Ego 7 — level with Lock-Step for the highest social ability score on the team.
  * **What it costs:** his healing factor, his dodge, his Suppressive Fire and every point of Agility.
* **Oracle's two Rank 3.5 advances are declared**, after three sessions undeclared: **Agility 4 → 5 and Accuracy 1 → 2**, the last two items on his Rank 3→4 plan. His line is now ×5+5 and his physical defences 18/18/16. Neither advance is the ego- or logic-based power that projects strength, or Uncanny, so the four ⟨Flag⟩s the last edition carried on §4.2, §4.5, §6.4 and §7.3 resolve to *no change*: the counter-build is still open. The roster lists him at Rank 3.5 with all five Rank 3→4 items taken.
* **The mentor tag is settled.** Edge's roster tags now read *Mentor (Oracle / Barbara Gordon)*: the Oracle who found him online and called the Birds was Barbara Gordon, not the team's Oracle. The ⟨Flag⟩ the last edition carried on §1.8, §2.6, §5.12 and §5.15 is closed.

**One axis was added.** ⟨New axis⟩ **1.15 Heroism**, in §1: the willingness to take a risk or pay a cost for someone else — the public, a teammate, or an enemy. Its Session 16 figures are a retroactive baseline, and every Session 16 aggregate in §10 is restated on 102 axes so that the comparisons stay like for like. Its order is the same at both points — Oracle, Intrepid, Edge, Quasar-Force, Lock-Step — and the only rise on it in Session 17 is the newcomer's.

**The session is the story of this revision.** At the end of Session 16 the team was not at home and briefing Wonder Woman, as the last edition reported. ⟨Retcon⟩ It was at a staging point in the next building, where Oracle's module *"makes him feel weird"* and he passed out, and Lock-Step, held up only by Let's Go, went down with him. They woke in a military bunk room in a pocket dimension, *"selected for the newest episode of arena ball,"* and *"elected by a representative of your world"* — *"Minji Lin aka Firefly aka the tiny terror aka Death from Nowhere."* Every outside line failed inside the first hour:

* **The drones** did not respond.
* **Guy Gardner's communicator** answered *"The dimension you're trying to reach is currently unavailable."*
* **Lock-Step's teleport** found its target out of range.
* **His hexed telepathic link** to Blitzkrieg found no mind to reach.
* **The mother box** could not reach the Source, and said so, a letter at a time, ending in *"Help."*

The hosts gave the team 24 hours of rest, a maze and a colosseum, and set it against Earth X357's Teen Titans:

* **Facade**, a power copier who had already copied Oracle.
* **Pink-Shift**, a shape-shifter who heals.
* **Capricorn**, a horned brick.
* **Four-Fold**, a four-armed telepath.
* **Briar Patch**, a plant controller.

The hosts promised to return the team *"in whatever state you end up in, precisely at the moment and place that you left."* **The fight paused at the start of Round 2.**

**The newcomer asked the question this document has carried for two revisions.** No Titan had objected on the record to the Destined errand — Lock-Step's private request in Session 15 that a villain team kill a detainee. In Session 17 the member who had been on the team for an afternoon asked: *"if they're really killing people that's kind of our problem too right?"* The answers set the session's character scores:

* **Lock-Step:** *"deaths we're not worried about right now. We're trying to save the world first,"* and *"we can either save and protect our righteousness or actually save people's lives."*
* **Quasar-Force:** *"One thing I'm very confident on is that the destined will kill every man and woman in that lab, whether the progenitor is there or not,"* and then *"we needed to save the world and we were short on hands."*
* **Oracle** vouched for Lock-Step's account of the stakes — that the Justice League wants them to save the world: *"There was a time I doubted Lockstep, and he's preaching the gospel"* — and did not object to the killing.
* **Intrepid:** *"I get what you're saying."*
* **Edge,** later, as a rule for the arena that Lock-Step accepted: *"We follow the rule when it's easy and when it's hard. Otherwise, it's not a rule, it's a mood."*

**The lead held, to within half a hundredth.** Oracle +3.895 against Quasar-Force +3.463 is a lead of 0.432, against 0.427 last revision restated with Heroism (0.394 as published); both fell by about four hundredths, for different reasons. **Edge rose 0.367, to +0.980, and 86% of the rise is play rather than the rebuild.** He is now third on both headline composites (the 95 non-fame axes), so the score-based and position-based methods agree on third place for the first time. One hundred and thirty placements moved, 62 of them Edge's (31 of those are the re-spec).

* **Oracle** moved on 18 axes, five of them his advances. He emptied his battery to 5 of 180 getting the two teammates without healing factors on their feet, and then said the sentence that moves him: *"I still believe in saving people. I just think that most of the people telling us what to do don't know what they're doing."* In the arena a copy of him beat him with his own Point-Blank Parry.
* **Quasar-Force** moved on 19, twelve for the worse. He drew a one-minute penalty for ripping a nullifier out of a wall, which froze him at the door. He told another universe's Titans that he will not age, where his father works, and that *"the crooked one"* works in his building. The box he calls Stella asked him for help, and he could not reach her.
* **Lock-Step** moved on 18, fourteen for the worse. He defended the errand to the newcomer as doctrine, refused to rehearse a formation, and held the team at the door. He stayed with the frozen Quasar-Force (*"I just don't feel comfortable leaving our youngest"*) and gave Earth X357 his real first name.
* **Intrepid** moved on 13, eleven for the better — the largest net gain among the four veterans. He was the first into the uniform, called the rest the team took, and worked out at the door that *"time is a factor in this competition."* Then he argued with Lock-Step and walked on without him.
* **Edge** moved on 62. He knocked out his opponent in the Marvel round with two Force blasts and then sat down in the stands. He fed everyone, and pushed for a team maneuver until he gave up (*"I give up, I completely give up"*). He also punched a teammate who had asked to be punched, with a full combat punch, for 15.

**Principal evidence gaps:**

* **The retcon.** Some of the end of Session 16 was withdrawn. **Gone:** Oracle's post-fight heals on Lock-Step and Quasar-Force, the trip home, and the debrief with Wonder Woman. **Kept:** Dragonborne's flight, Edge's rear guard, the extraction (run on Let's Go), Blitzkrieg's withdrawal, the destroyed device and the villains left behind. Every Session 16 claim built on the withdrawn part is corrected where it sits and marked ⟨Retcon⟩.
* **The paused fight.** Session 17's combat evidence is one Marvel round and one round. It is scored as recorded, and nothing in §4 moves on it by more than half a point except through the sheets.
* **Who took them, and why.** *"Firefly"* is unknown to the team. Whether the abduction is connected to the hive's Session 16 order to *"collect"* the team is unknown. ⟨Volatile⟩
* **Earth X357.** Its Titans are opponents, not enemies. Their mentor, *"Batman,"* used to be *"Raven"* and is a chaos mage. The pairings were mostly theirs, and Facade picked Oracle.
* **The return promise.** If the hosts keep it, nothing on Earth moved while the team was away, and the 24-hour clock from Session 15 stopped with them. This document takes the promise as stated.
* **Two Rank-4 powers at Rank 3.5.** Lightning Actions and Supernova both list Rank 4 as a prerequisite and sit on Edge's Rank 3→4 track, which the campaign appears to allow. ⟨Mechanical flag⟩ Scored as printed.
* **Point-Blank Parry** appears on Oracle's power list. This document had never recorded it, and it was used against him by his copy.
* **Nightwing and Batman are off screen for a fourth session.** No report to anyone was possible from the pocket dimension, and the retcon removes the one report the last edition thought had been made.
* **Wonder Woman as the team's de facto mentor** is still the user's reading. The GM has not declared it, so this document treats it as practice rather than appointment.
* **The roster's poster-prompt section** still describes Edge's old kit (*"Spatial-severing ranged weapon attacks and suppressive fire"*). It is ignored.

**Scoring.** Every entry carries a cardinal score alongside its position, because ordinal rank alone cannot tell a rout from a photo finish. **Three native scales are used, chosen by what each axis actually measures**; the full rationale is in 10.0:

* **Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`** — Low means *absent*, never *harmful*.
  * Capability and exposure:
    * §3.6–3.9 – Team Internals, Command Chair & Pecking Order
    * §4 – Power / Ability – Combat
    * §5 – Power / Ability – Non-combat
    * §6 – Capability Robustness & Conditionality
    * §7 – Power / Ability – Overall
    * §8 – Fame (Exposure)
  * Anchors:
    * 0 none, completely absent capability
    * 5.0 competent baseline for a Rank 3 street-level hero
    * 8.5 elite for the tier
    * 10 best plausible at Rank 3.
* **Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`** — These have a real negative pole: a person can be an active liability, not merely lacking.
  * Psychology, discipline, standing and opinion:
    * §1 – Psychology, Morality, and Discipline
    * §3.1–3.5 – Team Internals: internal reputation, influence, trust, cohesion, and low operational support burden
    * §9 – Reputation / Opinion
  * Anchors:
    * +10 maximal asset
    * 0 genuinely neutral
    * −10 maximal liability.
* **Risk `−10.0 to 0.0`** — Always signed negative, because exposure is only ever a cost.
  * Threat exposure:
    * §2 – Enemy Risk
  * Anchors:
    * 0 = none meaningful
    * −10 = maximal, operations already underway.

510 scores total (102 scales times 5 PCs).

**Score Quick Recognition Pattern:**

* §2 – Enemy Risk is always negative, **`−10.0 to 0.0`** Risk.
* Everything else – If the score has a sign, it is **`−10.0 to +10.0`** Valence. Otherwise it is **`0.0 to 10.0`** Magnitude.

## **1\. Psychology, Morality, and Discipline**

**Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`**

*Session 17 is the most character-heavy session since Session 15: a day of enforced rest in a captor's bunk room, an argument about the Destined errand, an argument about a formation, an argument at a door, and a short staged fight. Section 1 moved on 29 placements, nineteen of them among the four veterans — more than any other section. It also gains a fifteenth axis, 1.15 Heroism (⟨New axis⟩).*

### **1.1 Current Psychological Stability**

1. **Oracle** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** The most settled person in the building: a purposeful role, accepted structure, and until this revision no running quarrel with any authority. Session 14 was the test — held in an inescapable grab, drained from 180 Focus to 3, unable to heal a teammate one-shot in front of him — and he called Wonder Woman himself and reported the failure plainly. Eidetic Memory is the hazard that never shows: he does not get to forget the bisection, the jars or the body he failed to revive. Session 15 recorded the first weariness in his voice (*"It's no different than Nightwing"*). Session 16 tested him physically: a 72-point Banging Heads put him at −2, his healing factor got him up, and he ran back in on a sliver of Health — *"Drop me again, it was worth it."* **Stability is what he showed; weariness is what he said.**
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.5.** He was the first to act in the bunk room (*"Let's get everybody up"*), reported his own state honestly (*"My battery's about gone now"*; *"my power is broken"*), and gave the arena the steadiest line in it: *"I just want to get through this and all of us turn out without anybody getting hurt."* **The weariness acquired a target:** *"I still believe in saving people. I just think that most of the people telling us what to do don't know what they're doing."* That is the first running quarrel with authority on his file, and it is scored where it costs, in 1.3, 1.14 and §9. His behaviour all session was the calmest in the room. Holds.
2. **Edge** `+3.0`
   * **All-time.** Composed from his first line in Session 16 (*"I don't want to break your hope"*) to his last stand, when a glitch bomb put him at exactly 0, his healing factor got him up, and he held off Blitzkrieg alone for a round. Against it, the heaviest identity load on the roster: constant headaches since the powers arrived, eyes that glow, white hair, and a legend that he is a monster reborn that once *"cut down armies."*
   * **Session 17: holds at +3.0.** The chronic symptom appeared in play for the first time — *"Where are we, guys? Wow, my head hurts"* — and then he was the most constructive person in the room for a day: a plan, food for everyone, and *"Okay, don't lose hope... There's always a way out."* He also said what holding himself together costs: *"I practice my concentration a lot because if I don't, things could get bad."* The roster's rewritten profile says the same thing about him — *"actively restrains the lethal impulses of his past life"* — and no longer describes the question as open. At the door, after an argument he could not win, came *"I give up, I completely give up."* **Composure that takes constant work is the score he already has.**
3. **Quasar-Force** `+2.0`
   * **All-time.** His composure has been a fixture of the campaign, and part of it is not his. The mother box holds a *"Peter Pan think happy thoughts"* baseline in its host and damps the panic-fear and anger that would impair his logic. **The famous calm is partly a second party's thermostat**: a pencil sketch of Azimuth overrode it for five minutes in Session 14. He worked out mid-attack that the panic was not his, recovers absurdly fast, named the box Stella in Session 15, and said *"I might end up better off dead"* without visible distress. Session 16 knocked him to −5 and then to −30, the deepest knockdown on his file, with no panic recorded.
   * **Session 17: holds at +2.0.** **The thermostat was tested from its own side.** In the pocket dimension he had *"trouble concentrating"* through *"something about the buzzing,"* and when he finally reached the box after several hours, she spelled out that she could not reach the Source, that the dimension was isolated, *"I am still with you. Don't be afraid,"* and then *"Source—no! No! No—"* and *"Help."* He failed the Ego roll to comfort her, and she withdrew. What the log records of him afterwards is the same boy — *"think happy thoughts!"*, *"Well, we can be friends!"* — and either his calm is now his own or the box is still damping it from withdrawal. Nothing in the log tells the two apart. Holds; the risk is scored in 1.2 and 2.6.
4. **Intrepid** `+1.0`
   * **All-time.** Iron Will and Fearless are on the sheet, and for most of the campaign his interior surfaced only in asides — the Session 10 monologue and its self-medication ladder. Session 14 put it into words as a fear of being discarded; Session 15 held the first forward-looking line he has said (*"Not yet anyway"*). Session 16 was the most punishing fight on his file — his Sturdy stolen, down twice — and when offered an exit he refused it.
   * **Session 17: holds at +1.0.** Two voices, evenly matched. The practical one put the uniform on first, called the rest and worked out that the penalty meant *"time is a factor."* The fatalistic one said *"We're basically going to lose,"* *"We just lost the fight, but it's okay,"* and *"I give up too. I'm gonna keep going. Screw it."* Nothing about losing control. Holds.
5. **Lock-Step** `−4.5`
   * **All-time.** Mechanically the most armoured mind in the party, and psychologically the most defended by avoidance. He is an orphan who says he is *glad* to have no parents, fills his time with dates and parties, distrusts adults by default, and has Berserk on his sheet. Session 15 put the most load on him of any session, and he stayed lucid. Session 16 opened with the longest anti-adult statement on his file and then the first explanation he has ever given of himself — *"Adults being adults and actually being helpful. Once I get that back, I think I'll be a bright-eyed kid just like you... But until then, this is me"* — followed at once by *"Maybe I just need another party."*
   * **Session 17: holds at −4.5.** The most visibly rattled member of the bunk room: *"My brain went on vacation,"* *"I'm freaking out right now,"* *"Oh fuck,"* and a request to be punched to find out whether he was dreaming. Told who had sent them, he ran the anti-adult file again (*"another failed adult... Are they all just free balling"*), and ended on *"I feel like we have too much to do."* In the arena he asked another universe's Titans whether their adults were useless too, got *"yeah, pretty useless"* in unison, and turned to Quasar-Force: *"Did you hear that?"* That is confirmation, not repair (*"once you grow up, you know, it's just bad"*). Against it: lucid all day, no Berserk, and the warmest decision of his session, staying with the frozen youngest. **The structure is unchanged.**

### **1.2 Psychological Resilience vs Future Trauma (Trauma Processing & Recovery Rate)**

1. **Quasar-Force** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** The best demonstrated recovery curve on the team. Stabbed from 60 Health to −12 in Session 14, he was pacing the air within the hour. The roster explains the curve, and the explanation is not entirely reassuring: the box is deliberately building his resilience to trauma and panic. **The resilience is real, it is engineered, and the engineer has PTSD.** Session 15 put that half under expert care. Session 16 showed the curve between two knockdowns: kept upright by Let's Go at −5, he healed on Karma, went to cover and was back at work.
   * **Session 17: +7.5 → +7.0 (−0.5).** The second recovery of Session 16 is still untested — he woke from −15 only when Oracle put 60 into him. **What Session 17 tested was the engineer.** Cut off from the Source in an isolated dimension, the box panicked mid-sentence and asked him for help, and he could not reach her. For seven revisions this axis has said half of his resilience is her work; this is the first session in which she needed more support than she gave. Still first, by half a point.
2. **Oracle** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** A steady base, a purposeful role, Determination, and caretaking that protects him. The fracture line this axis has named since Session 9 is specific: *if Endo is seriously hurt on Oracle's watch, Oracle is the one who breaks.* Session 14 came as close as it could without arriving. In Session 16 it arrived — Endo at −5 and then −30 — and he had the tools: Let's Go for four rounds, 15 Health in, and his own recovery from −2 in between. **He has still not talked about Session 14.**
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.5.** The fracture line again, in its quiet form: Endo lay at −15 through the abduction, and Oracle's first act on waking was to put 60 into him and 60 into Edge, leaving himself 5 of 180. Nothing was said about Sessions 14 or 16. What is new is the coping style — the problem is the people above them (*"most of the people telling us what to do don't know what they're doing"*) — which is new on his file and is scored in 1.3. Holds.
3. **Edge** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** His backstory is a trauma with a clean before and after: an assault near him, the attacker's weapon sliced to pieces, then the headaches, the glowing eyes and the white hair. **His response was the healthiest one available — he went looking for help**, and trained *"all-out"* with Black Canary when it came. In Session 16 he took 102 damage, went to exactly 0, got up and held a doorway.
   * **Session 17: +3.0 → +3.5 (+0.5).** Abducted to another dimension on the day he joined, he did what his backstory says he does and looked for structure: a plan (*"We can train for at least three of those 24 hours"*), food for the room, hope (*"There's always a way out"*) and a method (*"one problem at a time"*). The one disclosure he made is the load: *"if I don't, things could get bad."* **The coping is active, outward and healthy; the cost is permanent.**
4. **Intrepid** `+1.5`
   * **All-time.** Iron Will, Fearless, Enduring Constitution, and a real willingness to take hits so others don't. Session 14 showed how he metabolises defeat: by lowering expectations. The trauma he is least equipped for is still the one most likely to arrive — he has come within one die roll of killing ordinary humans three times — and the only coping he has ever described is the Session 10 ladder from aspirin to buying off the street. Session 16 brought the first power ever stolen from a Titan, and he recorded no reaction to it.
   * **Session 17: holds at +1.5.** Defeat arrived as a forecast rather than an event: *"We're basically going to lose,"* and *"if the powers that be tell us we lost, then we lost."* That is the Session 14 method — expect less, and it can't hurt — used before the fight instead of after it. Holds.
5. **Lock-Step** `−5.5`
   * **All-time.** Lowest, and the mechanics agree with the fiction: Berserk is a hardwired route from trauma to violence, avoidance is his default, and every pressure on him delivers the same message, *you are dangerous and you don't belong.* Session 15 gave him the first admitted cost (*"it would hurt my soul"*). Session 16 gave the first accurate account of the wound — adults who were meant to be *"actually being helpful"* and weren't — and then *"Maybe I just need another party,"* and a one-shot from 60 to −24.
   * **Session 17: holds at −5.5.** The wound named in Session 16 was reopened by the loudspeaker (*"another failed adult"*) and closed by agreement from another universe. Taken from a fight that had one-shot him into a dimension he could not leave, he asked to be punched, was punched hard, and talked about the Protection Hex instead of the fight: *"I definitely don't ever want to take that off."* Processing: none. Recovery: fast and theatrical, as always. Holds.

### **1.3 Operational Longevity / Low Burnout Risk**

*(Position 1 = most sustainable.)*

1. **Oracle** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** He sleeps, eats, cooks, patrols, reports and accepts the structure he is given, which removes the largest single source of teenage-hero attrition. **For six revisions his one real risk was load, not morale.** He is the medic, the cook, the comms relay, the overwatch and Quasar-Force's unappointed guardian. Session 14 drained him to 3 Focus; Session 15 recorded the first doubt; Session 16 named him *"our plan guy"* and made him the only healer in a fight that put all five Titans on the floor.
   * **Session 17: +6.5 → +6.0 (−0.5).** **Both halves of burnout showed up in one session for the first time.** Exhaustion: *"My battery's about gone now,"* 5 of 180, and most of the rest period asleep. Cynicism: *"I still believe in saving people. I just think that most of the people telling us what to do don't know what they're doing,"* and, of Nightwing's briefing of the newcomer, *"So now you know the kind of stuff we got to put up with."* **The morale entry is now on file.** Still first, on a tie.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** Intrinsically motivated to an unusual degree, with no physical attrition curve: pristine cellular maintenance, frozen ageing, and a full 60 Health back in ten hours of sleep. **The failure mode is self-maintenance, not motivation**: with structure imposed from outside he is fine. Session 15's six days of building were restorative; Session 16 cost him time at −30 rather than motivation.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.0.** His motivation was the most durable thing in the room — *"We kind of lost the last two missions. We got to try not to fail this one,"* and *"Better just some of us than none of us."* What he lost was structure: no drones, no parts, and a rest period spent on a box that answered *"Help."* Holds.
3. **Intrepid** `+2.5`
   * **All-time.** He loves the tank role, is good at it and heals between fights, so his physical attrition is low. The risk is psychological accumulation behind the physical durability, which surfaced out loud in Session 14. Session 16's 290 damage, and his refusal to leave, are both the tank's motivation working and exactly the accumulation this axis watches.
   * **Session 17: holds at +2.5.** He did what keeps a tank going — *"It's time we need to meditate. We need to sleep. I think we got a fight coming up"* — and the accumulation came out as fatalism (1.2). Holds.
4. **Edge** `+2.0`
   * **All-time.** The case for him is structure — two mentors, deliberate training — and the case against is the body: *"constant headaches"* since the powers arrived, a chronic symptom tied to the power itself, and grades that *"were already bad"* before training took the time.
   * **Session 17: holds at +2.0.** The item to watch was logged: *"my head hurts,"* on waking. The structure case got stronger (he asked for training time) and the attrition case got a second item, the one he volunteered — constant concentration practice *"because if I don't, things could get bad."* ⟨Re-spec⟩ His combat loop is no longer cheap (§4.3), which is a cost of the job rather than of the person. And at the door, *"I give up, I completely give up."* The items cancel.
5. **Lock-Step** `−2.0`
   * **All-time.** The only member whose attrition risk comes from his relationship with adults: a file of structural grievances, school to the legal minimum, and *"keep it street level."* What changed across Sessions 13–15 is that the grievances kept being answered — pay, Wonder Woman's commitment, a ring and a succession — and kept coming back. Session 16 had both halves at full volume: the longest grievance on file, and then the *"if it all goes to hell"* job done on the last of his Focus.
   * **Session 17: holds at −2.0.** *"I still felt like we already had a lot to do, and now I feel like we have too much to do"* is the first time he has described the load rather than the grievance. His view of the arena — *"this is like a side mission... we still have a mission mission after the side mission"* — is the motivation that matters, holding. Holds.

*Closest call:* Oracle over Quasar-Force on a tie at `+6.0`, broken on structure: Oracle slept when the battery emptied; Quasar-Force spent the rest period on a box in distress.

### **1.4 Personal Discipline Outside of Combat**

1. **Oracle** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** Structured mornings, breakfast for the house, patrols on schedule and reports filed. What sets him apart is that he holds a line under social pressure. The only deduction in sixteen sessions is the Session 12 all-nighter, managed through the proper channel. Session 14 added a correctly managed recovery under medical orders.
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.5.** The session's day of downtime he spent the right way: heal the two who could not wake, report the battery honestly, sleep. Holds.
2. **Intrepid** `+4.5`
   * **All-time.** First up every morning, oatmeal, out on patrol while still wounded, and he defers to team decisions without sulking. Against him: no initiative, no questions when invited to ask them, and a private drift toward self-medication.
   * **Session 17: +4.0 → +4.5 (+0.5).** The member who did what the situation required, first and without fuss. He was first into the uniform — *"That's just exploitation,"* and then *"Actually, that might be my new uniform from going forward"* — and the one who called the rest the team took. **He passes Quasar-Force.**
3. **Quasar-Force** `+4.0`
   * **All-time.** Rule-following but not self-regulating, and the gap between the halves defines his score. On duty he is excellent; off duty he needs structure from outside. Session 14 was four tasks at once and a sedative. Session 15 was the reverse: an order of work stated three times and three builds delivered, with the Watchtower supplying the structure.
   * **Session 17: +4.5 → +4.0 (−0.5).** **The off-duty half, with a bill attached.** Looking for parts, he found the hidden cameras and the power-nullification projectors beside them, then pulled one out of the wall with telekinesis — *"Player B3, purposeful damage of facilities, penalty one minute"* — and began opening it after the warning, until Lock-Step told him to put it in the box. The penalty froze him at the door when the countdown ended. **Curiosity outran the rules, and the team paid for it in time.**
4. **Edge** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** The one off-duty fact in his Session 16 file ran both ways: he trained *"all-out"* with Black Canary, and his grades *"were already bad"* before that. On arrival he asked for the plan, then for the situation, which is the right order.
   * **Session 17: +2.5 → +3.5 (+1.0).** A full day off duty, and it reads like discipline. He told the team how he prepares (*"I practice my concentration a lot"*), asked for three of the 24 hours to train a formation, and fed the room. He also moved from *"I will not do what the speakers are saying because I don't like to follow a strangers saying what to do"* to *"for now, I think we should do what it says"* as soon as the facts were in. The punch is scored in 1.9.
5. **Lock-Step** `−7.5`
   * **All-time.** He skips school, ignores summons, once posted a sticky note saying to contact him only if someone was dying, sedated a teammate twice, concealed an artifact from his mentor, and treats breaking and entering as unremarkable. Session 15 added sleeping through make-up classes and streaming a Justice League station without asking. **Nearly all of his self-directed effort goes into his own audience.** His one off-duty contribution in Session 16 was a briefing for the newcomer that was wrong on both facts it asserted.
   * **Session 17: holds at −7.5.** Both halves at once. He was the one who stopped the penalties: *"Just put it in. First off it already said further penalties."* He also turned down the newcomer's request to train (*"We don't really practice it"*), slept in his gladiator uniform, and at the door refused to move until somebody told him the rules: *"I just like to have you know the rules and what we're supposed to do said before I engage and waste my time."* Holds.

### **1.5 Personal Discipline in Combat**

*Session 17's fight is the shortest on record — a Marvel round and one round before the session paused — and it was a staged one-on-one bout under the hosts' thirty-second minimum.*

1. **Oracle** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** He opens nearly every engagement by demanding surrender, holds actions rather than burning them, stays in position, reassesses targets on precognitive input, and heals rather than chasing kills. Session 14 was output under total neutralisation. Session 16 was the plan executed — a super-Marvel stun on the agreed target and Let's Go where it was needed — against one serious lapse: knocked out in round two, he ran straight back in at a sliver of Health.
   * **Session 17: +8.0 → +7.5 (−0.5).** He said the right thing before the bout — *"I don't plan on getting any closer"* — and his first action was the right one: Suppressive Fire on Pink-Shift for 15. Then, swapped next to Facade with his copy beside him, he made a melee attack: Melee −1, against an opponent carrying his own Point-Blank Parry. She parried it into 34 damage and a stun; his Return Fire took 15 Focus off her first. **The ranged specialist walked into his own reaction.** The second lapse in consecutive sessions.
2. **Edge** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** A complete first fight in Session 16. He named the target before contact and the plan was built around it. He shot and then moved, spent actions assisting at the moments that mattered, chose the device over a body when the device was the objective, and finished holding a door.
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.0.** A disciplined six seconds. He took the opponent who chose him (*"I guess that leaves you with me, silver hair"*), blasted her twice with Lightning Actions, knocked her out, and stepped out of reach into the stands. He also declined the suggestion to cheat the pairings with area attacks (*"I know when I steal your opponent"*). His rewritten profile promises *"an absolute refusal to perform for wanton bloodsport,"* and sitting down once the job was done reads as exactly that. Facade's reaction stunned him on the way. Holds.
3. **Quasar-Force** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** The best target-discriminator on the team, and his objective play from cover is textbook. The defining deduction is Session 14, when he left concealment for an artifact and one action took him from full Health to −12. Session 16 was that lesson applied: hit before he could act, he went to cover and fought from there, and a phaser found him anyway.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.5.** The right target. He took the enemy healer — the shape-shifter who had just brought Four-Fold back from −29 to 1 — and threw him out of the arena into the staging room for 28. Briar Patch's branches restrained him in reply. Holds.
4. **Intrepid** `+5.0`
   * **All-time.** Willing discipline and indiscriminate tools. He takes positioning suggestions without ego and tells teammates when to take an opening. Against him: a grab that has done nothing in two sessions, and a stomp that does not distinguish a goddess from a security guard. Session 16 was his cleanest fight in three sessions: Banging Heads on the right two, Smash only with an assist behind it, and one stomp that reached only an enemy.
   * **Session 17: holds at +5.0.** *"Let's go AOEs. Let's go,"* and a few lines later, *"Just be careful because you know AOEs affect everyone in the area"* — the reminder he needed in Session 13, now given to others. In the bout he accepted Capricorn's challenge, took a Marvel ram for 72, got up and swung. Holds.
5. **Lock-Step** `−0.5`
   * **All-time.** The record against him is severe: two Berserk episodes, teammates teleported out of combat without their consent, a blind teleport alone into a room of ten monsters, a proposed arson and a captive beaten conscious. Against it, clean combats from Session 11 onward. In Session 16 he changed the item that had defined this axis for him and asked before moving anyone: *"Who is comfortable where they are at and who wants to move a little bit."*
   * **Session 17: 0.0 → −0.5 (−0.5).** No Berserk, and a 42-point Chain Strike on the opponent who volunteered for him. Against: before the bout he proposed disguising area attacks as one-on-one — *"just pretend like you're trying to fight... 1v1, but just use AOE"* — against opponents who were playing it straight. And after his strike he teleported to the stands and drew a penalty that froze him for a turn. The log gives no reason for the penalty; *in, strike, out* is his habit, and in this arena it cost a turn.

### **1.6 Operational Security Discipline**

1. **Oracle** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** Textbook instincts without formal training. He shut down internal discussion in front of the Destined, keeps his reporting channel clean, and treats a teammate's misconduct as something a supervisor needs to know. In Session 14 he was the only person in the room who did not say a Justice Leaguer's civilian name aloud.
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.0.** In a facility with cameras in every room — bathrooms included — and in front of another universe's Titans, he gave away nothing. Holds.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+5.5`
   * **All-time.** The team's most active security practitioner and one of its two largest leaks. He built the code rotation, rebuilt the drones' security, and in Session 15 built the signal mask and the teleport shield. **The leak is a habit: he answers before deciding whether the asker should have the answer.**
   * **Session 17: +6.5 → +5.5 (−1.0).** **The habit, on camera, to the opposition.** He found the hidden cameras himself and looked for implants. Then in the arena he told Earth X357's Titans:
     * that he will not age — *"Guy Gardner said I'm not going to grow older anymore"*;
     * that his team knows a *"Ravenwing"* — Briar Patch: *"Man, you're giving away all the tells up there"*;
     * that *"Wonder Woman kind of gives us missions now"*;
     * and that *"my dad's a scientist, he works at S.T.A.R. Labs. He's not the crooked one that works in our building."*

     When his own team said that last one was news, he said *"I told people, I don't keep that a secret."* The circlet stayed on, and he kept the one fact he does conceal: that the nanosuit can come off, which he lets everyone believe it cannot. **Discipline about his hardware; none about himself.**
3. **Intrepid** `+4.0`
   * **All-time.** Secure by having nothing to spill and a Secret Identity to protect. In Session 15 he was streamed as the future Superman in his hero identity only.
   * **Session 17: holds at +4.0.** He gave nothing and found the introductions baffling (*"I don't get it"*). Holds.
4. **Edge** `+3.0`
   * **All-time.** A public identity, *"not very well known,"* and no lapses in his first session; no practices on file either.
   * **Session 17: +2.5 → +3.0 (+0.5).** The first practices on file. On waking he used his senses to check whether anyone was watching. He gave Earth X357 his code name and nothing else (*"My code name is Edge. A pleasure"*). His first instinct towards the voice in the wall was distrust. Small, and all in the right direction.
5. **Lock-Step** `−4.5`
   * **All-time.** Personally the hardest member to trace and institutionally the most corrosive: a concealed artifact, *"Nightwing can't be trusted,"* team decisions routed around. **Session 15 was the largest single-axis fall in the document's history (−5.0)**: a livestream of the Watchtower, a broadcast surveillance operation, his real name confirmed by text to an unknown sender, a telepath invited into his memories. Session 16 broadcast nothing.
   * **Session 17: holds at −4.5.** One lapse and two good instincts in five minutes. The lapse: *"Actually, you know, you could call me Eric. It's not like we'll ever meet again,"* said on camera to a team with a power copier in it. The instincts: he phased his hand through Facade's rather than touch it (*"I can't trust nobody"*), and she turned out to copy powers; and asked whether his powers were good, he said *"Oh, no, they're horrible,"* a reflex he explained to himself as learned from facing too many power thieves. Holds.

### **1.7 Civilian Life Stability & Maintenance Load**

*How stable the non-hero life is, and how much overhead it imposes. High = stable and low-maintenance.*

1. **Intrepid** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** Secret Identity, school, and nothing else on the board. The absence of complications is the entire score, and he has held first since Exemplar II left.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.0.** Nothing touched it. If the hosts keep their promise, no time passed at home.
2. **Oracle** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** Stable inside the tower and structurally unable to live a normal civilian life outside it. **Extreme Appearance means he cannot walk into a shop unremarked**, and the roster records his civilian identity as *lapsed*.
   * **Session 17: holds at +3.5.** Nothing changed.
3. **Edge** `+1.5`
   * **All-time.** A public identity with low recognition, a school record that was poor before the powers arrived, and a visibly changed body. He lives at the tower, in Exemplar II's old room, and nothing on file describes his family. **Positive because there is still a civilian life to protect; low because almost nothing about it has been described.**
   * **Session 17: holds at +1.5.** The roster now calls him *"a soft-spoken teenager,"* and his civilian life is still undescribed. The one new fact is a maintenance load that travels with him — the concentration practice. Holds.
4. **Quasar-Force** `−1.0`
   * **All-time.** Publicly identified, with parents at a known address in Slate Hill, a family in court-adjacent therapy, a personal maintenance load nobody else carries, and ⟨OOC⟩ ageing that has stopped.
   * **Session 17: holds at −1.0.** He told another universe where his father works. Nothing reached Slate Hill. Holds.
5. **Lock-Step** `−3.5`
   * **All-time.** No parents, no fixed address and a truancy record he keeps adding to, filled with dates and parties rather than anything load-bearing. **The instability is the thing he says he is glad about.** The weekly stipend is the one structure he asked for; Session 15 added a surname, a named best friend and a public channel.
   * **Session 17: holds at −3.5.** Asked how he prepares for a fight, he said the call usually finds him *"out doing somewhere, maybe a family barbecue or you know, hanging out with my friends."* ⟨Volatile⟩ A family barbecue is a new item in the file of an orphan who says he has no parents. Unexplained, and not scored.

### **1.8 Adult / Guardian / Peer Support & Safety Net**

1. **Quasar-Force** `+9.5`
   * **All-time.** By a wide margin the best-supported person on the team. He has two engaged parents, a family therapy arc, a father at STAR Labs, Nightwing watching since before he had a codename, Guy Gardner, and Oracle as an unappointed guardian. Session 14 added Iron-Side's surgery, Wonder Woman's personal sanctuary offer and Doctor Taleria; Session 15, a founder who came in person when called and a Green Lantern who picked up mid-fight. **He is the only member for whom the League's support is personal rather than institutional.**
   * **Session 17: holds at +9.5.** The network did not change, and **for the first time none of it could be reached.** Guy Gardner's communicator said *"The dimension you're trying to reach is currently unavailable"* three times, and Lock-Step chose that moment to tell him Guy had *"left you on red."* His answer — *"He really cares about the creepozoids. I think he would maybe even bring a bunch of his friends over too"* — is a fourteen-year-old's faith in his safety net, intact. This axis scores the net, not the signal. Holds.
2. **Edge** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** He arrived with a support network nobody else on the team brought. Black Canary volunteered to train him because his power is dangerous, and Nightwing, operating as Batman, recruited him personally and briefed him on the villains before his first fight. His family is not described.
   * **Session 17: +6.5 → +7.0 (+0.5).** ⟨Flag closed⟩ The roster now tags him *Mentor (Oracle / Barbara Gordon)* alongside *Mentor (Black Canary)*. The Oracle who found him online, investigated his origin and called in the Birds was Barbara Gordon. **Two named mentors from the Bat-family's orbit is a net nobody else arrived with.** Like everyone's, it was out of reach this session.
3. **Oracle** `+5.0`
   * **All-time.** Nightwing is a mentor he actually uses and the one most often absent; STAR Labs is an institutional origin. League hospitality and Taleria's interest raised the score in Session 14. In Session 15 he said it first: *"It's no different than Nightwing."* **He is used to being the one who looks after people**, and in Session 16 his support was his team.
   * **Session 17: holds at +5.0.** His support was the team again, and it was attentive. Edge brought food *"especially to Oracle since he's recovering,"* and Intrepid said *"Oracle definitely needs rest."* His own view of the adults above him dropped (*"most of the people telling us what to do don't know what they're doing"*), which is scored in 1.3 and §9 rather than here. Holds.
4. **Intrepid** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** Nightwing, and a Secret Identity that implies an intact civilian family, with no detail on either. The one time he asked for something, in Session 14, a teammate called him a coward until he changed his mind.
   * **Session 17: holds at +3.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Lock-Step** `−3.5`
   * **All-time.** An orphan who volunteers that he is glad he has no parents. He distrusts Iron-Side and Nightwing and keeps his confidence for Zatanna, who is not present. **Since Session 13, adults he distrusts have kept acting in his interest anyway.** Session 15 brought a ring and the first question he asked Iron-Side; Session 16 brought the clearest statement of need he has made (*"Adults being adults and actually being helpful"*) and the first time he settled a question in the field by citing an adult — Nightwing.
   * **Session 17: holds at −3.5.** Two new names on his list, neither reachable. The Grey Lantern became *"my mentor technically now"* — the first time he has called anyone in that order a mentor — and he apologised to Guy Gardner in absentia (*"If it is, I'm sorry"*). Against: *"another failed adult,"* and another universe's verdict, *"yeah, pretty useless."* Holds.

### **1.9 Emotional Regulation / Impulse Control**

1. **Oracle** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** He has held lines twice against the most socially dominant person on the team without escalating, and rations healing by arithmetic while people bleed. Session 14 was regulation under helplessness; in Session 15 he was talked over on a stream without rising to it; in Session 16 he was knocked out and his only line was a joke at his own expense.
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.5.** Outplayed and stunned by a copy of himself, his only recorded reaction was the useful one: *"Don't shoot that other Oracle. Okay, don't do it."* Holds.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** Regulated to a degree that would be impressive in an adult, and part of the reason is that the box damps his panic-fear and anger. **That is regulation and outsourcing at once, and this axis scores the outcome.** His deficit is attentional rather than emotional.
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.0.** The attentional deficit cost a penalty (1.4). Emotionally he was the calmest member of the bunk room — he woke and immediately answered the question everyone was asking — and he failed only at regulating someone else, the box. Holds.
3. **Edge** `+4.5`
   * **All-time.** Calm, dry and polite through a Session 16 briefing delivered mostly as complaint (*"Ok, that told me nothing"*), and no recorded flinch across 102 damage. The one deliberate display was a weaponised one — he *"uses his monstrousness to intimidate"* — and the risk this axis watches is the same part of him used once and not on purpose.
   * **Session 17: +5.5 → +4.5 (−1.0).** Three impulse items in one session, none of them the frightening kind.
     * **The punch.** Lock-Step asked to be punched to find out whether he was dreaming. Quasar-Force tapped him and Intrepid refused. Edge said *"Hey, you wanna have a little fun with Jeffy then? I can punch you"* and threw a full combat punch for 15, taking a teammate who had woken from −24 nearly back to zero.
     * **The door.** *"Get your ass back here!"* and then *"I give up, I completely give up."*
     * **The arena.** Hysterical laughter at an opponent's name (*"The name sucks"*), and an apology when Lock-Step said *"That's not nice."*

     **The restraint his whole profile is built on held where it mattered — the power — and slipped where it didn't.**
4. **Intrepid** `+0.5`
   * **All-time.** He voices his fear of losing control during fights, and the Session 10 ladder is impulse management sliding downhill in one monologue. Session 14 added four reversals and an escalated jab; Session 15, enthusiasm ahead of information; Session 16, a ram he thought about and did not make.
   * **Session 17: holds at +0.5.** Frustration in words — *"I give up too... Screw it"* — and restraint in deeds: *"I'm not punching you."* Holds.
5. **Lock-Step** `−6.5`
   * **All-time.** **Berserk is loss of impulse control rendered as a game mechanic.** Around it sit sedating a teammate on impulse, beating a captive conscious and proposing arson. The violent impulses have stayed down in recent fights; the social ones have not, and Session 16 opened with an unprompted grievance delivered to a stranger.
   * **Session 17: holds at −6.5.** The social impulses, without the violent ones: *"Somebody punch me,"* *"Oh fuck,"* the speech about adults on hearing a name he did not know, cutting the arena announcer off mid-sentence, and the argument at the door that ran out the penalty clock. He also said the steadiest thing anyone said about the maze: *"Do you want me to try to direct you in a maze? Come on. Let's just be honest there. That is not me. That's an Oracle thing."* Holds.

### **1.10 Moral Agency / Ethical Independence**

*Capacity for independent ethical judgement, as against deference and obedience. High = reasons from his own principles and acts on them.*

1. **Oracle** `+8.5`
   * **All-time.** **The clearest demonstrated moral agency on the team.** He refused to drug Quasar-Force when asked by the team's most socially powerful member, objected aloud to the prisoner humiliation, called Nightwing about the artifact knowing the cost, and refused Psyche's offer from inside his own head. In Session 15 he changed his mind about the progenitor on the evidence. ⟨Note⟩ He was present for the Day 56 planning in which two teammates arranged a lethal outcome by proxy, raised no objection, and in Session 16 sat through two passing references to it in silence.
   * **Session 17: +9.0 → +8.5 (−0.5).** **The question this document carried for two revisions was put to the room by somebody else, and his answer was deference.** Edge asked whether a villain team killing people was *"kind of our problem too."* Lock-Step defended the errand; Quasar-Force predicted the Destined would *"kill every man and woman in that lab."* Oracle's one contribution vouched for Lock-Step: *"You haven't been with us long enough... There was a time I doubted Lockstep, and he's preaching the gospel."* That line answered Edge's doubt about the League, not his question about the killing, and to the killing Oracle said nothing, twice. Against it, independent judgement of another kind: *"Hey, let's not fight at all. See what they do,"* and *"Why don't we all just bust out of here together."* Still first, by half a point.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+8.0`
   * **All-time.** He reasons from an articulated frame rather than from instruction. He defended the box's personhood against the only qualified person in the room, asserted himself four times in Session 15, and moved, on his own reasoning, toward lethal force by proxy. In Session 16 he corrected the dominant teammate on the record and defended the absent adults.
   * **Session 17: holds at +8.0.** Independent in both directions. He defended the errand on his own reasoning (*"we needed to save the world and we were short on hands"*) and named what it costs (*"I think we're compromising on the killing"*). In the arena he set a standard nobody gave him — *"I think we should be honorable, at least to them. I don't think our little hosts here care"* — and asked the question that separates rules from rulers: *"Are you talking about Nightwing's rules or the rules of the sport?"* Agency is not direction. Holds.
3. **Edge** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** In Session 16 the evidence of independent judgement was tactical: the case for Argent first, made on his own reasoning, and the cynicism he was offered on arrival, declined. The backstory's one moral fact is a choice — when his power appeared, he went looking for someone to teach him restraint.
   * **Session 17: +4.0 → +7.0 (+3.0).** **The first Titan to object to the Destined errand on the record — on the day he joined, against three teammates.** *"I have this question hanging around in my head since before we entered that warehouse... if they're really killing people that's kind of our problem too right?"* He pressed it three times:
     * *"Wait, just a second, man."*
     * *"it was pretty wild what you say: that sending a team of murder hoboes."*
     * *"if you kill the progenitor, what happened with that people?"*

     He also refused the premise that the League would put them in that position. Then he gave the room a principle: *"We follow the rule when it's easy and when it's hard. Otherwise, it's not a rule, it's a mood."* **The largest single move on any §1 axis this revision.**
4. **Lock-Step** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** Maximum independence: he acts on his own judgement and defers to nobody. His Session 13 position on adults sending children into world-ending situations was vindicated in Session 14, and he wrote the team's only mission-vetting proposal. In Session 15 he abandoned his own stated principle under a clock (*"we don't have the time to be democratic"*), which caps the score; in Session 16 he set the warehouse rule by citing Nightwing.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.0.** Independent to the end of the argument. The utilitarian case is his own — *"we can either save and protect our righteousness or actually save people's lives... We don't have that moral high ground or the strength."* So is the concession for the arena: *"I'm hearing it... this is light work, we're following the rules."* Holds.
5. **Intrepid** `+2.0`
   * **All-time.** *"Will go with any team decision"* — and in Session 14 he went with it and then argued there had been nothing else to go with. Session 15: *"I'm not really a thinker."* Session 16 brought the first judgement he exercised against a teammate's offer and kept: he refused to be teleported clear.
   * **Session 17: holds at +2.0.** Two items cancel. *"That's just exploitation"* is the first unprompted moral judgement on his file about anything other than himself. *"If the powers that be tell us we lost, then we lost"* is deference to the people holding him prisoner. On the errand: *"I get what you're saying."* Holds.

### **1.11 Morality / Moral Alignment**

*Not judgement or discipline — simply how good this person is.*

1. **Oracle** `+9.0`
   * **All-time.** He heals people who were trying to kill him minutes earlier, demands surrender before every engagement, attempted to revive a man he had no reason to save, and refuses to take part in cruelty, out loud. In Session 15 his instinct about a predator was elimination and his conclusion, reached by listening, was relocation; in Session 16 every point of damage he dealt was Focus damage.
   * **Session 17: holds at +9.0.** He spent the battery on the two teammates who could not get up on their own, and his stated aim for the arena was the most humane line spoken there: *"I just want to get through this and all of us turn out without anybody getting hurt."* His silence on the errand is scored in 1.10 and 1.12 — agency and line-holding — not here. Holds.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** He treats captured enemies as people, pulled bodies out of a gassed room, gave a statement against his own interest, and took a knife through the abdomen for a nation he has never visited. **The fatality on his record was an accident he has never tried to minimise**, and his stated rule is to kill only against lethal intent that cannot otherwise be stopped. Against it, two sentences: Session 15's *"we would be more comfortable unleashing Fade-Out with his full lethal fury on the controller,"* and Session 16's *"We sent the murder hobos after the bad progenitor."*
   * **Session 17: +8.0 → +7.0 (−1.0).** **The line moved in words again, and further.** *"One thing I'm very confident on is that the destined will kill every man and woman in that lab, whether the progenitor is there or not"* — and in the same conversation, *"we needed to save the world and we were short on hands. And the other progenitor is probably the perfect person to send these murder hobos after."* That is foreknowledge of the deaths of people who are not the target, accepted as the cost. What keeps the fall to a point: he named it as a compromise (*"I think we're compromising on the killing"*), and in the arena he asked for honour towards the opponents and offered his own one friendship (*"Well, we can be friends!"*). Still second.
3. **Edge** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** The first recorded use of his power was protective: an attacker's weapon sliced apart during an assault. His first fight was spent taking a mind-controller off the board, shooting a device out of a villain's hand, and holding off Blitzkrieg alone so the others could be got out. ⟨Volatile⟩ The roster's legend is that what he was *"cut down armies."*
   * **Session 17: +5.5 → +6.5 (+1.0).** Concern for people the team was not responsible for: the lab staff the Destined might kill, and the infected the team might strand (*"if you kill the progenitor, what happened with that people?"*). He fed a room of people who were *"pretty down,"* and in the arena he knocked out his opponent and sat down rather than keep hitting. Against it, the punch. **The person in the room still uses a lethal power for protective ends, and is now the team's loudest voice against killing.**
4. **Intrepid** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** Straightforwardly decent. He takes hits meant for softer people, is afraid of hurting anyone, and said his reason for going to the Watchtower was *"I'm just going to protect you guys."* He sits below the top of the table only because his goodness is passive.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.0.** *"That's just exploitation,"* *"I'm not punching you,"* and before the bout the one note of caution: *"They might be bad guys in that world."* Holds.
5. **Lock-Step** `−1.5`
   * **All-time.** Genuinely split rather than simply bad. **For:** handcuffs offered first, bloodless captures, a bank's worth of hostages teleported clear, the Session 14 heal, a species saved on Kwa, and in Session 16 five infected teenagers he insisted be knocked out rather than hurt. **Against:** a fourteen-year-old teammate sedated twice, a restrained prisoner humiliated, consent treated as a formality, five released teenagers shamed on camera, and a detainee's death commissioned so the Titans could *"keep our hands clean."* **People when they are human; not when they are not.**
   * **Session 17: −1.0 → −1.5 (−0.5).** **The errand became doctrine, told to a recruit.** *"Deaths we're not worried about right now. We're trying to save the world first. And then we can worry about the deaths later,"* and *"we can either save and protect our righteousness or actually save people's lives."* For the fight in front of him he set the opposite rule — *"if we don't have to, we should always try to not, you know, be lethal,"* and in the arena *"nobody kills each other"* — and added, of Nightwing's rule, *"I think here's exceptions."* For: he stayed with a frozen fourteen-year-old rather than leave him alone (*"I just don't feel comfortable leaving our youngest"*). The shape is unchanged — people when they are in front of him, a policy when they are not — and the policy is now stated.

### **1.12 Line-Holding Under Duress**

*Not what they believe — what survives contact with pressure.*

1. **Oracle** `+8.5`
   * **All-time.** Four documented holds against four kinds of pressure — peer (the sedation request), group (the humiliation), loyalty (the call to Nightwing) and compassion (Psyche, inside his head). He has never folded. Since Session 15 one open question: no objection when two teammates arranged a lethal outcome by proxy.
   * **Session 17: +9.0 → +8.5 (−0.5).** **The open question became a scored one.** For two revisions the item was silence in a room where nobody pressed the line. In Session 17 somebody did — the newcomer, twice — and Oracle did not take the line up; he backed the teammate who had crossed it. That is not folding under pressure. It is not holding a line somebody handed him. Still first.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** He recited the conduct standard from memory, held his position against a Lex-Corp attorney, and held against expertise on the box's personhood. **What he does not do is convert holding a line into imposing one.** Session 15 had two holds against a teammate and one shift: his line on force moved under a 24-hour clock to lethal action by proxy, and it has stayed there.
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.0.** He held — the moved line. Pressed by Edge, he did not move back toward his own rule (*"only kill if they are trying to kill you"*); he defended the errand. In the arena he held a line of his own against the de facto leader's suggestion of disguised area attacks: *"I think we should be honorable, at least to them."* Holds.
3. **Edge** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** In Session 16 his physical line held — knocked to zero, up again, holding Blitzkrieg off alone — and no moral line was pressured. Restraint, the value his profile is built on, was not tested against a reason to drop it.
   * **Session 17: +3.0 → +6.0 (+3.0).** **Tested on the day he joined, by three teammates, and held.** The de facto leader, the team's intelligence and the team's medic gave him between them the whole case for the errand: the stakes, the League, the shortage of hands. He kept saying the same thing until it became a rule for the arena that Lock-Step accepted: *"I'm hearing what you're saying."* One reversal sits in the file too, and it was the right kind: from refusing the hosts' instructions to following them once there was nothing to gain by refusing.
4. **Intrepid** `+4.0`
   * **All-time.** None of his lines was tested until Session 14, and then one broke four times in a single conversation under one teammate's single word. In Session 16 he held the first line on his file against the same teammate: offered an exit under fire, he refused it.
   * **Session 17: +3.5 → +4.0 (+0.5).** The second held line against the same teammate, and this time it was about where the team went, not about himself. When the door opened and Lock-Step stayed with the frozen Quasar-Force, Intrepid argued — *"I'm going where I'm supposed to go,"* *"It's a penalty!"* — and went on, and the newcomer went with him. Earlier he had refused Lock-Step's request to be punched.
5. **Lock-Step** `−4.0`
   * **All-time.** **He has been the pressure the others hold against** — the sedation, the humiliation, the concealment and the non-consensual extractions were all his initiative. Session 15 gave him the first hold not in his own favour, on Kwa, and then the collapse in its most literal form: *"No, I'm not coming around on lethal force"* in the room, and *"cinder blocks in the ocean"* in private. In Session 16 he held the non-lethal line for the warehouse.
   * **Session 17: holds at −4.0.** He held his position against the newcomer — the errand stands — and accepted the newcomer's rule for the arena. Neither is a line surviving pressure in this axis's sense; the Session 15 collapse is now policy. Holds.

### **1.13 Judgment Under Stress**

1. **Oracle** `+8.5`
   * **All-time.** He retargets mid-action on precognitive input, holds rather than burns actions, and made the coldest correct call of the campaign in Session 9. In Session 14 he made the failure call nobody else wanted to make; in Session 15 he turned a failed Logic check into a structured forecast. In Session 16 he supplied the plan after saying there wasn't one, and moved Let's Go to the one teammate who could teleport, so the extraction happened.
   * **Session 17: holds at +8.5.** Good calls and bad ones, and the good ones were bigger.
     * **Good:** heal the two members without healing factors first; ask whether Lock-Step can teleport them out; consult Intuition before the bout and find the copier; and hang back at the door, *"knowing the team wasted most of the penalty time arguing,"* so that Quasar-Force had someone to catch up with.
     * **Bad:** *"I think the odds are 100%"* that the other team was the Destined (it was not), and the melee swing at his copy (1.5).

     Holds.
2. **Edge** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** The best tactical read in the Session 16 pre-fight conversation came from the person who had been on the team for ten minutes: take the mind-controller first, because she *"can turn 5 vs 5 into 6 vs 4."* Under fire he picked targets by threat and chose the device over a body when the device was the objective.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.5.** His reads were again the most accurate in the room. The captors were *"not entirely hostile because we wake up on beds and we don't seem to be under the same control"* as the five teenagers. Lock-Step's worst cases were *"a lot of assumptions,"* and they were wrong. His method was *"one problem at a time."* Against it, the punch, and a bout he took himself out of after one turn. Holds.
3. **Quasar-Force** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** His read of what matters is often the best in the room; his read of what it will cost has failed twice on the record, worst in Session 14. Session 16's deductions were good, and the villains had a plan for him that he did not have for them.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.0.** Two good deductions: *"If we did get nabbed by these arena press gangers, we may not actually have parasites on us,"* and the camera-and-implant sweep that found the nullifiers. One costly impulse: the penalty. One excellent bout decision: remove the enemy healer from the arena. Holds.
4. **Intrepid** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** He takes positioning guidance well and has visibly improved, and he still committed a full action to a grab on a target whose force field had already beaten a whip, and still stomps rooms with ordinary humans in them. In Session 15 he called for an attack before any facts were in.
   * **Session 17: +3.0 → +3.5 (+0.5).** The best deduction on his record, made mid-argument: *"Quasar-Force is taking his penalty, which means time is a factor in this competition."* He reasoned about the maze before he saw it (*"what if we're in a maze, and if we don't get to certain points..."*) and corrected himself on area attacks. Against it: *"if the powers that be tell us we lost, then we lost."*
5. **Lock-Step** `0.0`
   * **All-time.** The old record — the blind teleport into ten monsters, the arson proposal, the captive beaten conscious, Berserk — has been climbing toward zero. The Session 14 healing hex was a cold, correct call; Session 15 held the best judgement sequence of his campaign, on Kwa, and then the Destined. Session 16 was sound in action, with a pre-fight briefing that was wrong on both facts.
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.0.** A split ledger again.
     * **For:** *"Just put it in"* (it stopped the penalties); the phased handshake with a stranger who turned out to copy powers; *"they're horrible"*; and the television gambit, which got both teams a look at each other.
     * **Against:** two worst-case forecasts, both wrong (the Destined, and *"the others that we just failed capturing"*); the refusal to leave the barracks until the rules were stated, the argument that *"wasted most of the penalty time"*; his real first name; and a penalty for retreating to the stands.

     Holds.

### **1.14 Justice League Vetting — Character & Conduct**

*Character side only; capability is 7.4. Scored as valence: +10 = would clear vetting and be championed by whoever sponsored him; 0 = nothing for or against; −10 = the record is disqualifying and reflects badly on the team that put him forward.*

1. **Oracle** `+8.0`
   * **All-time.** Exactly the profile a senior organisation wants. He reports honestly, defers to legitimate authority, refuses unethical instructions from peers, prioritises life over victory, and has generated no incident in seventeen sessions. **Session 14 added the entry a panel values most and sees least: he made the failure call himself.** Since Session 15, one line a panel would mark for follow-up: his silence on the Destined errand.
   * **Session 17: +8.5 → +8.0 (−0.5).** A panel would find the follow-up already answered. The newcomer asked the question a panel would ask, and the candidate vouched for the teammate who designed the errand. A panel would also read *"most of the people telling us what to do don't know what they're doing."* That is not misconduct; it is the one sentence on his file a sponsor would want to discuss before forwarding it. Still first, by two and a half points.
2. **Edge** `+5.5`
   * **All-time.** Clean conduct, a League-adjacent sponsor in Black Canary, and a recruiter who wore the cowl to recruit him. The question a panel would ask first is the legend — that he is a reborn weapon that cut down armies.
   * **Session 17: +3.5 → +5.5 (+2.0).** **The page a panel reads first is now his objection.** On the day he joined he told the team a commissioned killing was *"kind of our problem too,"* and turned it into *"We follow the rule when it's easy and when it's hard."* That recruit is the profile a vetting officer is looking for. Two sponsors now (Black Canary and Barbara Gordon), one punch thrown at a teammate who asked for it, and the legend still unexamined. **He passes Quasar-Force.**
3. **Quasar-Force** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** His conduct would genuinely impress a panel: voluntary police cooperation, unprompted intelligence sharing, evidence discipline and an exemplary stay as the League's guest. **He is held below the top by an open fatality investigation**, and since Session 15 by a sentence no vetting officer reads past: a fourteen-year-old endorsing Fade-Out's *"full lethal fury,"* repeated as *"murder hobos"* in Session 16.
   * **Session 17: +4.0 → +3.5 (−0.5).** *"One thing I'm very confident on is that the destined will kill every man and woman in that lab"* — said while defending the decision. The leaks to Earth X357 would be in the file too. For: the toss that took the opposing healer out of a staged bout without much hurting him, and honour stated as a standard.
4. **Intrepid** `+2.0`
   * **All-time.** Clean, obedient and uncomplicated. A panel reading Session 14 would find a candidate who argued, in front of the sponsoring Justice Leaguer, that more information would not have helped. **That is not misconduct; it is a candidate talking himself down in the interview.** *"Not yet anyway"* is the counterweight.
   * **Session 17: holds at +2.0.** Nothing a panel would weigh, unless it counted that he called the arena exploitation.
5. **Lock-Step** `−9.0`
   * **All-time.** **The one file that is actively worse than no file.** Doctor Fate has publicly declared him a danger to the world. Beneath that: the sedations, the humiliated captive, the concealed artifact, two Berserk episodes and Session 14's lasso. Session 15 put a species saved and a station livestreamed beside a killing solicited through a villain team. Session 16 added his best combat-conduct page, which a panel would read after the solicitation.
   * **Session 17: −8.5 → −9.0 (−0.5).** **The solicitation now has a rationale, delivered to a recruit.** A panel would read *"deaths we're not worried about right now"* and *"we don't have that moral high ground"* as the candidate's own account of why the killing was commissioned. The spread on this axis stays at 17.0, the widest in the document.

### **1.15 Heroism**

*⟨New axis⟩ Added at Session 17. The willingness to take a risk or pay a cost for someone else — the public, a teammate, or an enemy. Scored on what was chosen and paid, not on what a power set makes cheap; the capability to protect is 4.19. Scored as valence: +10 = repeatedly risks and spends himself for teammates, bystanders and opponents alike, and never leaves anyone behind; 0 = nothing for or against; −10 = endangers or sacrifices others to spare himself. The Session 16 figures are a retroactive baseline, scored on the record through Session 16 with the retcon applied.*

1. **Oracle** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** **The only file on the team with heroism in all three directions.** Teammates: he revived Quasar-Force in Session 1, resisted Lock-Step's teleport in Session 10, *"not wanting to leave without the others,"* and in Session 16 came up from −2 and ran back into the warehouse — *"Drop me again, it was worth it."* The public: he stayed back to cover Sarah in Session 8, and in Session 12 interposed at the Celestial Core and took heavy damage to keep its force field up. Enemies: in Session 7 he finished dragging an unconscious Dargrave assistant out of a room filling with chemicals and hit the decontamination; in Session 8 he revived the man Intrepid had knocked out. Two things hold him below the ceiling: prudence (*"I'm willing to help, but I'm not being the one charging"*) and, since Session 15, silence while the Destined errand was arranged.
   * **Session 17: +8.0 → +7.5 (−0.5).** For: he emptied the battery on the two teammates without healing factors before anyone knew where they were (*"My battery's about gone now"*), hung back at the door so the frozen Quasar-Force had someone to catch up with, and set the most protective aim in the arena. Against: after the newcomer asked whether the killing was their problem, and Quasar-Force had counted the lab staff's deaths, Edge doubted that the League had really put saving the world on them, and Oracle vouched for the errand's author: *"he's preaching the gospel."* He was backing the Justice League's stakes rather than the Destined, but to the killing he said nothing, and he did not speak for the people in that lab. The heal is his usual role; the vouching is new evidence on this axis's open question, and it outweighs the heal by half a point. Still first.
2. **Intrepid** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** **His heroism is his body between harm and someone softer, offered without being asked.** He held the bank vault against Hijinx in Session 4; in Session 8 he broke out of the webbing and moved in front of the paralysed Quasar-Force; in Session 12 he interposed for Doctor Varga; on Kwa he offered *"You could always use my body as a shield"*; and in Session 16 he refused Lock-Step's exit and fought on until a 60-point marvel put him at −49. Toward enemies his record is restraint born of fear of his own strength, and for the controlled teenagers, *"they were victims."* What is scored is the choice to step in, not the Health that makes it survivable. He sits below Oracle because nearly all of it runs in one direction.
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.0.** He walked the stepping puzzle three times under the orbs (Lock-Step's last step finished it); went first into the colosseum; and took Capricorn's ram for 72 when asked (*"Yep, sure thing"*). Against: *"I get what you're saying"* to Lock-Step's doctrine — an acknowledgement, not an endorsement — and walking on at the door while the frozen Quasar-Force stayed behind with two teammates. Holds.
3. **Edge** `+5.5`
   * **All-time.** One session long through Session 16, and that session was spent on threats to his teammates.
