He argued to take the mind-controller down first, was knocked to exactly 0 and got back up to face Blitzkrieg, and — in the part of the ending the retcon kept — *"manages to hold off Blitzkrieg for a round, allowing Lock-Step to get Oracle and Quasar-Force out."* **That rear guard is the clearest act on the Session 16 record of risking himself so others could escape.** No debits, and no evidence yet toward the public or toward enemies. Provisional.
   * **Session 17: +5.0 → +5.5 (+0.5).** **The only Titan who spoke for the people the errand would kill.** *"If they're really killing people that's kind of our problem too right?"* — and for the infected, *"if you kill the progenitor, what happened with that people?"* — pressed against three veterans on the day he joined, and turned into a rule the de facto leader accepted for the arena. It cost him standing, not safety, so the move is half a point. Against: a full combat punch into a teammate just up from −24 (who had asked for it); *"Enter when you can"* to the frozen Quasar-Force before walking on; and two opening blasts (the second a Marvel for 86) that put Four-Fold to −29 in a staged bout, after which he sat down in the stands.
4. **Quasar-Force** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** A real heroic record, mostly at moderate personal risk and kind to enemies in person. In Session 3 his barrier stopped Fade-Out's murders of the Burnout Kings and he *"made himself a target"*; in Session 7 he pulled an unconscious enemy out of the gassed lab; in Session 13 he moved the controlled Star Heart away from the Breeder and cut Doctor Varga free; in Session 14 he *"moves out of his safe space"* to take the Sphere and was stabbed to −12. On Kwa he argued that a non-predatory species should not be hunted. Against: he fights from cover while others take the hits, and since Session 15 he has endorsed killing by proxy — *"full lethal fury,"* then *"murder hobos."* **The boy who shielded Fade-Out's victims endorsed sending Fade-Out.**
   * **Session 17: +4.5 → +3.5 (−1.0).** **The endorsement became acceptance of bystander deaths.** *"The destined will kill every man and woman in that lab, whether the progenitor is there or not"* — the obvious cost, stated plainly — followed by a defence of the errand: *"we needed to save the world and we were short on hands."* Seeing the cost to people who are not the target and backing the plan anyway is the opposite of what this axis measures. Naming it a compromise keeps the drop to a point; honour and an offered friendship in a staged bout buy nothing back.
5. **Lock-Step** `+3.0`
   * **All-time.** **The campaign's most courageous rescuer and the author of its worst anti-heroic act.** For: he teleported into the Session 2 ritual *"ignoring the whole argument"*; teleported the bank's hostages out in Session 4; went into a room of armed kidnappers for Sarah in Session 8; went back into Omega Grade's lab for Oracle and Exemplar in Session 10; and at the end of Session 16 got Oracle and Quasar-Force out, then went back for Edge, and for Intrepid *"using just about the last of his focus."* Against: the Destined commissioned to kill a detainee so the Titans could *"keep our hands clean,"* a restrained captive humiliated, and a fourteen-year-old teammate sedated twice. **He goes into more danger for more people than anyone on the team; his debits are acts, not words, which is why he is last.**
   * **Session 17: +3.5 → +3.0 (−0.5).** Against: the commission became doctrine, delivered to the recruit who asked about the people it would kill — *"deaths we're not worried about right now."* For: at the door he stayed with the frozen youngest in case someone *"found Quasar-Force while he can't move"* (*"I just don't feel comfortable leaving our youngest"*), and in the arena he set the no-kill rule for both teams. The stay is real but cheap; the doctrine is about real lives.

## **2\. Enemy Risk**

**Risk `−10.0 to 0.0`**

⟨Note⟩ **Session 17 is the first session in which the whole team was taken.** A party nobody on the team had heard of — the producers of *"arena ball,"* acting on the nomination of *"Minji Lin aka Firefly aka the tiny terror aka Death from Nowhere"* — lifted all five out of Blüdhaven at the moment they were weakest. It held them in a pocket dimension with cameras in every room, bathrooms included, and banks of power nullifiers in the walls and floors, and it enforced its rules by freezing people in place. Session 16 ended with the brood announcing that its orders had changed to *"collecting"* the team; Session 17 began with somebody else doing it. ⟨Volatile⟩ Whether the two are connected is unknown. The arena took the team as a unit, so it is scored once, in 2.2, as a half point of exposure on every member not already at the floor.

### **2.1 Vulnerability to Outside Subversion**

*(Position 1 = most vulnerable.)*

1. **Lock-Step** `−10.0`
   * **All-time.** The only member with a **confirmed, completed hostile operation** run against him: a temporal artifact purpose-built to lure him, profile him, transmit his data to an unknown recipient and brick itself. Around it sit the recruitment attempts, the three-way pull between Doctor Fate, Zatanna and Constantine, the Chaotic / Supernatural / Hounded tags, and a power source that may have been made from a piece of him. **Session 15 opened every front at once**: the progenitor entered his mind past the Protection Hex and read his memories with his consent, a cosmic order put a ring on him he cannot charge, and the Destined and Fade-Out came away holding a debt and his private request to have a detainee killed. Session 16 found a second device keyed to his time manipulation in the same mob warehouse.
   * **Session 17: holds at −10.0 (the floor).** A new party took him with everyone else, and he gave it his first name. He also put the Grey Lantern's claim on him into his own words for the first time: *"Remember that Grey Lantern?, my mentor technically now."* Nothing was closed.
2. **Quasar-Force** `−9.5`
   * **All-time.** The strongest structural case in the document. An alien intelligence with a damaged personality, residual Apokoliptian corruption, its own survival drive and its own agenda lives inside his central nervous system and cannot be removed without killing him. Session 14 gave the threat a face, Azimuth. Session 15 answered two of three findings in hardware — the circlet masks the signal Azimuth's search depends on, and Uncanny 3 raises every mental defence — and the Grey Lantern's verdict was *"no longer merely human."* Session 16 gave the hardware its first hostile test, and it held: a power thief's Swipe failed against his fortified mind. **What remains is the manner: he answers whoever asks politely.**
   * **Session 17: holds at −9.5.** Both halves of the case grew, and neither reaches the floor, which this axis keeps for operations already underway.
     * **The manner.** He answered every question Earth X357 asked and several nobody asked (1.6).
     * **The box.** Cut off from the Source in an isolated dimension and frightened, she spelled out *"I will find a way. Source—no! No! No—"* before *"Help."*

     **An alien intelligence that badly wants something is easier to bargain with than one that is content**, and the people who run this dimension control the way out. ⟨Volatile⟩
3. **Oracle** `−7.5`
   * **All-time.** His cybernetics are a STAR Labs product he did not build and cannot fully audit — *Bio-Symbiotic Mutation Core, Host 'WADE' — Initiated & Maintaining*: the language of a relationship, not a prosthesis. In Session 12 something wrote into his predictive module and his own diagnostics reported no intrusion; **the channel has never been fixed.** His Ego defence of 10 is the softest door on the roster, and in Session 15 a telepath read his intent without any recorded defence. The module *"did not adapt well"* to temporal transport and now resonates with Lock-Step's time-altering powers. What protects him is character: no divided loyalties, no factions courting him, and nothing hidden.
   * **Session 17: −7.0 → −7.5 (−0.5, exposure up).** **The module was the first thing on the team to react to the abduction.** At the staging point after the warehouse it *"makes him feel weird,"* and he fell unconscious before anyone else; in the pocket dimension he said *"my power is broken."* That is the third time something outside him has acted through the module — a write in Session 12, temporal transport in Session 15, a dimensional transfer now — and nobody has explained any of them.
4. **Edge** `−6.0`
   * **All-time.** Three routes in Session 16, none yet used. **Psychic:** middling mental defences. **Biographical:** the widest — a legend that he is an ancient monster reborn whose true name is lost, powers that arrived with headaches and glowing eyes, and a question about what he is that whoever claims to know the answer could exploit. **Social:** the newest member, with his strongest attachments outside the team.
   * **Session 17: −5.5 → −6.0 (−0.5, exposure up).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ The psychic route widened, and the biographical one acquired a mechanism.
     * **Psychic.** His Vigilance defence is now 13, the lowest on the team (Ego and Logic rose to 18), F-DR fell to 1, and the first mental-track hit of his file came through Vigilance: Facade's Suppressive Fire, 34 Focus and a stun.
     * **Biographical.** He told the team his control is effortful: *"I practice my concentration a lot because if I don't, things could get bad."* **Anything that breaks his concentration is now a known route to the part of him he is holding down.**
     * **Social.** It narrowed slightly: the mentor tag is two named people, not a confusion.
5. **Intrepid** `−3.0`
   * **All-time.** Least vulnerable, and it is a genuine virtue rather than an absence: a mutant origin, no technology, no artifacts, no patrons, no factions, no secrets from the team, Iron Will, and the only Secret Identity on the active roster. **Mental defence is the way in**, and it has been used twice: the progenitor's 14-Marvel in Session 15, and in Session 16 the first power ever stolen from a Titan, his Sturdy 3.
   * **Session 17: holds at −3.0.** A telepath got in again — Four-Fold opened a link in the middle of the bout — to ask whether he was all right. A Telepathic Link can be refused by anyone who does not want it, so this one he let in. Nobody is working on him. Holds.

###

### **2.2 Target Priority for Hostile Organizations**

*(Lex-Corp, the Destined, the Marrow Syndicate, STAR Labs' leadership, the Breeder and its brood, Apokolips-adjacent interests — and, from Session 17, the arena's producers. Position 1 = highest priority target.)*

1. **Quasar-Force** `−10.0`
   * **All-time.** At once the **evidence engine**, the **object of study** and **the quarry.**
     * **The evidence engine.** The Lex-Corp case, three archives, the drone fleet, the tower shield, and the only evidentiary account against the organisation that holds the Breeder.
     * **The object of study.** New Genesis technology with a public face, a known father at STAR Labs, 60 Health and the build of an eleven-year-old. Doctor Varga wants him in a laboratory; the Prodigy breeder called this team *excellent progeny.*
     * **The quarry.** A New God who defected to Apokolips is searching for the object fused to his nervous system.

     In Session 16 a hostile team arrived with a doctrine for him and said so — his fame, his known fragility, his grappling — and put him at −30 before its orders changed to collecting the team.
   * **Session 17: holds at −10.0 (the floor).** Taken with everyone else. The one hostile act aimed at him specifically came from an opponent, not an organisation: Briar Patch's grab. At the floor already.
2. **Lock-Step** `−8.5`
   * **All-time.** Someone spent a purpose-built temporal artifact on him, which is a demonstrated willingness to invest; add the magical factions and the Marrow Syndicate's willingness to talk to him specifically. Session 15 supplied the reason — his powers are time-altering and lantern-derived. In Session 16 the same mob warehouse held a second device keyed to his time manipulation, the brood came for it, and Edge destroyed it.
   * **Session 17: −8.0 → −8.5 (−0.5, exposure up).** The arena. He spent the rest period predicting which of his own enemies would be in it — the Destined, *"and the others that we just failed capturing"* — and was wrong on both. That says more about how many organisations he expects to be after him than about who is.
3. **Oracle** `−5.0`
   * **All-time.** STAR Labs cybernetics are worth reverse-engineering, and the predictive module is a standing tactical nuisance to anyone fighting the Titans. Session 14 showed the counter, and in Session 16 the brood planned around him: 72 physical damage, and a reaction that turned his Return Fire onto a teammate.
   * **Session 17: −4.5 → −5.0 (−0.5, exposure up).** The arena, and a pairing that looks chosen. The opposing team's power copier *"has copied his already"* before the bout began, and Facade picked him: *"that leaves you and me... I've got a good feeling about this."* Whether the producers arranged it is unknown; Quasar-Force's view was that *"if they had the ability to set up such a fight, they absolutely would."*
4. **Edge** `−4.5`
   * **All-time.** On nobody's list when Session 16 began, and by its end the member who cost the brood its objective. Behind that, a Monster origin any organisation that studies metahumans would want a sample of. His identity is public but *"not very well known,"* which is what keeps him below Oracle.
   * **Session 17: −4.0 → −4.5 (−0.5, exposure up).** The arena. Nothing aimed at him specifically.
5. **Intrepid** `−3.5`
   * **All-time.** A problem to be solved on the battlefield, not a target to be acquired, until Session 15 put *"maybe the next Superman"* on a viral stream. In Session 16 he was the Titan the brood came closest to taking — unconscious at −49 — and his weight was one of the two reasons Blitzkrieg left without him.
   * **Session 17: −3.0 → −3.5 (−0.5, exposure up).** The arena. The weight that kept the brood from carrying him off kept nobody from taking him this time.

*⟨Note⟩ Lobo is still deliberately excluded.* He is hunting Shein Qua, not a Titan, and Wonder Woman took the engagement personally. In Session 17 he came up once, as Quasar-Force's measure of the stakes back home: *"That'll depend on Wonder Woman versus Lobo and the Destined versus the other progenitor."* ⟨Retcon⟩ The last edition's note that the team briefed Wonder Woman after the warehouse is withdrawn.

###

### **2.3 Target Priority for Amanda Waller — Squad Value**

*Position 1 = highest priority as a Squad conscript.*

*(Waller has already appeared in-fiction — Session 6, Argus Station: she moved Shadow-Boxer to Belle Reve and pointed Quasar-Force at Project Catalyst.)*

**This axis asks one narrow question: who does she put in a harness?** A deniable field slot on Task Force X, with a bomb in the neck, sent somewhere she cannot be seen to have sent anyone. The criteria are fit, not value — coercible, expendable, quiet enough that nobody makes noise if they don't come back, and morally flexible enough to do what's asked. **Everything else she might want from these five is scored separately in 2.4.**

1. **Lock-Step** `−9.0`
   * **All-time.** The textbook profile. He is powerful, chaotic, morally flexible enough to sedate a teammate and humiliate a restrained prisoner, and **he has no parents, so nobody files the missing-persons report.** His near-immunity to mental coercion is a *specification*: she reaches for a bomb rather than a leash, which she prefers anyway. Session 15 produced the most Squad-relevant item any Titan has: a killing commissioned by proxy, through a villain team, *"so we could technically keep our hands clean."* **The one thing keeping him off the floor is that he is no longer quiet.**
   * **Session 17: holds at −9.0.** The Task Force X sentence got a paragraph. *"We can either save and protect our righteousness or actually save people's lives"* is a recruitment pitch Waller would not need to write. The noise still keeps him off the floor.
2. **Edge** `−6.0`
   * **All-time.** In Session 16, **the closest skill fit on the roster**: a dedicated marksman, the Squad's oldest archetype, with a Monster origin, a legend about a creature that *"cut down armies,"* and an identity that is public but *"not very well known."* ⟨OOC⟩ In at least one recent continuity Waller runs a squad made entirely of monsters. What kept him behind Lock-Step was the handle and the noise: no record, no debts, and a mentor one phone call from the harness. **He fit the slot; he did not fit the leash.**
   * **Session 17: holds at −6.0.** ⟨Re-spec⟩ The fit and the leash moved in opposite directions. **The fit improved:** the marksman became area artillery, and an *"army-slaying cataclysm"* with a storm of blades is a Squad asset by description. **The leash got harder:** he is now on record as the member who will not accept a killing for convenience, and Waller would find that out in the first conversation. Holds.
3. **Quasar-Force** `−5.0`
   * **All-time.** **A Squad slot is the worst possible use of him.** The homicide on file is real leverage and his power is obvious. Against it, he is the most photographed hero in Blüdhaven, publicly identified, reads as eleven, and has parents who would go to every camera in the state. Since Session 15 a Waller file would quote *"full lethal fury"* in full.
   * **Session 17: holds at −5.0.** A Waller file would put *"the destined will kill every man and woman in that lab"* beside *"full lethal fury."* A Squad slot is still the worst use of him.
4. **Intrepid** `−3.0`
   * **All-time.** A Secret Identity is genuine deniability, and he is by a wide margin the most physically survivable person on the team. He takes orders without argument. What keeps him low is that Waller can buy muscle anywhere, and he offers her no handle; two different powers have now got into him through his mental side, so she could use a leash rather than a bomb.
   * **Session 17: holds at −3.0.** Nothing new.
5. **Oracle** `−2.0`
   * **All-time.** **The worst Squad fit on the roster.** A spotless record, no leverage of any kind, an Extreme Appearance that makes him unmistakable on any operation, a STAR Labs provenance that guarantees institutional questions, and an instinct to report irregularities to his mentor at once — demonstrated in Session 14, when his first action after a total failure was to ring the sponsoring authority.
   * **Session 17: holds at −2.0.** ⟨Retcon⟩ The last edition's *"The team briefed Wonder Woman the same afternoon"* is withdrawn. Nothing else changed: he would still ring somebody the evening she spoke to him.

### **2.4 Target Priority for Amanda Waller — Strategic Interest**

*(Everything that is not a field slot: asset cultivation, informant recruitment, containment files, laboratory interest, and leverage held in reserve. Position 1 = highest priority.)*

1. **Quasar-Force** `−9.5`
   * **All-time.** She has already tasked him once: the Project Catalyst nudge used a fourteen-year-old as an unpaid intelligence asset. He is New Genesis technology bonded to a compliant minor and the best technical mind in Blüdhaven; Session 14 added a New God hunting him, and Session 15 three builds ARGUS would want the specifications for before it wanted the builder. Too visible to strap a bomb to; perfect to *cultivate*. **It is the most valuable file in this document.**
   * **Session 17: holds at −9.5.** The file gained one page ARGUS would pay for — a mother box cannot reach the Source from an isolated dimension, and says so — and nobody from ARGUS saw it.
2. **Oracle** `−7.5`
   * **All-time.** **Second because of Precognition.** A sixteen-year-old who sees twenty-four hours ahead is worth more to a planning cell than any brawler she could conscript, and the module has shown it can *receive* as well as forecast. The STAR Labs cybernetics are a second interest; since Session 15 they resonate with time-wave phenomena. He is compliant with legitimate authority, so she can approach him through channels.
   * **Session 17: holds at −7.5.** The module reacted to a dimensional transfer before its host did. A planning cell would want that dissected too; it is the same file.
3. **Lock-Step** `−7.0`
   * **All-time.** Until Session 15, a poor investment for anything but a harness. **Session 15 made him three things she collects:** a cosmic-order asset (a Grey Lantern successor wearing the ring, in a campaign where time powers are banned), an informant (the Destined owe him and he owes them), and a public voice. Session 16 added third-party confirmation: a crime syndicate trafficking a device keyed to his time manipulation.
   * **Session 17: holds at −7.0.** Nothing new reached her.
4. **Edge** `−6.5`
   * **All-time.** **A containment file before he is anything else.** A Monster-origin fifteen-year-old whose legend says he is an ancient creature reborn, who can slice through objects by concentrating, and whose powers arrived with chronic headaches and glowing eyes is exactly the profile ARGUS opens a laboratory file on. He also brings a route through Black Canary to the Birds of Prey, a network she would rather watch than fight.
   * **Session 17: −6.0 → −6.5 (−0.5, exposure up).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ The containment file thickened. The power that sliced an attacker's weapon now has an area form — Supernova, every enemy within ten spaces — and the roster's profile no longer hedges the legend: *"An ancient army-slaying cataclysm reborn into a soft-spoken teenager."* Still below Lock-Step, because nobody has confirmed what the legend is.
5. **Intrepid** `−2.5`
   * **All-time.** A mutant physiology file and nothing more, held above zero only because ARGUS keeps files on metas as a matter of routine — and a viral stream calling a sixteen-year-old *"the next Superman"* is the sort of claim ARGUS opens a file on.
   * **Session 17: holds at −2.5.** Nothing new.

### **2.5 Susceptibility to Enemy Legal & Bureaucratic Action**

*(Position 1 = most exposed. Lex-Corp's counsel is already engaged: a cease-and-desist is on record, a wrongful-death threat is circulating via the deceased's uncle, and Nightwing operates under the Batman mantle with correspondingly higher scrutiny.)*

1. **Quasar-Force** `−10.0`
   * **All-time.** Not close. **He caused a death**, gave a statement to police, is publicly identified, is the named custodian of contested evidence, and argued statutory law with a Lex-Corp attorney while unqualified to do so. He is a minor, which makes his parents the real defendants and his statements easy to characterise as unreliable, and a "science advocate" is seeding a diminished-control narrative. In Session 15 he endorsed sending a villain team to break into a research facility. He is not *a* legal exposure for this team; he is *the* case.
   * **Session 17: −9.5 → −10.0 (−0.5, exposure up; the floor).** **Foreknowledge completes the case.** He endorsed sending the Destined in Session 15. In Session 17 he said, in front of four witnesses and an unknown number of cameras, that he is *"very confident"* they *"will kill every man and woman in that lab, whether the progenitor is there or not,"* and defended the decision. If the Destined act, an accessory theory no longer needs to prove that he foresaw it. He also told another universe that his father works at STAR Labs.
2. **Lock-Step** `−9.0`
   * **All-time.** A defence attorney's gift: a fourteen-year-old teammate sedated twice with a witness who refused to take part, a restrained captive humiliated over an objection, habitual breaking and entering, evidence concealed from his own mentor, and a stipend that makes him a payee. Session 15 added the solicitation, the five teenagers' faces broadcast with *\#SomeKidsJustNeedAWhooping*, a STAR Labs employee broadcast, and a livestreamed League facility. In Session 16 he met one of those teenagers, offered her the cuffs, knocked her out and left her cuffed in a crime syndicate's warehouse. **He was the member who once said out loud that this team needs lawyers. He is now the member who most needs one.**
   * **Session 17: −8.5 → −9.0 (−0.5, exposure up).** The solicitation acquired a stated motive and a statement of indifference to its side effects: *"deaths we're not worried about right now."* That is the sentence a prosecutor reads to a jury.
3. **Intrepid** `−2.5`
   * **All-time.** Ground-Shaking Stomp discharged into rooms containing ordinary humans is a mass tort waiting for one bad roll, and he has come within a hair of it three times. No casualty, no public identity and no assets, so his exposure is entirely prospective.
   * **Session 17: holds at −2.5.** No stomp, and a reminder to his teammates that area attacks hit everyone in them. Holds.
4. **Edge** `−2.0`
   * **All-time.** Little on file: fifteen, a public identity that is *"not very well known,"* and no record. The one incident in his backstory ended with an attacker's weapon sliced to pieces. **What would make him a case is the lethal ceiling of his power**, and nothing has come near it.
   * **Session 17: holds at −2.0.** ⟨Re-spec⟩ The prospective exposure changed shape: the ceiling that would make him a case is now an area power. On the record he is the one member who objected to the errand, which is exculpatory for him and for nobody else. Holds.
5. **Oracle** `−0.5`
   * **All-time.** The cleanest record on the team, and actively exculpatory for everyone else: he demands surrender, heals opponents, revived a man Intrepid nearly killed and attempted to revive the man Quasar-Force killed, and in Session 14 made a contemporaneous report of a loss, voluntarily, on the night. **If this team is ever deposed, Oracle is the defence's best witness.** ⟨Note⟩ He was present for the Day 56 planning, and has sat through references to it since.
   * **Session 17: holds at −0.5.** The deposition question now has an answer, and it does not help his side. Asked by a teammate whether the killing was their problem, he vouched for the teammate who arranged it. That moves 1.10 and 1.12. His own exposure is still the smallest on the team. Holds.

### **2.6 Personal Leverage / Exploitability**

*What an adversary can hold over this person specifically. Position 1 = most exploitable.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `−9.5`
   * **All-time.** Every category at once: parents at a known address, a public identity, an accidental death on record, an artifact fused to his nervous system that cannot be removed without killing him, and a habit of answering honestly whoever asks politely. **He is fourteen, so leverage on him is leverage on his family and the reverse.** Session 14 found the most precise lever — a pencil sketch of Azimuth — and Session 15 a lever of a different kind: the entity inside him has a name, and he loves her (*"second mother," "Stella," "It's two people's home"*). **Leverage on one of them is visibly leverage on both.**
   * **Session 17: holds at −9.5.** **The lever acquired a voice.** *"I am still with you. Don't be afraid... I will find a way. Source—no! No! No—... Help."* He could not comfort her, and she withdrew. An adversary who can offer the box a way back to the Source, or threaten to keep her from it, now holds something a fourteen-year-old would trade a great deal for. Nobody is holding it yet; holds short of the floor.
2. **Lock-Step** `−8.5`
   * **All-time.** A misconduct file that would end him with every authority figure in his life, and magical entanglements pulling three ways. Session 15 removed the insulation of having nothing to seize: **a villain holds his private request to have someone killed, in his own words, and he knows it.** Around it: a favour owed to the Destined, an audience and a reputation, a named best friend, a civilian name in an outside party's hands, and a ring he cannot charge.
   * **Session 17: holds at −8.5.** He handed a new party his first name and gave the team a new lever on himself (*"my mentor technically now"*). Both are already priced. Holds.
3. **Edge** `−4.5`
   * **All-time.** Three levers on the sheet and in the backstory. **The identity question:** anyone who claims to know what the monster is, or how to stop the headaches, is offering something he has wanted since the powers arrived. **The people:** Black Canary and the Birds behind her. **The obligations:** school, with grades that were *"already bad,"* and a public identity.
   * **Session 17: −4.0 → −4.5 (−0.5, exposure up).** A fourth lever, in his own words: *"I practice my concentration a lot because if I don't, things could get bad."* Whoever can make him lose it, or credibly threaten to, holds something. The people lever got a second name, Barbara Gordon.
4. **Oracle** `−3.0`
   * **All-time.** A clean record, no divided loyalties and nothing concealed. The one lever that seemed to fit him — a voice in his own head presenting as a frightened sixteen-year-old asking to be saved — was tested under the worst available conditions and did not move him. What remains is structural: cybernetics he cannot audit, the weakest mental defence on the team, and a proven route into his cognition.
   * **Session 17: holds at −3.0.** ⟨Flag closed⟩ Edge's mentor tag is Barbara Gordon; the newcomer is not someone Oracle is responsible for. The one new thing an adversary might use is disillusion — *"most of the people telling us what to do don't know what they're doing"* — which is a recruiter's opening rather than something to hold over him. Holds.
5. **Intrepid** `−2.0`
   * **All-time.** A Secret Identity, no assets, no factions and no secrets from his own side. His levers are personal and he supplied them himself: the fear of hurting people, the fear of being replaced, and an ambition (*"Not yet anyway"*) that is an offer waiting for somebody to make it.
   * **Session 17: holds at −2.0.** Nothing new.

### **2.7 Value to Enemy as Hostage / Leverage**

*How much would be surrendered to get this person back. Position 1 = most valuable to hold.*

*⟨Note⟩ Session 17 is the first session in which the whole team was held — and no ransom was asked. "Substitution may not be allowed until the episode is over": the hosts wanted a show, not a price. This axis prices what parties would give to get each Titan back, and that did not change.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `−9.5`
   * **All-time.** The team would give up almost anything, and so would several institutions. He is fourteen, presents as eleven, is universally liked, and carries New Genesis technology that Batman, STAR Labs and ARGUS each have separate interests in. Holding him means negotiating with six parties: the team, his parents, the Justice League, Guy Gardner, Doctor Taleria, and Azimuth. **Nobody on this roster is worth more alive.**
   * **Session 17: holds at −9.5.** None of the six could be reached, including the one who picks up mid-fight.
2. **Oracle** `−7.0`
   * **All-time.** Taking Oracle removes the team's entire recovery capability as well as a person, and he is the most trusted member, so any ransom put to this team would be paid. Session 14 ran the scale model without anyone having to take him, and Session 16 ran it at full size: while he was down at −2, nobody healed anybody, and every recovery the team made went through him.
   * **Session 17: holds at −7.0.** The scale model on a smaller scale: the two members without healing factors stayed down until he woke.
3. **Lock-Step** `−6.0`
   * **All-time.** No family to ransom and a damaged relationship with his own side. What raised him was external: Doctor Fate, Zatanna and Constantine all hold positions on him, and Session 15 added a time-travelling order that designated him a successor and a people who owe their existence to him. In Session 16 he was the team's way out.
   * **Session 17: holds at −6.0.** The way out failed — *"out of range"* — for the first time, and the price his outside parties would pay is unchanged.
4. **Edge** `−5.0`
   * **All-time.** The team had known him for an afternoon, so **the value comes from outside**: Black Canary and the Birds, and Nightwing, who recruited him personally.
   * **Session 17: holds at −5.0.** The outside got a second name, Barbara Gordon, and it was already priced.
5. **Intrepid** `−4.0`
   * **All-time.** Universally liked and institutionally invisible. The team would come for him; nobody else would. In Session 16 the team did, on the last of Lock-Step's Focus.
   * **Session 17: holds at −4.0.** Nothing new.

###

### **2.8 Consequence Severity in the Event of Compromise**

*Conditional severity, not likelihood — how bad is it if this person defects, is mind-controlled, or is turned by leverage. Position 1 = worst outcome.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `−10.0`
   * **All-time.** **The worst single scenario in the campaign.** Psyche ran the scale model across Sessions 12 and 13, bringing down the main containment field on the Celestial Core with his telekinesis. A compromised Quasar-Force delivers the complete Lex-Corp evidence chain, three archives, the drone fleet and its codes, three laboratories, the tower's teleport shield and the team's operational picture. It also delivers a body that lifts cruise-ship tonnage without a check, wrapped around an Apokoliptian-modified intelligence with its own agenda. And he could simply take off the device that hides him from Azimuth.
   * **Session 17: holds at −10.0 (the floor).** Nothing changed the payload. The box in distress is a matter of likelihood (2.1, 2.6).
2. **Lock-Step** `−9.0`
   * **All-time.** Teleport Together means a compromised Lock-Step can put anything anywhere, including inside the tower. Berserk has shown what he does without restraint, and Doctor Fate's objection is that his chaotic nature is a danger on a scale larger than Blüdhaven. Session 15 added a ring of unknown capability, a time-coded power source and the team's entire public voice; Session 16 demonstrated the first sentence, moving the team out of a hostile building one teleport at a time.
   * **Session 17: holds at −9.0.** The teleport that could not get the team home still moved it through a maze in three jumps. Holds.
3. **Edge** `−8.0`
   * **All-time.** Third on arrival in Session 16, and the reason was the legend. A turned Edge was the team's best rifle, with a healing factor, a dodge and the only Heightened Senses on the roster, living in the tower. Behind the kit: a power that slices through objects by concentration, the title *The Reborn Calamity*, and a monster that *"cut down armies."* **If the legend is literal, the severity is not the kit but what the kit is attached to.**
   * **Session 17: −7.5 → −8.0 (−0.5, exposure up).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ The payload grew. A turned Edge is now area artillery: every enemy within ten spaces at ×7+7 and up, and two Force blasts a round through Lightning Actions, from someone who sleeps in the tower. His own account of the failure condition — concentration lost, *"things could get bad"* — is the scenario this axis prices.
4. **Oracle** `−7.0`
   * **All-time.** Underrated and severe. A turned Oracle hands the enemy **twenty-four hours of precognitive lookahead and a perfect eidetic record of everything this team has ever done or discussed**, then heals the people fighting it. The channel into his module is proven and unrepaired. What kept him below the members above was auditability, and a payload measured in information and recovery.
   * **Session 17: −6.5 → −7.0 (−0.5, exposure up).** **Session 17 ran the scale model with a copy.** Facade, wearing Oracle's powers, stunned Edge with a reaction Suppressive Fire for 34 Focus and parried Oracle into 34 damage and a stun. Two Titans were neutralised in two actions by somebody using nothing but his kit. His payload is not only information and recovery; turned, it is a combat kit that finds the team's softest defences.
5. **Intrepid** `−6.0`
   * **All-time.** A hostile Intrepid is a containment problem this team cannot solve with the tools it fields: 210 Health, H-DR 3, Healing Factor 7, Immovable and an indiscriminate area attack. Session 16 ran a partial version: the team spent an afternoon unable to put down the one enemy built like him.
   * **Session 17: holds at −6.0.** Capricorn, a brick of his own class, put him prone with a 72-point Marvel ram. The containment problem is the one Session 16 described.

*Read this axis against 2.1.* Lock-Step and Quasar-Force are first and second on likelihood and second and first on severity, so the two most dangerous people to lose are still the two most likely to be taken. **Session 17 moved Edge and Oracle half a point on both axes, and the orderings held.** Edge's rebuild lowered his Vigilance door and put an area weapon behind the question his file has carried since he arrived. Oracle's module reacted first to the abduction, and his copy showed what his own kit does when somebody else is wearing it.

## **3\. Team Internals**

*Session 17 is the first session in which the newcomer disagreed with the room. He did it twice, on the two most serious questions the team had — the Destined errand and whether to rehearse a formation — and a third disagreement, at the door, split the team in two. Section 3 moved on 21 placements.*

### **3.1 Internal Reputation with Teammates**

**Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`**

1. **Oracle** `+9.0`
   * **All-time.** Universally trusted, and it is earned rather than granted. He feeds the team, heals it and keeps his word, and what actually generates respect is that he declines to do the wrong thing even when a teammate with more social pull asks him to; Lock-Step still comes to him for help *after* being refused. In Session 15 Lock-Step took him into first contact as *"my boy"* and joked on a stream that he is *"the guy that always tells us what we do wrong and tries to fix it."* **That is the joke you make about the person whose judgement you depend on.** In Session 16 the joke became a title: *"Oracle is our plan guy."*
   * **Session 17: holds at +9.0.** The title is now a maneuver. Asked by the newcomer whether the team has formations, Lock-Step listed *"the not dying maneuver"* and *"the Ask Oracle,"* and of the maze, *"That's an Oracle thing."* Edge's verdict on his warehouse fight was *"It was amazing,"* and Edge's first burger went to him. Already at the top of the band.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+8.5`
   * **All-time.** Genuinely loved. Oracle calls him the only sane person in the group and cooks around his appetite, and the team carried him home unconscious in Session 14 and spent Focus it did not have to wake him. **The discount is structural:** the team talks to him as a pre-teen. Session 15 raised the intellectual respect (*"That's our smarts"*) and opened a doubt (*"Are you human or are you mother box?"*); Session 16 spoke the discount aloud to the newcomer (*"that kid that looks like he is like 12"*), and he corrected it himself.
   * **Session 17: +8.0 → +8.5 (+0.5).** **The respect overtook the discount, from the person who applies it most.** *"All right, Quasar, you're the brains. That's why I've really, really stayed with you because you know, people are telling us where to go, but you actually know where we should go."* The discount was in the same breath (*"our youngest"*) and in another universe's mouth (*"you look like you're like 12"*), and the regard was the larger of the two.
3. **Intrepid** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** No enemies, no complaints, no friction. He plays video games with Lock-Step, takes hits for everyone and goes along with team decisions cheerfully, and nobody defers to him about anything. Session 14 was the first time a teammate went after him — the word *coward* — and Session 15 the public reversal (*"I will never doubt you again"*). In Session 16 the same teammate asked his permission before moving him.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.5.** *"Hey, actually, it looks good on Intrepid,"* from the newcomer. From Lock-Step, an argument at the door that Intrepid settled by walking. Holds.
4. **Edge** `+5.5`
   * **All-time.** Welcomed in Session 16 before he had done anything — *"Oh, thank god, finally, we get somebody competent"* — and then he earned it in order: the target call the team adopted, two decisive assists, the shot that destroyed the device, and a round alone against Blitzkrieg. His reply to Oracle's briefing, *"Ok, that told me nothing,"* is the kind of line a room keeps.
   * **Session 17: holds at +5.5.** Both accounts moved, in opposite directions.
     * **Professional:** *"It was amazing"* was his verdict on Oracle's fight, and his own bout lasted one turn.
     * **Character:** a full combat punch on a teammate, a day of pushing a formation on people who did not want one, and Lock-Step's *"I'm still unsure about you."*

     Intrepid sided with him at the door, and Quasar-Force backed his training plan (*"Better just some of us than none of us"*). Holds.
5. **Lock-Step** `+4.5`
   * **All-time.** Two accounts that don't reconcile. **Professional regard is high**: four consecutive clean engagements by Session 14, the healing hex, Kwa, and in Session 16 the knockout on Argent and the whole extraction. **Character regard is damaged**: the sedations, the humiliated prisoner, the concealed artifact, the *coward* fight, the lasso, and in Session 15 every teammate used as stream content. In Session 16 he made two wrong claims to the newcomer, and both were corrected in front of him.
   * **Session 17: +5.0 → +4.5 (−0.5).** **Two teammates ran out of patience with him in the same five minutes.** Edge: *"Get your ass back here!"*, *"I give up, I completely give up,"* and *"I can't believe that guy saved my life."* Intrepid: *"You're gonna leave us to fend for ourselves last time."* Against those, Oracle's *"There was a time I doubted Lockstep, and he's preaching the gospel"* — the warmest thing Oracle has said about him on record — and Quasar-Force's *"Come on, Lock-Step."* The professional account gained nothing this session to set against the friction.

### **3.2 Internal Influence with Teammates**

**Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`**

1. **Lock-Step** `+8.0`
   * **All-time.** Raw, demonstrated influence. He moved the team to a party none of them wanted to attend, decided the artifact would go in the vault, and made the surrender ritual de facto doctrine. Inside the Justice League's own station he assigned work and people. Session 15 was the most complete demonstration in the document — three teammates turned from *fight* to *talk first* on Kwa, and a Day 56 plan split across three parties — and Quasar-Force beat him in direct disagreement three times. In Session 16 he set the chair down himself (*"Oracle is our plan guy"*) and kept the crisis.
   * **Session 17: +8.5 → +8.0 (−0.5).** He won the argument that mattered to him and lost the one that mattered to the team.
     * **Won:** no formation was trained, and the newcomer's three hours of practice became a request to *"enlighten the group on everything that you do."* Quasar-Force put the nullifier back because he said so, and the arena bout was played as a show because he framed it as one.
     * **Lost:** when the door opened he stayed, and Intrepid and Edge walked on without him.

     Edge called him *"Captain,"* and then Edge gave up on him. Still first.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** Substantial and self-limited. His analysis sets the targets and his intelligence sets the plans; Session 13 produced the first two orders he gave inside a fight, both obeyed. He rarely spends influence against a teammate, and when he does it works: in Session 15 he beat the de facto leader three times without raising his voice. In Session 16 he shaped the plan without authoring it.
   * **Session 17: +7.0 → +7.5 (+0.5).** **The maze ran on his word.** Lock-Step asked him, *"where should we go, Quasar?"*, and his answer set the team's division of labour: *"That's me, and Oracle. And you teleport, Oracle predicts, and I figure."* When the team split on a direction in the water room, *"Quasar-Force pushes for south because of the forming partial consensus,"* and the team went south. He also tipped the formation argument: *"Better just some of us than none of us."*
3. **Oracle** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** His moral and logistical influence became directive influence several sessions ago. He calls Lock-Step in when he needs him, shuts down loose talk in front of enemies, called Nightwing knowing how Lock-Step would react, and in Session 14 said the team should rest and it rested. Session 15 was the only time he handed direction to someone else (*"I'll follow your lead"*). In Session 16 the room turned to him for the plan and he supplied one.
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.5.** The team followed him to the door — *"Oracle moves towards the door, the rest of the team follows"* — and nowhere else. His best idea of the session, not fighting at all, drew no answer from anyone, and his one intervention in the errand argument went to Lock-Step's side. Holds, and third on the tie.
4. **Edge** `+5.5`
   * **All-time.** **Influence within minutes of arriving.** In Session 16 he named the first target and the reason, and the plan was built around it; he also made the team answer the two questions it had skipped, a plan and a situation report. One adopted call, ratified by Oracle's module rather than won in argument.
   * **Session 17: +5.0 → +5.5 (+0.5).** He moved two things. The arena rule, *"We follow the rule when it's easy and when it's hard,"* was accepted by Lock-Step. *"For now, I think we should do what it says"* became the team's position with Intrepid's *"I agree too."* The formation he wanted was not trained, and at the door he gave up arguing.
5. **Intrepid** `+2.0`
   * **All-time.** *"Will go with any team decision."* He once offered to squeeze objects until they break as his contribution to a security discussion. In Session 14 he reversed his stated position four times under one teammate's single word; in Session 15, asked for an idea, *"I got nothing."* In Session 16 his answer to Lock-Step stood for the first time — about himself.
   * **Session 17: +1.0 → +2.0 (+1.0).** **The most influential session of his file, by a distance.** Two of his calls became the team's: *"We're gonna have to follow along with this arena thing,"* and *"Well, let's rest up. We're gonna have to fight in arena."* At the door, when the team stalled, he walked, and the newcomer walked with him. He also put *"Let's go AOEs"* into the pre-bout conversation, and Lock-Step built a plan on it.

*Closest call:* Quasar-Force over Oracle on a tie at `+7.5`, broken on Session 17 evidence: his calls were adopted, and Oracle's were not.

### **3.3 Internal Trust / Reliance**

**Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`**

*Not affection — who the team actually depends on.*

1. **Oracle** `+9.0`
   * **All-time.** Everyone relies on him for something they cannot do without: healing, food, honest reporting, and the medic station holding. He is the only member whose absence would change every other member's day. Session 14 showed what his absence looks like without his being absent. In Session 16 the reliance ran the whole fight: Let's Go kept Quasar-Force on the board and then held up an unconscious Lock-Step long enough for him to teleport everyone out.
   * **Session 17: holds at +9.0.** The two members who could not get up on their own got up on his Focus — 60 each, leaving him 5 of 180. ⟨Retcon⟩ These are the heals the last edition placed at the end of Session 16; they happened here, in a different room, on different patients. Already at the top of the band.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+8.0`
   * **All-time.** The team routes intelligence, analysis, tactical calls and evidence custody through him without discussion, and in Session 14 spent Focus it did not have to wake him, because it needed him working. **You do not spend your last resource waking somebody you can do without.** In Session 16 the drones were the team's eyes before the door opened.
   * **Session 17: holds at +8.0.** The maze relied on him — the keypad, in seconds, on a 26 — and so did the bunk room, for the cameras and the nullifiers. The drones did not answer, and he spent a minute frozen at the door. Holds.
3. **Intrepid** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** Relied upon absolutely for exactly one thing: nobody worries about whether Intrepid will be where the damage is. When his tools failed in Session 14, nobody blamed him, which is itself the reliance. In Session 16 he was where the damage was, and refused to be moved out of it.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.5.** He walked the stepping puzzle while the orbs fired at him, and went through the colosseum door first. Holds.
4. **Lock-Step** `+5.5`
   * **All-time.** Professionally indispensable and personally unreliable, and the team behaves accordingly: they hand him negotiations and then discuss him behind his back. **Oracle went around him to Nightwing once and would do it again.** The professional half improved sharply from Session 13, and in Session 16 the team relied on him for the way out, and he delivered all of it.
   * **Session 17: holds at +5.5.** The professional half delivered again: two teleports moved the team through two rooms of the maze, and his Protection Hex finished the stepping puzzle. The way out failed for the first time — *"out of range"* — and the personal half held the team at the door. Holds.
5. **Edge** `+5.0`
   * **All-time.** The team had known him for minutes in Session 16, and by the end it was relying on him for the exit: he held off Blitzkrieg alone so the extraction could happen, assisted two of Intrepid's attacks, and destroyed the brood's objective. The trust was earned in one fight and untested by anything else.
   * **Session 17: holds at +5.0.** He was tested by something else, as the last edition asked: a disagreement and a quiet day. The result is mixed. The team ate his food and let him take his opponent; it did not train his formation, and Lock-Step told him *"I'm still unsure about you."* Holds.

### **3.4 Team Cohesion Contribution**

**Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`**

1. **Oracle** `+9.0`
   * **All-time.** He is the reason this is a household rather than a group of people in a building. Breakfast is timed around who wakes when, and the medic station is also the place people end up talking. In Session 14 he was the one who said Wonder Woman's apology counted. To a newcomer who had just been told the adults were trash, his first words in Session 16 were *"Don't worry, the bar is low, it's alright."*
   * **Session 17: holds at +9.0.** He healed the room, slept, and stayed behind at the door so that the youngest would not come through it alone. Holds.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** Universally liked; he thanks people constantly and defuses rather than escalates. **He is the person nobody on this team wants to be in conflict with.** In Session 15 he disagreed with the most socially dominant person on the team three times without a moment of friction. In Session 16 he drew out the most honest thing Lock-Step has ever said about himself, and answered it with a yacht party.
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.5.** *"Better just some of us than none of us,"* *"Come on, Lock-Step,"* and a friendship offered across the arena floor (*"Well, we can be friends!"*). He took no side in the door argument; he was frozen in it. Holds.
3. **Edge** `+5.0`
   * **All-time.** A good first afternoon in Session 16. He met a complaint about adults with tact, asked for a plan without making anyone look bad for lacking one, and backed two of a teammate's attacks. No friction, and nothing yet that bound the group.
   * **Session 17: +4.5 → +5.0 (+0.5).** **The first person other than Oracle to feed the team.** The log: *"Edge sees that everybody is pretty down, so he tries to chill everyone by preparing and bringing food to people especially Oracle."* Then the explicit cohesion proposal — *"I need to form part of that and I want to see how do I need to move so we work together"* — and *"keep our code together."* Against it, the friction he generated getting there: the punch, *"You're not really giving up before you try. That's your problem,"* *"Get your ass back here!"* and *"I give up."* **Net, something that binds.**
4. **Intrepid** `+4.5`
   * **All-time.** No friction with anyone. He plays video games with Lock-Step, takes the hits and never sulks about a decision going the other way. The only friction on his file came in Session 14 and was defensive. His cohesion is passive and reliable.
   * **Session 17: +5.0 → +4.5 (−0.5).** The second friction on his file, and the first he started: *"You're gonna leave us to fend for ourselves last time,"* at the door, and then he walked on. He was right about the clock, and the team split.
5. **Lock-Step** `+3.0`
   * **All-time.** **The team's largest source of both morale and conflict.** He brings the popcorn, the licorice, the parties and the video games, and he got everybody paid; he also called a teammate a coward in front of a Justice Leaguer, used every teammate as stream content, and brought the Destined into the tower with a debt attached. **The morale he generates is real and has always come with a bill.** Session 16 balanced: a warm welcome to the newcomer, delivered as a lecture about adults.
   * **Session 17: +3.5 → +3.0 (−0.5).** The morale side was there in the arena, where he charmed both teams. The conflict side ran the door: he refused to go until the rules were stated, argued the penalty clock away, and the team split. He also turned down the newcomer's one cohesion proposal (*"We don't really practice it just kind of you know just"*). Staying with Quasar-Force is the best thing on this page of his file, and it is also why the others went on without him.

### **3.5 Low Operational Support Burden**

**Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`**

*What the team spends to keep this person in the field: gear, medical draw, money, transport, cover maintenance, and consequence cleanup. **Not** how sustainably they occupy the role (1.3) and **not** how stable their private life is (1.7) — this is the squad's logistics bill. High = cheap to field, or a net contributor.*

1. **Intrepid** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** He costs almost nothing. He absorbs the worst punishment on the team and repairs it himself, with no technology, no artifacts, no patrons and no consumables, and his Secret Identity maintains itself. **He is the cheapest sustained combat capability this team fields.** In Session 16, for once, he cost an extraction.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.5.** His healing factor woke him. A 72-point ram in the arena has cost nobody anything yet. Holds.
2. **Edge** `+4.5`
   * **All-time.** The second-cheapest member on arrival in Session 16. He healed himself up from exactly 0, used no technology, and his constructible weapon meant the team kept no armoury for him. His training was paid for by Black Canary. Against that: fifteen, a School obligation, housed by the team, and a legend nobody had priced.
   * **Session 17: +6.0 → +4.5 (−1.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ **He gave up the healing factor, and the medic paid for it within the hour.** He was one of the two Titans who did not wake on their own, and Oracle put 60 Focus into him. He no longer carries an iconic weapon, so there is nothing to maintain. He also paid some of the bill back in a currency nobody but Oracle spends: he fed everyone. Still second.
3. **Oracle** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** Low draw and real overhead. He is the medic, so he never consumes medical support, and he heals himself. Against that, he has **no mobility power at all**, his cybernetics are a STAR Labs maintenance dependency, and Extreme Appearance means errands that need an unremarkable face fall to someone else. **The bill for Oracle is rarely money and never medicine. It is time, and it arrives all at once.**
   * **Session 17: holds at +3.5.** The time bill again: after emptying himself on two teammates he needed most of the rest period asleep. And the module put him down at the staging point before anyone else. Holds.
4. **Lock-Step** `+1.0`
   * **All-time.** **Cheap to field and expensive to keep.** He supplies his own transport, summons his own weapon, and has no address to maintain. The bill arrives elsewhere: at 60 Health with H-DR 0 he is a heavy medical draw whenever anything lands, and his conduct generates cleanup the team pays for, now including a favour owed to the Destined.
   * **Session 17: holds at +1.0.** He woke on his own healing factor, asked to be hit, and was: the 15 Edge put into him was a draw he requested. The Destined favour is still outstanding. Holds.
5. **Quasar-Force** `−3.0`
   * **All-time.** The largest medical draw on the roster: 60 Health and no healing of his own. The −12 in Session 14 cost a surgical extraction, an overnight watch, most of Lock-Step's remaining Focus, a League medical robot, a sedative and twelve hours of mandated sleep. He needs a drone fleet, laboratory access at three sites, and imposed structure to keep him eating and sleeping. Session 15 ran the bill the other way with new hardware; Session 16 showed the hardware did not stop the draw.
   * **Session 17: −2.5 → −3.0 (−0.5).** He cost 60 of Oracle's Focus to wake. Then he cost a minute of the team's time at the door, for a penalty he drew by pulling a nullifier out of a wall. Lock-Step stayed with him, and the team argued about it until the clock ran out.

*⟨Note⟩ The anchor is still only half-restored.* Exemplar II was the only net contributor this axis has had. Edge arrived second in Session 16 at +6.0, *"cheap, not free."* **The rebuild made him cheap to equip and costly to keep upright**, and the medic now carries two members who cannot recover on their own. The burgers are the first thing on this axis that anyone but Oracle has paid back in kind.

### **3.6 Command Chair — External Appointment**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

*(Who Nightwing, Batman, Iron-Side, or the League would install.)*

1. **Oracle** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** The obvious appointment. He is sixteen, mature, medically indispensable and Nightwing's most reliable channel. He will report honestly against a teammate's wishes, complies with imposed structure, and carries **Precognition, precisely the power an appointing authority wants in a field-leader's chair.** When Nightwing needed a Titan in a room with a STAR Labs CFO, he arranged it for Oracle, and in Session 14, when the operation failed, Oracle was the one who rang the client.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.5.** No authority could appoint anyone into a pocket dimension. The appointee said, for the first time, that most of the people who would appoint him *"don't know what they're doing,"* and that Nightwing's briefing of the newcomer showed *"the kind of stuff we got to put up with."* An appointing authority would still choose him. It would now be choosing someone who has stopped assuming it is right. Holds.
2. **Quasar-Force** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Strong indicators and hard blockers. He is the historical primary Nightwing correspondent, the evidence custodian, and the team's point of contact with the League; when Lock-Step asked who the smartest person in the Justice League was, Wonder Woman pointed at him. The blockers have nothing to do with competence: he is fourteen and presents as eleven, a fatality investigation is open, an Apokoliptian-modified intelligence is bonded to his brain, and since Session 15 he has been on record endorsing lethal force by proxy. **Being the person an institution protects is close to the opposite of being the person it puts in charge.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.5.** *"The destined will kill every man and woman in that lab"* would stop an appointment on its own. It does not move a score the blockers already hold low.
3. **Edge** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** **The only member an outside authority chose for anything in Session 16** — Nightwing, as Batman, recruited and briefed him — but the appointment was to the team, not to its chair. Fifteen, an afternoon into the job, Logic −1, and a mentor whose opinion would carry weight with the League.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.0.** The appointment now has two named mentors behind it. Nothing moved.
4. **Intrepid** `1.5`
   * **All-time.** Brave and reliable, with no command indicators: Logic 0, no communications role, and no questions when Nightwing invited them.
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.5.** Nothing moved.
5. **Lock-Step** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** He has told Iron-Side to her face that her school is fake, told the room Nightwing can't be trusted, tried to conceal a dangerous artifact from him, and sedated a minor teammate. In Session 15 an external order did appoint him — to the succession of a Grey Lantern — but none of the authorities this axis lists would. **The one institution that chose him is not one his mentors can see.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.0.** He called the Grey Lantern *"my mentor technically now,"* and asked the arena's announcer to stop talking. Nothing a League appointer would see.

### **3.7 Command Chair — Internal Emergence**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

*(Who the team converges on without anyone appointing anybody. Iron-Side deliberately declines to appoint a leader or impose a chain of command, so this path is currently the live one.)*

1. **Lock-Step** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** **Emergence follows initiative, and nobody else on this team reliably supplies it.** Even inside the Justice League's station he was the one assigning work. His authority decays with absence. In Session 15 the team converged on him from the first minute to the last, and under pressure he governed by decree (*"we don't have the time to be democratic"*). In Session 16 he handed the plan to Oracle in front of the newcomer and kept the crisis.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.0.** Called *"Captain"* by the newcomer, and the team's voice in every exchange with the hosts and with Earth X357. Then the team did not follow him through the door, and he ceded the maze outright: *"you're the brains... where should we go, Quasar?"* Still first; the convergence now has edges.
2. **Quasar-Force** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** In practice he sets objectives, deconflicts targets, manages the operational picture and holds the institutional memory of the conduct standard; the room follows his analysis without treating him as its leader. In Session 15 his view prevailed every time it collided with the leader's. In Session 16 he was the brood's first target and spent most of the fight down.
   * **Session 17: 7.0 → 7.5 (+0.5).** **The team converged on him for the maze**, by name. His summary of the division of labour — *"you teleport, Oracle predicts, and I figure"* — is the clearest statement anyone has made of how this team actually runs. **He passes Oracle.**
3. **Oracle** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** The default in a crisis, by gravity: he says surrender first, holds the medic station and shuts down loose talk. Across two sessions without a leader in the room he simply *was* the lead. Session 15 was the first time he explicitly handed it to someone else; in Session 16 the room turned to him for the plan by name.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.0.** The room followed him to the door, and in the maze it followed someone else. His one strategic proposal — don't fight, *"bust out of here together"* — found no takers. Holds.
4. **Edge** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** On his first afternoon he asked the room for a plan, got *"No plan,"* and supplied the target, which became the plan within minutes. One call is not emergence, and he directed nobody in the fight.
   * **Session 17: 4.0 → 4.5 (+0.5).** He asked the room for a plan again, and this time tried to build one: a formation, three hours of practice, a map of who does what. It did not happen. The team's one moment of convergence on his line was real — Intrepid at the door. *"Captain"* was his word for someone else, which is how a newcomer behaves while he learns the room.
5. **Intrepid** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** The roster notes that he tries to lead and is not always the right man for it. The attempts are visible and they don't land. In Session 15: *"I'm not really a thinker."* In Session 16: *"Nothing, I guess."*
   * **Session 17: 2.5 → 3.0 (+0.5).** The rest period was his call. The walk through the door was his decision, and the newcomer followed it. **The first time this axis has had something to give him.**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

*(Weighted toward emergence, since no appointment currently exists.)*

1. **Oracle** `8.2`
   * **All-time.** The only member with no hard blocker on either path: first on appointment, and first in practice in every session the incumbent missed. Session 15 took 0.2 off on emergence; Session 16 gave it back when the team's emergent leader pointed at him for the plan.

Session 15 was the first session in which holding it produced a result nobody else could have produced, and a decision nobody else would have made.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.1.** Emergence held on a weaker day; appointment was already as low as it goes.
