*Volatile:* Quasar-Force over Lock-Step by 0.2. **This is the fourth consecutive session without Nightwing on screen.** ⟨Retcon⟩ The last edition's *"after the fight the team briefed Wonder Woman"* is withdrawn: the team was taken from the staging point and has reported to nobody. ⟨Note⟩ The user's reading that Wonder Woman is becoming the team's mentor is still not GM-confirmed. **Two external authorities have acted on this team without formalising a chain of command, and in Session 17 neither could reach it; a third party, the arena, set its rules and enforced them by freezing people.**

### **3.9 Overall Pecking Order**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

1. **Oracle** `8.7`
   * **All-time.** The highest reputation, moral authority and indispensable support, and a willingness to overrule that has stopped being occasional. Nightwing routes through him, and the room defers to him when it is not arguing. In Session 16 the jab became a job title — *"Oracle is our plan guy"* — and the gap to second widened to three tenths.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.7.** Unchanged on the facts. The gap to second widened again because second slipped.
2. **Lock-Step** `8.2`
   * **All-time.** The highest raw influence on the team. He sets group direction and treats others' consent as negotiable, and the ceiling has always been that the adults manage him rather than listen to him. Session 15 closed him to a tenth of first. In Session 16 he ceded the plan, kept the crisis and asked before moving a teammate, and described his own standing in new words: *"I'm just the if it all goes to hell."*
   * **Session 17: 8.4 → 8.2 (−0.2).** *"Captain,"* and then two teammates walked past him. He deferred to Quasar-Force in the maze and lost the door. **The gap to first is now half a point.**
3. **Quasar-Force** `7.4`
   * **All-time.** Indispensable capability and the team's actual brain, socially positioned as the little brother. A founding member of the League offered him sanctuary; the room still talks about him as *"our smarts"* and as *"that kid that looks like he is like 12."* **Value very high; status capped by the way the room talks to him.**
   * **Session 17: 7.2 → 7.4 (+0.2).** *"You're the brains,"* from the person whose word has carried most, and a maze run on his answers. The cap was in the same sentence (*"our youngest"*).
4. **Edge** `4.8`
   * **All-time.** Ahead of Intrepid on his first afternoon: greeted as *"somebody competent"* before he had done anything, and by the end of Session 16 his call had set the plan and his shot had ended the brood's objective. He had not yet been in a room where the team disagreed.
   * **Session 17: 4.5 → 4.8 (+0.3).** Now he has. The first disagreement on his file was with the de facto leader, on the most serious question the team has, and he was not cowed. He did not win it either.
5. **Intrepid** `3.3`
   * **All-time.** Beloved and essential in the line, and weightless in decisions. In Session 16 the first decision he held against Lock-Step was about himself.
   * **Session 17: 3.0 → 3.3 (+0.3).** **The first session in which his decisions carried other people with them.** Small, and it counts here now.

## **4\. Combat Power / Ability / Value**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

***A note on this section this revision.*** **Session 17's fight is the shortest on record: a Marvel round and one round before the session paused.** It was a staged bout. The hosts wanted at least thirty seconds of fighting, and the two teams agreed to split it into one-on-one pairings, chosen mostly by Earth X357:

- **Lock-Step v Briar Patch**, a plant controller whose barrier pushed him back and whose branches grabbed Quasar-Force.
- **Intrepid v Capricorn**, a horned brick who opened with a Marvel ram for 72.
- **Edge v Four-Fold**, a four-armed telepath.
- **Quasar-Force v Pink-Shift**, a shape-shifter with Swap Places, a teleport, healing and Lightning Actions.
- **Oracle v Facade**, a power copier who had already copied him.

What happened before the pause:

- **Edge** blasted Four-Fold twice in the Marvel round (the second a Marvel for 86) and knocked her out, then sat down in the stands. Facade's reaction Suppressive Fire stunned him for 34 Focus.
- **Pink-Shift** brought Four-Fold back from −29 to 1 with two heals, and **Quasar-Force** threw him out of the arena into the staging room.
- **Oracle** hit Pink-Shift for 15 Focus, then swung at Facade in melee and was parried into 34 damage and a stun; his Return Fire took 15 Focus off her first.
- **Lock-Step** hit Briar Patch for 42, was pushed back, teleported to the stands and was frozen for a turn by a penalty.
- **Intrepid** was rammed prone for 72, got up, and missed.

**Most of this section's movement is the two sheets.** Edge's rebuild moved 15 of his 22 combat scores and Oracle's advances four of his; play moved four, all on doctrine (§4.17, §4.18). The fight also tested several things this document has been carrying:

- **The counter-build against Oracle** was not used, and a fourth way to beat him was: his own kit, worn by somebody else.
- **The healing backup** (Lock-Step's hex-heal) was not needed, for a third session; the healer emptied himself before the bout instead.
- **Edge's senses against obscurement** were not tested.
- **Quasar-Force's Energy Absorption** has still not been bought, and nothing in the bout hit his Health.

### **4.1 Durability, Defense & Sustain — Health / Physical**

1. **Intrepid** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Best in every column: the largest Health pool on the team (210), H-DR 3, physical defences of 20/20/20, a Healing Factor that returns 7 Health every turn, Immovable and Enduring Constitution. **He is the only member who can absorb a bad round and simply keep standing.** Session 16 was the first fight in which that was not enough: 290 damage by the log's count, his Sturdy stolen for two rounds, and two knockdowns, to −3 and to −49.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** Capricorn's opening ram rolled a Marvel before its edge, so Karma could not touch it. It did 72 and put him prone, and Capricorn took 24 in recoil. He stood up and swung. Holds.
2. **Oracle** `6.3`
   * **All-time.** 90 Health looks fragile and isn't. **Real Jinx imposes trouble on every direct attack against him, permanently**, stacked with H-DR 3, Charmed Life, Danger Sense, a Healing Factor of 3 a turn, and Healing Hands, which converts his own Focus into his own Health. In Session 14 his body was never attacked. In Session 16 it was, and it lost: 28 and then a Marvel Banging Heads for 72, down at −2, and up the next round on his own healing.
   * **Session 17: 6.0 → 6.3 (+0.3).** ⟨Advance⟩ Agility 5 lifts his Melee and Agility defences to 18 through Evasion and Sturdy 3. The one hit he took in Session 17 came through all of it anyway: his copy's Point-Blank Parry, for 34 and a stun. Real Jinx makes direct attacks on him harder, and a reaction to his own swing is a hard thing to jinx. **He passes Edge.**
3. **Edge** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** On the Session 16 sheet: 90 Health, H-DR 2, physical defences 19/19/15, a Healing Factor of 3, and Slow-Motion Dodge, which worked three times in four. He took 102 damage, including glitch bombs for 37 and 43 that ignored his damage reduction, went down at exactly 0, and got up on the healing factor.
   * **Session 17: 6.2 → 5.5 (−0.7).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ Sturdy 3 replaces Sturdy 2 (H-DR 3; defences 19/19/16), and the two things that kept him up in Session 16 are gone: the healing factor and the dodge. He was one of the two Titans who did not wake on their own. **A sound mid-weight body with no sustain.**
4. **Lock-Step** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Feast or famine. Protection Hex routes everything to Ego 20, which is functionally unhittable against most opposition. When something does land, 60 Health with H-DR 0 (1 under the Hex) evaporates. **His best armour is discipline**, and a teleporting phaser in Session 16 beat it with one Marvel-20 glitch bomb, 60 to −24.
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.5.** The famine in miniature, by request: Edge's punch did 15 and took him *"nearly back down to zero"* while the Hex was down. *"That defensive spell... I definitely don't ever want to take that off."* In the arena the plant barrier pushed him back without hurting him. Holds.
5. **Quasar-Force** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** 60 Health, no healing factor and no self-heal. He has finished sessions at 1, 4, −11, −12 and −30 Health. **There is no version of this axis where a character who can be removed from full to unconscious by one strike is not last.** Session 15 put Sturdy 3 in the nanosuit (H-DR 3, defences 19/19/15). In Session 16 neither knockdown was decided by it: a Marvel and a reaction strike came through his damage reduction, and a glitch bomb ignored it.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.0.** He took no Health damage in the session. He was restrained instead, by Briar Patch's branches. Holds.

*Roadmap note:* ⟨Ruling⟩ With Sturdy 3 in the nanosuit, Telekinetic Protection is closed to Quasar-Force by house rule. Energy Absorption, on his Rank 3→4 plan, converts Health damage after damage reduction into Focus, and it remains the single highest-leverage purchase on the team's board. Session 17 added nothing for or against it.

### **4.2 Durability, Defense & Sustain — Focus / Mental**

*Focus pool, Focus damage reduction, mental defences, and demonstrated resistance to psychic attack.*

1. **Lock-Step** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** The best demonstrated mental defence on the team. His Ego defence is 20 at baseline, and **Protection Hex routes every incoming attack and effect through it**, so there is no soft angle. His F-DR of 3 is tied for the highest on the team, and Argent's mind control failed against him twice. The cracks: a 90 Focus pool that can be drained fast, and since Session 15 the first logged entry past the Hex.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.0.** His hexed telepathic link went looking for Blitzkrieg and found no mind to reach. That is not a defence; it was the first sign that they had left the dimension. Nothing attacked his mind. Holds.
2. **Quasar-Force** `8.8`
   * **All-time.** A 180 Focus pool and a record that cuts both ways: Viral failed to get in, and Psyche made contact at will. Session 15's circlet put a lock on the door — Uncanny 3 for **Vigilance 18, Ego 20 via Integrity, Logic 20 and F-DR 3** — and in Session 16 the lock held on its first test, against a power thief.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.8.** No mental attack. The one strain was internal: *"trouble concentrating... something about the buzzing,"* and a box in distress that he could not calm. ⟨Volatile⟩ Whether the buzzing is the dimension, the nullifier banks or the box, nobody knows. *Closest call:* Lock-Step over Quasar-Force by 0.2, unchanged.
3. **Edge** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** A middle profile on the Session 16 sheet: Focus 90, F-DR 2, Vigilance 15 and Ego and Logic 16. Nothing attacked his mind.
   * **Session 17: 5.5 → 5.0 (−0.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ Two defences up and one down. Ego and Logic rise to 18 (Uncanny 1, with Wisdom on Logic); Vigilance falls to 13, the lowest on the team; F-DR falls to 1. **The first mental-track hit of his file went through the low door:** Facade's reaction Suppressive Fire, a Marvel, 34 Focus and a stun.
4. **Oracle** `4.0`
   * **All-time.** The most lopsided profile in the section: Focus 180 and Vigilance 16 against **Ego defence 10** and F-DR 0. Session 14 emptied him without using the soft door, and in Session 15 a telepath got in without visible effort. **F-DR 0 against a repeating drain is an open tap.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.0.** ⟨Advance⟩ The two advances are declared, and neither is Uncanny. The ⟨Flag⟩ this entry carried for three revisions resolves to no change: the soft door is still ten points below the top two. Nothing attacked his mind; his module, again, reacted to a transfer between places before he did (2.1).
5. **Intrepid** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** Ego 11 and Logic 11 are the weakest pair of mental defences on the team, partly offset by Iron Will and Fearless, which are real mechanical resistances, and F-DR 1. He survives mental pressure by being stubborn rather than defended. The progenitor's link in Session 15 and the stolen Sturdy in Session 16 both came in through this side.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.5.** A telepath linked to him mid-bout to ask whether he was all right, and he let her. A Telepathic Link can be refused by anyone who does not want it. Not an attack. Holds.

### **4.3 Stamina & Resource Efficiency**

1. **Intrepid** `9.2`
   * **All-time.** 90 Focus and almost nothing to spend it on: Clobber, Banging Heads, Crushing Grip and Immovable are free, and only Smash and Ground-Shaking Stomp cost. His Healing Factor returns 7 Health a turn against a 210-Health pool. He can fight all day; in Session 16 the healing factor worked on him while he lay unconscious at −49.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.2.** It woke him in the bunk room. Nothing in the bout tested it.
2. **Quasar-Force** `6.5`
   * **All-time.** The largest Focus pool on the team at 180, driving a cheap core loop (Grab 5, Attack 5, Barrier 5, Crush 10). **His limiting resource isn't Focus, it's Health.** He ended Session 14 with a nearly full Focus pool and −12 Health, and Session 16 ran the demonstration twice.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.5.** Grab and toss in one action; Focus was never the question.
3. **Edge** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** **The cheapest kit on the team to run**, on the Session 16 sheet: Suppressive Fire, Double Tap and Slow-Motion Dodge cost nothing, and only Stopping Power cost 5. On 90 Focus with a healing factor he could shoot every round of any fight the team has had, and in Session 16 he did.
   * **Session 17: 7.0 → 5.5 (−1.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ The cheapest kit on the team became a costed one.
     * **Elemental Burst** is still free.
     * **Elemental Blast**, his signature, costs 5 Focus or more, and scales at +1 damage for every 2 Focus spent; Lightning Actions lets him fire it twice a round.
     * **Supernova** costs 15 and up.

     On a 90 Focus pool, with F-DR 1 and no healing factor, that is a sprinter's budget if he spends it the way his kit invites. Still third, ahead of Oracle's medical budget.
4. **Oracle** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** 180 Focus, and he is the team's battery: every point of Healing Hands is a point of Focus, spent alongside Let's Go, Never Surrender, Return Fire and Precognition. In Session 9 he deliberately under-healed a teammate to keep reserves for a raid. In Session 14 an opponent worked out that draining him is worth more than hitting anyone, and did. In Session 16 the battery lasted a fight that put every member of the team below zero.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.0.** ⟨Retcon⟩ The last edition credited him with heals at the end of Session 16 that did not happen there. What the log now shows is better evidence of the same thing. After seven rounds, an abduction and an unknown interval he still had about 125 Focus, and he spent 120 of it in the bunk room getting two teammates up. That left 5 of 180, and a day of sleep to refill it. Holds.
5. **Lock-Step** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** A 90-point pool, tied for the shallowest, against the hungriest demands: Protection Hex at 10, Powerful Hex at base cost plus 5, Chain Strikes at 5, and teleports at 10 to 15. **He is a sprinter.** In Session 16 the pool was exactly as deep as the extraction and not a point deeper.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.5.** A Powerful Hex link that found nobody, a Phase Self for a handshake, Protection Hex for the orbs, two maze teleports for the team and a Chain Strike, on a day that started with a full rest. Holds.

###

### **4.4 Crowd Control & Area Denial**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** The most flexible controller by a distance. Telekinetic Barrier spans two spaces per rank, absorbs anything doing 10 or less, and lets him choose which side of it a target ends up on. Telekinetic Grab has a **break-out target number of 20**, which most opponents cannot roll. In Session 16 his grab held the brood's first target through her own force field until Lock-Step finished her.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.0.** He grabbed the enemy healer and threw him out of the arena into the staging room in one action. Then Briar Patch grabbed him with branches, and the session paused before he tried to escape. Holds.
2. **Lock-Step** `6.5`
   * **All-time.** Broad and inconsistent: ice control, paralysis riders on chain-whip hits, a Powerful Hex sleep effect that took Aegis-Girl out of a fight entirely, and Teleport Other for removing enemies or extracting allies. The output depends on the hex configuration and the die.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.5.** No control used. He struck, and a plant barrier pushed him back.
3. **Intrepid** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** Denial by physics. Ground-Shaking Stomp prones everything within reach plus rank on a Fantastic, Banging Heads takes two, Crushing Grip holds one, and Immovable makes him a wall that cannot be repositioned. In Session 16 Banging Heads collapsed a force field.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.5.** No stomp. He was the one knocked prone.
4. **Oracle** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** Zone control by threat rather than by physics: Suppressive Fire stuns on a Fantastic, Return Fire punishes anyone who declares against him, and held overwatch actions have frozen reinforcements in a hallway. In Session 16 the stuns landed — a super-Marvel on Argent and two on Dragonborne — and a demoralise drove the brood's strongest body out of the fight.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.0.** One Suppressive Fire and one Return Fire, 15 Focus each, and no stun. Holds.
5. **Edge** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** On the Session 16 sheet he carried the same Suppressive Fire as Oracle at ×7, which did 49, 27 and 31 Focus, and none of it rolled the Fantastic that stuns. No other control.
   * **Session 17: 4.0 → 5.0 (+1.0).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ He traded the Suppressive Fire for area denial. Supernova (Force) reaches every enemy within ten spaces, and on a Fantastic each of them suffers Force's effect: trouble on every action for a round. Elemental Blast's Fantastic carries the same rider on one target. No grab, no barrier, and no Supernova thrown yet.

*Tie:* Oracle over Edge at `5.0`, broken on record: Oracle's stuns have landed, and Edge's area effect has not been used.

### **4.5 Grappling & Restraint**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** **Telekinetic Grab's break-out target of 20** makes it soft removal rather than a hindrance. He has held Aegis-Girl, White-Out, Argent and Stote, and taken contested artifacts out of thieves' and a goddess's hands. In Session 16 he grabbed Argent through her force field, crushed Stote unconscious, and heard the brood say that grappling was why it went for him first.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** Pink-Shift was grabbed and thrown in one action. **For the first time on record somebody grabbed him back**: Briar Patch's branches, mid-round. ⟨Ruling⟩ Telekinesis exerts physical strength, so his Logic can stand in for Melee on the escape — the most favourable ruling in the campaign, for him. The pause came before he tried it.
2. **Lock-Step** `6.7`
   * **All-time.** The handcuff ritual is restraint as doctrine, and it works: Nova and Ghost-Ape cuffed themselves voluntarily. His chain-whip paralysis riders have landed repeatedly, and Melee 7 adds to both halves of the axis. In Session 16 Argent began to accept the cuffs before her controller reasserted itself.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.7.** No cuffs were offered; the one-on-one framing had no place for them. Holds.
3. **Intrepid** `6.3`
   * **All-time.** Crushing Grip at ×7+7, single-target and without finesse. For twelve sessions nothing got out; then it failed twice, against Umbra and against Ecliptica's force field. In Session 16 Banging Heads collapsed the force field his grab never found an answer to.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.3.** No grab.
4. **Edge** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** On the Session 16 sheet, a marksman with no grapple tools and Melee 0, and a Melee defence of 19 that made him hard to grab.
   * **Session 17: 2.0 → 3.0 (+1.0).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ The escape half improved sharply: Melee 6 with Brawling, and under the house rule Force elemental control can stand in for Melee on escapes, from his Ego 7. He still has nothing to hold anyone with.
5. **Oracle** `1.5`
   * **All-time.** A ranged attacker with no grapple tools; Suppressive Fire pins a position, not a person. In Session 14 Ecliptica grabbed him twice and still had him when she left: *"Oracle has no ego or logic based powers that project strength so he only has his melee of −1 to use for escaping the grab."*
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.5.** ⟨Advance⟩ ⟨Flag closed⟩ The advances bought Agility and Accuracy. **The escape power is not on the sheet, and with his Rank 3→4 track now complete, it cannot arrive before Rank 4.** This entry was understating nothing.

###

### **4.6 Objective Control**

*Taking and holding ground, protecting a point, denying a location.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.3`
   * **All-time.** Barriers spanning two spaces per rank, placed at will. **Nobody else on the team can create terrain.** In Session 14 his method against a theft — pick the objective up and put it where the enemy is not — was correct, and Phase Object beat it. Since Session 15 the tower's shield denies the Titans' floors to teleporters while nobody stands on it.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.3.** In a one-on-one the objective is the opponent, and he removed his from the arena. Holds.
2. **Intrepid** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** **Immovable is objective control as a physical law.** He stands where the objective is, cannot be repositioned, and clears everything near it with a stomp. In Session 16 Argent's sphere pushed Oracle out and did not move him.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.0.** Capricorn's ram put him prone; Immovable answers knockback, not a fall. Holds.
3. **Edge** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** **He decided the Session 16 objective.** The brood came for a device, and when Blitzkrieg was one turn from phasing out with it, he shot it out of her hand — with trouble for the disarm — and destroyed it. At the end he held off Blitzkrieg alone so the extraction could start.
   * **Session 17: 7.5 → 7.0 (−0.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ The called shot that decided Session 16 belonged to a kit he no longer has: Accuracy 4 on a disarm. What he has instead is area denial — a ten-space Supernova that makes standing near an objective expensive.
4. **Oracle** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** Control by threat rather than by presence: held overwatch actions that froze reinforcements in a hallway, and Real Jinx making approach a losing proposition. In Session 12 he interposed to keep the Celestial Core's containment standing; in Session 14 the enemy made *him* the objective; in Session 16 he drove off the one member of the brood who had found the device.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.0.** He was swapped next to a copy of himself and lost the exchange. Holds.
5. **Lock-Step** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** Superb at *contesting* objectives and poor at holding them. He arrives instantly anywhere and leaves the same way.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.0.** He struck, was pushed back, and retreated to the stands.

*Tie:* Edge over Oracle at `7.0`, broken on record: the one objective this team has had decided at range was decided by Edge.

### **4.7 Reaction / Response Speed**

*Initiative, reactions, interrupts, and acting before the enemy does.*

1. **Oracle** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Not close. The module's Danger Sense guarantees him a turn in the bonus round of every combat, and he runs Combat Reflexes, Return Fire, Charmed Life and Precognition 1 — he acts on information the enemy has not generated yet. In Session 16 an enemy found the first counter: Dragonborne spent his own reaction redirecting Oracle's Return Fire onto Lock-Step.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** Bonus round as always, with a Suppressive Fire on Pink-Shift, and Return Fire went off before Facade's stun landed. The counter this time was his own reaction set, worn by somebody else. Pink-Shift swapped him into position before he moved, and Facade's Point-Blank Parry answered his swing. Holds.
2. **Edge** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** On the Session 16 sheet: Combat Reflexes, Situational Awareness and Slow-Motion Dodge, a free reaction that put trouble on attacks against his Agility defence and worked three times in four.
   * **Session 17: 7.5 → 8.5 (+1.0).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ **Lightning Actions changes what his reactions are.** It turns his initiative Marvel die into a Fantastic, so he is always in the bonus round. Once a round it lets him take a standard action as a reaction: a second Elemental Blast, or anything else. In Session 17's Marvel round he used both halves — in first, two blasts, one knockout. He lost the dodge and Situational Awareness. **Second, behind only Oracle, and past Lock-Step.**
3. **Lock-Step** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** Blink as a reaction negates an attack outright, and Quick Phase via hex adds another interrupt layer. In Session 14 he was never punished for four turns of teleport-in and strike; in Session 16 a teleporting enemy reached him first, outside the warehouse.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.0.** He phased his hand through a handshake before it could be touched, which was the right reflex in front of a power copier. Holds.
4. **Quasar-Force** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** Vigilance 5, permanent flight, and Evasion converting Agility attacks onto a high Melee defence. Fast in position, with no reaction powers to spend; the circlet raised his Vigilance *defence*, which makes him a harder target, not a faster actor. In Session 16 he rolled the first Marvel initiative of his career and spent the bonus round being hit.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.5.** He acted after Pink-Shift and Oracle, and was grabbed before he could do more. Holds.
5. **Intrepid** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** Agility 0 and a Vigilance defence of 14. He is the last to act and the hardest to move once he has.
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.0.** A Marvel ram landed before its edge, the one case where Karma cannot help, and his swing missed. Holds.

### **4.8 Personal Mobility & Positioning**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Flight 2 is unmatched: combat speed at base × rank, and non-combat speed at **base × rank × 50**. He hovers permanently out of melee reach, and nobody else operates in three dimensions by default. In Session 14 and again in Session 16 he showed that positioning is a choice, not a property.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** *"Everyone just flies over the shifting rocks room."* At the door he was frozen for a minute by a penalty — the first time anything but a knockdown has stopped him moving — and in the arena branches held him. Holds.
2. **Lock-Step** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** The best *tactical* repositioning in the party, if not the fastest. Blink negates attacks, Teleport 1 covers rank × 10 in combat, and Teleport Blind reaches places he has never seen. In Session 16 the one position that failed him was the one he chose to rest in.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.0.** The teleport home failed (*"out of range"*); the teleports through the maze worked. Holds.
3. **Intrepid** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Flight 1 at base × rank. Agility 0 caps the finesse; in Session 14 he considered punching through a floor to chase a phaser, which is the exact shape of his mobility problem.
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.5.** He flew over the rocks room and walked the stepping puzzle.
4. **Edge** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** No movement power on the Session 16 sheet, and Agility 7 made him quick on foot: he shot and stepped away all fight. The roster's *Spatial Infiltrator* role had nothing on the sheet to support it.
   * **Session 17: 2.5 → 2.0 (−0.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ All seven points of Agility moved to Melee and Ego, and the roster dropped *Spatial Infiltrator* from his role. Still no movement power; he walked to the stands after his knockout. Still above Oracle.
5. **Oracle** `1.5`
   * **All-time.** No mobility power at all: Agility 4 at the time, and one STAR Labs acrobatic roof-jump. He walks, and it has cost the team tempo more than once; in Session 16 his only moves besides running were Lock-Step's.
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.5.** He was moved twice by Pink-Shift's Swap Places: once next to his copy, and once in a shell game that left the arena unsure which Oracle was which. Holds.

### **4.9 Team Mobility & Positioning**

1. **Lock-Step** `9.8`
   * **All-time.** Overwhelmingly. He is the team's strategic mobility layer. Teleport Together has inserted the entire team into a fortified lab, extracted teammates mid-fight, lifted Robin off a sacrifice altar, cleared a bank of hostages, and in Session 13 reached a facility he had never seen using a drone camera feed. In Session 16 he used it three ways in one fight, and asked first.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.8.** **The first time his way out failed.** Asked to teleport the team back to the warehouse, he found the target out of range — *"Oh fuck."* Then he moved everyone across the swinging chain-ball room and past the robot guarding the galaxy room. The failure was the dimension, not the power. Holds.
2. **Quasar-Force** `6.5`
   * **All-time.** Not transport, but the highest-value *positioning* asset: the telekinetic nudge that converts a teammate's miss into a hit, decisive in at least nine engagements, plus placing Oracle where he asks to be placed and funnelling enemies with barriers.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.5.** The maze's route was his, and so was the keypad; his nudge on Intrepid's Clobber did not land it. Holds.
3. **Oracle** `4.0`
   * **All-time.** Positioning as an information service: precognitive warnings broadcast before contact, and explicit guidance that has set up some of the team's best attacks. In Session 16 his module steered Intrepid's stomp onto the right enemy.
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.0.** Intuition named the copier before the bout, and *"Don't shoot that other Oracle"* kept the team's shots off him. Holds.
4. **Intrepid** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** He moves himself only.
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.5.** He walked through the colosseum door first.
5. **Edge** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** He moves himself only. His Session 16 rear guard is scored in 4.6 and 4.19.
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.5.** Nothing changed.

*Tie:* Intrepid over Edge at `0.5`. Nothing separates them, and the existing order stands.

### **4.10 Single-Target Damage**

*Multiplier-plus-add convention; expected values use a Marvel-die mean of 4.33 and exclude Fantastic doubling, edges, assists, and Focus investment.*

| PC | Attack ability | Multiplier + add | Avg damage / hit | On a Fantastic |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Intrepid** | Melee | ×7 +7 | **37.3** | 49 (98 doubled) |
| **Edge** | Ego (ranged Force) | ×7 +7 | **37.3** | 49 (98 doubled) |
| **Quasar-Force** | Logic | ×7 +7 | **37.3** | 49 (98 doubled) |
| **Lock-Step** *(whip)* | Melee | ×6 +7 | **33.0** | 43 (86 doubled) |
| **Oracle** | Agility | ×5 +5 | **26.7** | 35 (70 doubled) |
| *Lock-Step — unarmed* | *Melee* | *×3 +7* | *20.0* | *25 (50 doubled)* |

*Smash and Elemental Blast both add +1 to the add for every 2 Focus spent, so the first two rows are the floor of what Intrepid and Edge can do with a Focus investment.*

1. **Edge** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** On the Session 16 sheet: ×7+7 at range off Agility 7 with Accuracy 4, and Stopping Power as his Signature Attack. One Stopping Power put 101 into Stote in one action, and another Marvel knocked out Blood Blade.
   * **Session 17: 9.0 → 9.5 (+0.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ **The same base line, with Smash's scaling and a second shot.** He is now Ego ×7+7 through Elemental Blast:
     * **Two edges**, the Blast's own and Signature Attack's — the house rule's maximum.
     * **+1 to the damage bonus for every 2 Focus spent**, exactly the property that kept Intrepid ahead last revision.
     * **A second Blast each round**, as a reaction, through Lightning Actions.

     In Session 17's Marvel round two blasts, the second a Marvel for 86, took Four-Fold from full to −29. **First on the axis — the first time Intrepid has not held it.**
2. **Intrepid** `9.2`
   * **All-time.** A ×7 multiplier, and **Smash is the differentiator: +1 to the add for every 2 Focus spent, with a built-in edge**, on a Focus pool that is otherwise nearly idle. He has the highest floor, a top-two ceiling, and most of the knockouts on named opposition; in Session 14 a Marvel on Umbra removed the hammer-wielder in one action. In Session 16 the fight's knockouts went to others.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.2.** One Clobber at Capricorn, assisted by Oracle and nudged by Quasar-Force, and it missed; Capricorn pretended to be hurt anyway. Second for the first time, because the newcomer now has his scaling and fires twice.
3. **Quasar-Force** `8.7`
   * **All-time.** ×7+7 off Logic 7, tied for the best base line, delivered at five spaces per rank. Telekinetic Attack doubles and stuns on a Fantastic, and Grab into Crush chains in a single round; he is held back by what he targets and by Crush needing the grab first. In Session 16 one crush finished Stote.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.7.** The toss did 28 to Pink-Shift. Holds.
4. **Lock-Step** `7.8`
   * **All-time.** **The ×6 is not his, it is the whip's**: his base multiplier is ×3. What he does hold is **the highest Fantastic rate in the party**: Chain Strikes carries an edge, Signature Attack adds a second, and Combat Trickery guarantees a 1 on the Marvel die once per battle. In Session 16 the spike landed on people: 84 and a knockout on Argent, and 69 on Blood Blade.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.8.** A 42-point Chain Strike on Briar Patch before the plant barrier pushed him off. Holds.
5. **Oracle** `4.0`
   * **All-time.** The lowest line on the team, and **first for Focus damage rather than Health**: Suppressive Fire and Return Fire attack a different resource entirely. In Session 14 Ecliptica refunded it by draining him; in Session 16 nobody did, and the stuns and the demoralise it produced are scored in 4.4 and 4.14.
   * **Session 17: 3.5 → 4.0 (+0.5).** ⟨Advance⟩ Agility 5 and Accuracy 2 make his line ×5+5, an expected 26.7 against 21.3. Still last by almost four points, and still mostly Focus damage: in Session 17, 15 into Pink-Shift and 15 into Facade.

*Closest call:* Edge over Intrepid by 0.3, on identical base lines. Edge's two edges and second blast are set against Intrepid's record, and one turn of the new kit is a small sample.

### **4.11 Area-of-Effect Damage**

1. **Intrepid** `9.6`
   * **All-time.** Ground-Shaking Stomp checks Melee against the Agility defence of every target within reach plus rank in spaces. It has removed six monsters in one attack, then four more, then a room of mobsters, then every Lex-Corp guard in an alley. Banging Heads takes two at full damage. Until this revision he was the only member with a real area attack.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.6.** Not used. *"Let's go AOEs. Let's go,"* he said, and then reminded the team that area attacks hit everyone in them. Holds, and no longer alone.
2. **Edge** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** On the Session 16 sheet, single-target only: three attack powers, each taking one target.
   * **Session 17: 0.5 → 8.5 (+8.0).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ **The team's second area attack, and on paper the better-shaped one.** Supernova (Force) is an Ego check against the Resilience defence of every enemy within ten spaces. It does half damage on a success, and full damage plus Force's effect on a Fantastic, with +1 to the damage bonus for every 2 Focus over its 15-Focus floor. Against Intrepid's stomp it reaches more than twice as far, and it is worded to hit enemies only. **The largest single-axis move in the document's history**, and none of it tested: he did not throw it in Session 17 (*"I have AOEs, but we were supposed to go 1v1?"*). Second, 1.1 behind the member who has removed six monsters with one stomp.
3. **Lock-Step** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** Elemental Burst is functionally single-target, and whatever area effect he brings comes out of a Powerful Hex configuration that costs a turn to set up.
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Quasar-Force** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** **Effectively zero.** Telekinetic Toss damages the thrown person and one target, and that is the whole list. Nothing on his Rank 3→4 plan closes the gap.
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.0.** He did exactly that: Pink-Shift thrown, and nobody else hit.
5. **Oracle** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** Single-target ranged only.
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.5.** Nothing changed. Last again, now that the newcomer has an area power.

*The second-widest first-to-second gap in the section closes from 6.6 to 1.1.* For three revisions this axis said the team's entire area-damage capability was one person's stomp. **On paper it is now two people's.**

###

### **4.12 Ranged Combat / Engagement**

1. **Edge** `9.4`
   * **All-time.** First on arrival in Session 16, on the old sheet: Accuracy 4, a ×7 line, three ranged attack powers and the Cutting Edge. His best shot was not damage — with trouble for disarming a hand-held item, he shot the device out of Blitzkrieg's hand at the wall and destroyed it. **He also answers, on paper, the counter that beat Oracle in Session 14**: Heightened Senses 2, the profile that saw through invisibility and fog when Exemplar II had it.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.4.** ⟨Re-spec⟩ **A different rifle at the same rating.**
     * **Out:** Accuracy 4, Stopping Power, Double Tap, Suppressive Fire and the Cutting Edge.
     * **In:** Elemental Blast and Burst at ten spaces, two edges on the Blast, and a second Blast per round.

     Heavier and faster, shorter-ranged and blunter: the called shot is gone. Heightened Senses 2 stayed, so the obscurement answer is still on paper. One turn of evidence: two hits, one knockout.
2. **Oracle** `9.2`
   * **All-time.** For fifteen sessions the team's dedicated ranged specialist: Suppressive Fire stunning on a Fantastic, Return Fire punishing anyone who declares against him, and held-overwatch discipline in every engagement. Session 14 showed the quality (the module let him hit an *invisible* opponent) and the counter: **a ranged specialist with no mobility is a turret, and Session 14's opponent fogged the turret and then bolted it to the floor.** In Session 16 he lost first place without losing anything.
   * **Session 17: 9.0 → 9.2 (+0.2).** ⟨Advance⟩ Accuracy 2 and Agility 5 give him better odds and a heavier line. The two tenths are what the sheet earned. What play did was a melee swing at a copy of himself, which is scored in 1.5. **Second by two tenths.**
3. **Quasar-Force** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** **Everything he does happens at five spaces per rank**, which makes him the most consistently ranged member of the team without a single ranged *attack* trait: Telekinetic Attack, Grab, Barrier and Toss all reach.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.5.** The grab-and-toss went in at range. Holds.
4. **Lock-Step** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** The chain whip has reach and Elemental Burst projects, but his kit wants him in the target's face, and he obliges.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.0.** In, strike, pushed back, out.
5. **Intrepid** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** No ranged option whatsoever. He flies to the problem and hits it.
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.0.** Nothing changed.

### **4.13 Support — Ally Buff**

*Powers and actions that make teammates measurably better.*

1. **Oracle** `9.7`
   * **All-time.** The team's only dedicated buffer, and the margin is enormous. **Let's Go** kept Quasar-Force conscious and acting at −11 Health. **Never Surrender** pulled Intrepid out of a demoralised stun. **Danger Sense** guarantees him a turn in every bonus round, usually spent on somebody else. Since Session 15 his module also resonates with Lock-Step's powers. In Session 16 Let's Go was the hinge of the fight.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.7.** In the bout, one assist on Intrepid's Clobber, which missed. ⟨Retcon⟩ The withdrawn hand-wave also clarifies how the Session 16 extraction ran. Lock-Step was unconscious at −24 and moving only on Let's Go, and when Oracle's module put him down at the staging point, Lock-Step went down with him. **That is the strongest version yet of what this axis measures.**
2. **Quasar-Force** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** No buff *powers*, and nonetheless the second-best buffer on the team: the telekinetic nudge that turns a teammate's miss into a hit is a pure action-economy gift, decisive in at least nine engagements. The nudges need him upright.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.5.** One nudge, on the Clobber that missed.
3. **Edge** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** No buff powers, and two assists in Session 16 that each decided something; one turned Intrepid's Marvel miss into a hit that stunned Blood Blade and gave Intrepid his Sturdy back.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.0.** No assists; he spent the round after his knockout stunned. Holds.
4. **Lock-Step** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Powerful Hex can be configured into buff-adjacent effects, and Teleport Together is force projection of a kind. It is real but occasional, and it always costs him the turn.
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.5.** None in the bout.
5. **Intrepid** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** Immovable holding a line and a body absorbing hits meant for softer people are genuine passive support, not buffs. Session 14 brought the first two active assists of his campaign.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.5.** Nothing changed.

###

### **4.14 Support — Enemy Debuff**

*Effects that degrade enemy capability rather than removing enemy Health.*

1. **Oracle** `8.8`
   * **All-time.** **Real Jinx imposes trouble on every attack made against him, permanently, with no action cost and no roll.** Suppressive Fire stuns on a Fantastic, Return Fire punishes anyone who declares against him, and his Focus damage degrades enemy capability directly. In Session 16 the debuff that ended the fight was his: the brood's strongest body left on Focus damage, not Health.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.8.** Two Focus hits for 15 each, and no stun. His copy did better with his kit: a Marvel Suppressive Fire that stunned Edge, which is the best evidence yet of what the kit does on a good roll. Holds.
2. **Lock-Step** `8.4`
   * **All-time.** The broadest active debuff kit on the team: paralysis riders on chain-whip hits, a Powerful Hex sleep effect, ice control and Disrupt Person. His effects are bigger than Oracle's and need rolls and setup that Oracle's do not. *Closest call* with Oracle for three revisions, and Session 16 settled it Oracle's way.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.4.** No riders recorded.
3. **Quasar-Force** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** Control as debuff, and the numbers are brutal: **a grab break-out target of 20** makes Telekinetic Grab a soft removal rather than a hindrance. Barriers deny positioning, and Telekinetic Attack stuns on a Fantastic.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.0.** The toss removed his opponent rather than degrading him.
4. **Edge** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** On the Session 16 sheet, **Oracle's tool at nearly twice the multiplier**: Suppressive Fire at ×7 did 49, 27 and 31 Focus and set up the demoralise that ended the fight. His attempt to intimidate the last two of the brood, who were under a controller's orders, did not move them.
   * **Session 17: 7.8 → 5.5 (−2.3).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ **The Focus-damage line is gone, and with it most of this score.** What remains:
     * **Force's rider** — trouble on every action for a round — on any Fantastic with Blast, or on every enemy within ten spaces with Supernova.
     * **Intimidation**, now run off Ego 7 with the Monster trait's edge, which under the house rule makes a target operate as if demoralised for a round.

     Neither has been used yet. Fourth.
5. **Intrepid** `5.3`
   * **All-time.** Ground-Shaking Stomp prones everything within reach plus rank on a Fantastic, which is mass debuff by physics. Crushing Grip holds one and Banging Heads staggers two. In Session 16 he took his own power back with a Smash Marvel stun.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.3.** Nothing changed.

### **4.15 Healing**

1. **Oracle** `10.0`
   * **All-time.** **The only 10.0 awarded in the document.** He is the sole member with a general capacity to restore another person's Health: Healing Hands converts his Focus into their Health one for one, and Let's Go and Never Surrender reverse conditions nobody else can touch. In Session 9 he rationed his own reserves. In Session 14, drained to 3 of 180, he could not heal a teammate at −12: **the monopoly failed once, under load, at the moment it was the only thing that mattered.** In Session 16 the monopoly carried the fight.
   * **Session 17: holds at 10.0.** ⟨Retcon⟩ The post-fight heals the last edition placed at the end of Session 16 happened here instead, and to different people. Quasar-Force got 60, from −15 to 45, and Edge got 60, to 53, leaving Oracle 5 of 180. *"My battery's about gone now, though, Okay?"* **Two of the five no longer have any way to recover on their own**, and one of them had one until this revision.
2. **Lock-Step** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** **Session 14 used Powerful Hex's healing potential**: with Quasar-Force at 0 and the medic empty, he configured healing hands and brought him to 5, the second person on the roster ever to restore another's Health. A hex configuration rather than a dedicated power, and dosed to the minimum.
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.0.** ⟨Volatile⟩ Not used, for a third session. Whether the backup recurs is still open.
3. **Intrepid** `2.7`
   * **All-time.** Healing Factor, the best regeneration on the team, returning 7 Health a turn. Self only.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.7.** It woke him in the bunk room.
4. **Quasar-Force** `0.0`
   * **All-time.** **Zero.** No healing factor, no self-repair and no in-combat regeneration of any kind. Outside combat the box's bio-mechanical constructs heal him fast — three hours from −12 to consciousness — but only the in-combat half has ever decided anything.
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.0.** He did not wake on his own. Zero for a seventeenth session.
5. **Edge** `0.0`
   * **All-time.** On the Session 16 sheet, a Healing Factor at Resilience 3, self only. It got him up from exactly 0 to hold the rear guard.
   * **Session 17: 2.0 → 0.0 (−2.0).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ He gave up the healing factor to raise Ego from 6 to 7. Zero: the second member of the team with none, and he did not wake on his own.

*Tie:* Quasar-Force over Edge at `0.0`. The box repairs Quasar-Force outside combat; nothing repairs Edge.

*Structural note.* One healer and one improvised backup. **The healer now carries two members who cannot recover on their own**, and in Session 17 that cost him all but 5 of his 180 Focus before the bout began. The scenario §10.4's single-healer finding is about — a mentalist takes Oracle out of a fight — is still untested, and the counter-build that drained him in Session 14 has still not been used again.

### **4.16 Ability to Avoid Collateral Damage**

1. **Oracle** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** Ranged precision with a Charmed Life edge. He moves nothing, shakes nothing and lifts nothing, and his misses hit a wall. The one stray shot of Session 16 was his Return Fire, redirected by an enemy.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.0.** Nothing stray.
2. **Quasar-Force** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** **The most interesting entry on this axis.** His powers are inherently surgical: firearms plucked individually off gang members, a van lifted without disturbing an alley, a sleeping man cuffed without being woken, and three turns of precision work in a gallery of irreplaceable objects in Session 14. Against it, the largest destructive ceiling on the team. **The control is genuinely there; the judgement occasionally isn't.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.0.** The one piece of property damage in the session was his, and it was not collateral: he pulled a power-nullification projector out of a wall on purpose and drew a penalty for it. The toss put a person exactly where he meant to. Holds.
3. **Edge** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** A marksman's profile in Session 16: every attack one shot at one target, the most delicate aimed at a device in a villain's hand. Against it, a power the roster described as *"spatial-severing"* and a backstory that begins with a weapon sliced to pieces. **Precise in his first fight; the ceiling of what he cuts was unknown.**
   * **Session 17: 7.5 → 7.0 (−0.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ The blasts are still single-target, and Supernova is worded to hit enemies only. But the power summary now describes *"sweeping storms of blades that carve through everything around him at once,"* and the precision tool that justified the old score is gone. Half a point for the ceiling.
4. **Lock-Step** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** Teleportation is clean, the whip is single-target and his ice constructs are localised. Against: he has proposed setting fires in bushes as a plan, and Berserk removes every restraint at once.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.0.** Clean.
5. **Intrepid** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** His signature attack is, by definition, an indiscriminate area effect, and a ×7 multiplier and 301 pounds arriving by air into an enclosed room is a structural event. In Session 16 he used the stomp and it hit only an enemy.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.5.** No stomp, and the session's one reminder that area attacks hit everyone in them was his. Holds.

### **4.17 Lethal vs Non-Lethal Precision**

1. **Oracle** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** The only member whose primary attacks **cannot kill**: Suppressive Fire and Return Fire deal Focus damage. He also carries Healing Hands, Let's Go and Never Surrender, so he can un-kill other people's mistakes, and has. In Session 16 he is the reason the fight with Dragonborne ended in a retreat rather than a body.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** Two Focus attacks, and a melee swing that never landed.
2. **Lock-Step** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** **He holds a throttle nobody else has**: half of his ×6 comes from the whip, so dropping it halves his multiplier, a deliberate non-lethal setting he has used and announced. Against: a captive beaten conscious, Berserk, and in Session 15 an exception clause — *"No, I'm not coming around on lethal force"* in the room and *"cinder blocks in the ocean"* in private. In Session 16 the doctrine was stated and held under the heaviest fire the team has taken: *"Nightwing did say we can bring them back, so knock them out."*
   * **Session 17: 7.5 → 7.0 (−0.5).** The throttle held in the arena — *"if we don't have to, we should always try to not, you know, be lethal"* — and the exception clause was restated as policy the same day: *"I think here's exceptions,"* and about the errand, *"deaths we're not worried about right now."* The precision is unchanged; the doctrine got an asterisk in writing.
3. **Edge** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** On the Session 16 sheet, half of his offence was non-lethal by construction (Suppressive Fire, used three times) and the other half the hardest-hitting ranged line on the team. Nobody died. **He had a throttle, and nobody had seen him at full.**
   * **Session 17: 6.0 → 5.5 (−0.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ His non-lethal half is gone: everything he fires is Health damage now. The throttle that remains is choice — the free Elemental Burst, or a Blast with as much or as little Focus as he likes. In his first bout on the new kit he chose the heavy end: 86 on a Marvel into an opponent in a staged fight, to −29. Against that, the strongest no-kill doctrine anyone stated in the session was his, and he sat down afterwards.
4. **Quasar-Force** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** The finest control on the team, the clearest stated ethic, and **the team's only confirmed kill.** Crush is an all-or-nothing damage reaction applying ×7+7 to whatever is held; **his calibration against unknown durability is the failure mode.** Since Session 15 a second risk: *"we would be more comfortable unleashing Fade-Out with his full lethal fury."* In Session 16 the calibration held on Stote.
   * **Session 17: 3.5 → 3.0 (−0.5).** The calibration is fine: a toss for 28 into a shape-shifter he wanted to be friends with. The delegation is worse. He expects the people he helped send to kill *"every man and woman in that lab,"* and still endorses sending them.
5. **Intrepid** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** Every large swing is a near-death event against ordinary humans — a coin flip on manslaughter — and he knows it, which is why he is afraid all the time.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.0.** His opponent was a brick; the coin was not flipped.

### **4.18 De-escalation / Conflict Termination Without Force**

1. **Lock-Step** `9.7`
   * **All-time.** **The most underrated thing he does, and it is genuinely excellent.** He opens engagements offering handcuffs; Nova and Ghost-Ape cuffed themselves rather than fight him. He talked the Burnout Kings into standing aside and negotiated the Destined out of the lobby. Session 15 was the single best de-escalation this document has recorded: a team that had talked itself into a fight paused, and first contact ended in terms both sides accepted. In Session 16 his offer reached the controlled teenagers and lost to the thing controlling them.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.7.** **He turned a death-match setup into a sparring session.**
     * He asked the announcer whether it was *"like playing dodgeball or something,"* and argued the hosts into letting the teams keep talking.
     * He proposed *"our best one or two moves"* for the thirty seconds the show required.
     * He set the rule: *"Let's put on a show, you know, have a good time, nobody kills each other."*

     Earth X357 agreed, and by the end of the first round Capricorn was pretending to be hit. Against it, the suggestion to disguise area attacks as one-on-one. Already at the ceiling this scale allows him.
2. **Oracle** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** He demands surrender before nearly every engagement, without exception and without being told to. His version is less charismatic than Lock-Step's and applied more consistently. On Kwa his first read was elimination, and he talked himself out of it on the evidence.
   * **Session 17: 8.0 → 8.5 (+0.5).** **The most de-escalatory position anyone took: don't fight.** *"Hey, let's not fight at all. See what they do."* *"Why don't we all just bust out of here together."* *"Somebody's got to be able to get us out of here."* It did not carry, because Four-Fold had just been frozen for suggesting the same thing. Half a point for being right.
3. **Quasar-Force** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** Compassion as a termination tool, effective in a narrow band and remarkable inside it: Argent surrendered while he held her, Cassidy Yanks gave her real name because he was kind to her, and World Ender's villain persona collapsed under sincerity. In Session 16 he read correctly that the controlled five could not be talked to.
   * **Session 17: 7.0 → 7.5 (+0.5).** *"Impress the file without hitting each other,"* and to his own opponent, *"Well, we can be friends!"* — which got Pink-Shift to give his real name. He argued for honour toward the other team. Then he threw Pink-Shift out of the arena, which in a staged fight is a way of ending one pairing without hurting anybody much.
4. **Edge** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** In Session 16 his instinct before the door opened was a plan, not a fight. His one attempt to end things without force was the Monster trait used to intimidate, and it failed against opponents whose will was not their own.
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.0.** He ended his pairing in one turn, by force, and then refused to continue, sitting in the stands. His contribution to the conversation was *"Let's try to not get more penalizations... Let's go to business."* Holds.
5. **Intrepid** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** On the record, Shadow-Boxer would have got nothing out of him but fist. In Session 15 he was the member most ready to attack a species the team then saved.
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.5.** *"Yep, sure thing,"* to Capricorn's offer to hit him.

### **4.19 Rescue / Civilian Protection**

1. **Lock-Step** `9.8`
   * **All-time.** The best rescue tool on the team by a distance. He teleported a bank's worth of hostages clear, lifted Robin off a sacrifice altar, extracted teammates mid-fight, and in Session 13 freed three mind-controlled hostages at once by knocking out the thing holding the leash. **Teleport Together is the only power on this roster that removes a person from danger instantly.** Session 15's largest rescue was done without a power, on Kwa. In Session 16 he got four teammates out of a hostile building, asking before he moved the one who was conscious.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.8.** The teleport home failed; the teleports through the maze did not. And he stayed with the frozen youngest rather than leave him to whoever might come — *"how about if... they found Quasar-Force while he can't move?"* Holds.
2. **Oracle** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** Rescue after the fact and by prevention: he revives, he heals, and he demands surrender first precisely so that nobody needs rescuing. In Session 14 he could not, for once; in Session 16 the person he would have reached first went down twice, and Let's Go reached him both times.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.5.** The heals in the bunk room were rescue in its plainest form.
3. **Quasar-Force** `8.4`
   * **All-time.** He pulled unconscious bodies out of a room filling with toxic gas, cut Doctor Varga out of webbing, proposed the parasite screening that caught three infections, and in Session 14 took a blade through the abdomen to deny a goddess an artifact. Since Session 15 the tower shield protects at the scale of a building.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.4.** *"If we did get nabbed by these arena press gangers, we may not actually have parasites on us"* — the screening logic, applied to themselves.
4. **Intrepid** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** He absorbs the damage that would otherwise land on someone softer, and Immovable makes him a wall people can stand behind. On Kwa he offered the scout *"You could always use my body as a shield."*
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.0.** First through every door.
5. **Edge** `6.8`
   * **All-time.** **The rear guard** of Session 16: he held off Blitzkrieg alone for a round so the extraction could start.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.8.** Nothing changed.

*⟨Note⟩ The five teenagers from Session 16 are where the team left them.* If the hosts keep their promise, the team returns to the moment it left: three of the five unconscious in a mob warehouse, one in Lock-Step's cuffs, two at large, and none secured, screened or cured. **Nothing in Session 17 moved that, and nothing could.**

### **4.20 Combat Synergy / Combined-Arms Compatibility**

*How much better this person makes everyone else's kit work.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** **The telekinetic nudge that converts a teammate's miss into a hit is the most efficient action economy on the team**, decisive in at least nine engagements. He guided Intrepid to the square from which one stomp removed six monsters, places Oracle where Oracle asks to be placed, and builds barriers that funnel enemies into the brawlers. In Session 16 his grab was the platform for the first knockout.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** A nudge on Intrepid's Clobber, and the clearest description anyone has given of how the team's kits fit: *"you teleport, Oracle predicts, and I figure."*
2. **Oracle** `9.3`
   * **All-time.** He heals, buffs, revives and positions, and takes a turn in every bonus round. **He raises everyone's floor where Quasar-Force raises their ceiling.** Session 15's Grey Lantern resonance was the first synergy on this roster that exists because two characters' powers are compatible. In Session 16 his Focus damage and Edge's stacked to break Dragonborne.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.3.** An assist on Intrepid, and Intuition's read on the copier before the bout.
3. **Lock-Step** `8.2`
   * **All-time.** Enormous positive potential and a recurring negative: he inserts the whole team into places it could not otherwise reach, and he removes people from fights without asking. Session 14 was the most-assisted performance on record: four pieces of teamwork from four teammates in one combat. In Session 16 he asked before moving a teammate and built a one-turn combined-arms play around Quasar-Force's grab.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.2.** He turned down the formation the newcomer asked for — *"our maneuvers is like dancing... you just fill it in the moment"* — and then asked the newcomer to explain his kit. The maze ran on two of his teleports. Holds.
4. **Edge** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** **His first attack for the team, in Session 16, was aided by all four of his new teammates.** He returned it: two aids to Intrepid, Focus damage that set up Oracle's demoralise, and the target call the plan was built on.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.0.** ⟨Re-spec⟩ A kit that fits better than the old one: Supernova is worded to spare allies, which matters on a team whose tank lives in melee. He also asked for exactly what this axis measures — *"I want to see how do I need to move so we work together"* — and did not get it. Holds.
5. **Intrepid** `6.5`
   * **All-time.** He holds the line so that four fragile people can operate behind it: passive synergy of high value. In Session 16 the two best moments of his fight came on Edge's aid.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.5.** He took Oracle's assist and Quasar-Force's nudge and missed anyway.

### **4.21 Overall Combat Value / Contribution**

*Not raw power — contribution to the team winning and surviving. This axis and the next are deliberately different questions, and the difference is the point.*

1. **Oracle** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** **He is the reason nobody has died.** Let's Go, Never Surrender, Healing Hands, Real Jinx and Danger Sense, and Session 14's absolute floor: grabbed, blinded and drained to 3 Focus, he still dodged an invisible attacker, landed Return Fire, and redirected the swing that freed him. In Session 16 he opened the fight, kept it going and ended it, and his Let's Go held Lock-Step up for the extraction.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.0.** The most valuable thing he did for the arena bout he did the day before: two teammates healed onto their feet. In the bout itself he got Intuition's read on the copier and one good shot, and lost the exchange with his copy. Holds.
2. **Edge** `8.7`
   * **All-time.** **Second on arrival in Session 16.** He named the first target, knocked out the member of the brood who had stolen a Titan's power, put 101 into Stote in one action, dealt 107 Focus damage, turned a Marvel miss into the hit that returned Intrepid's Sturdy, destroyed the device, and held the rear.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.7.** ⟨Re-spec⟩ One turn and one knockout, from first initiative: the fastest removal of an opponent in the campaign. Then a reaction stun and a seat in the stands. Holds.
3. **Lock-Step** `8.4`
   * **All-time.** Insertion and extraction nobody else can provide, the decisive blow on more named villains than anyone, and a surrender ritual that has ended fights before they started. Docked for two Berserk episodes and the non-consensual extractions. In Session 16 he finished the plan's first target and brought everyone home.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.4.** A 42-point strike, a push-back, and a penalty that cost a turn. Holds.
4. **Quasar-Force** `8.2`
   * **All-time.** Control that removes named opponents for multiple rounds, mobility, an assist economy, barriers and joint-highest base damage. **What caps him is not contribution but survival.** In Session 16 the brood said he was its first target because of what he does when he is standing.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.2.** He removed the enemy healer from the arena and was restrained in reply. Holds.
5. **Intrepid** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** He absorbs the damage that would kill anyone else on the team and ends fights outright. In Session 16 he absorbed 290 damage, took his own power back, and refused to leave; the fight's worst damage went to him, which is what he is for.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.0.** He took the bout's biggest hit, and missed his swing. Holds.

*Volatile:* positions two through five are separated by 0.7, and nothing in a one-turn bout moved any of them.

###

### **4.22 Overall Combat Power / Potency**

*Raw capability to impose an outcome by force, as distinct from 4.21's contribution. Compare the two orderings.*

1. **Intrepid** `8.3`
   * **All-time.** Elite physical durability, elite area damage, elite single-target damage, elite stamina, and the best objective-holding on the team via Immovable: **the highest raw combat output on the roster.** In Session 16 the weight showed (290 damage absorbed), and so did the limit: a power thief, and a flying brick of his own class he could not put down.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.3.** Unchanged, and now first by two tenths rather than four.
2. **Edge** `8.1`
   * **All-time.** **A specialist's potency** on the Session 16 sheet: tied for the best base damage, the best ranged attack, a free Focus-damage line, a dodge, a healing factor and the only Heightened Senses, against 90 Health, no area attack, no control and middling mental defences.
   * **Session 17: 7.9 → 8.1 (+0.2).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ More potent and less durable. He is now first on single-target damage and second on area damage and reaction speed. Against that, he lost the healing factor, the dodge and the Focus-damage line. **Two tenths behind Intrepid.**
3. **Quasar-Force** `7.8`
   * **All-time.** Best crowd control, grappling, personal mobility, combat synergy and objective creation, and joint-highest base damage. Against it: paper Health, no healing of any kind, effectively no area damage, and a demonstrated one-strike removal. **His ceiling is the highest on the team, and his floor is the reason it is rarely reached.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.8.** Nothing changed.
4. **Lock-Step** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** Best mental durability, team mobility, de-escalation and rescue, and the highest Fantastic rate in the party, held down by a shallow Focus pool, fragile Health, damage that belongs to the whip, and Berserk.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Oracle** `7.4`
   * **All-time.** First on more of the twenty-two combat sub-axes than anyone else, and sunk on raw potency by three narrow weaknesses — Focus durability, stamina and grappling — that Session 14's opponent hit with two effects. **A character whose weaknesses are narrow and known is a character an opponent can build against, and somebody did.**
   * **Session 17: 7.2 → 7.4 (+0.2).** ⟨Advance⟩ A heavier line and two points of defence. The weaknesses the counter-build targets are untouched, and a copy of him found a fourth way to beat him: his own kit. **Still last, a tenth behind Lock-Step**; he is first on seven combat sub-axes — Reaction, Ally Buff, Enemy Debuff, Healing, Collateral Avoidance, Non-Lethal Precision and Combat Value.

**Read this against 4.21 and the inversion holds.** Oracle is last here and first there; Intrepid is first here and last there. **Edge is second on both**, the only member whose contribution and potency agree. The spread on this axis narrows from 1.1 to 0.9, the narrowest full spread in the document.

## **5\. Non-Combat Power / Ability / Value**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

*Session 17 was mostly non-combat: a day in a bunk room with cameras in the walls, a maze, and a conversation with another universe's Titans. Two members gained most of the movement. Edge has Ego 7 now — level with Lock-Step for the highest social ability score on the team — plus a day of organising, feeding and arguing. Lock-Step drew the session's best intelligence out of a loudspeaker. Edge's Logic is still −1, the lowest on the roster, so ties between him and Intrepid on the Logic-driven axes are still broken on Logic.*

### **5.1 Leadership / Organization**

*Running things, as distinct from being followed — see 3.6–3.8 for command.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `8.3`
   * **All-time.** **Organisationally the best on the team, and long unwilling to command.** In Session 14 he started the whole Titans Tower security programme and recruited the one specialist qualified to build it. Session 15 was the first session in which he also ran things: a six-day engineering programme delivered in priority order, then a layered surveillance operation on Day 56. In Session 16 his organising was intelligence: the drone sweep and the deduction about the five.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.3.** He organised the searches — parts, cameras, implants — and the maze's route. *Closest call* with Oracle at a tenth, for a third revision.
2. **Oracle** `8.2`
   * **All-time.** He runs the household on a schedule, staffs the medic station, keeps the patrol rota and files honest reports, and he has acted unilaterally on principle and run two sessions outright. In Session 15 his forecast gave the room something to plan around; in Session 16 the plan came from him after he had said there wasn't one.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.2.** He triaged the room and then slept, which was the right organisation for a medic at 5 of 180. Holds.
3. **Lock-Step** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** He leads by acting first, and for most of the campaign organised nothing. Session 14 brought real staff work, and Session 15 the largest movement on this axis: preparation before first contact, a team huddle that diagnosed the gap correctly, a week-long broadcast operation, and a three-party division of labour under a clock. In Session 16 he handed the plan to Oracle and ran the exit.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.0.** The organising he did was the intelligence work with the hosts (5.4) and the frame for the bout. The organising he refused to do was the formation. Holds.
4. **Edge** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** **The newcomer made the team plan.** In Session 16, *"Do we have a plan for when we enter this place?"* and *"Can you tell me the situation, please?"* were the two questions the team had not asked itself, and the plan's target was his.
   * **Session 17: 4.5 → 5.5 (+1.0).** **The first organising on his file, and it was the right kind.** He proposed a team maneuver, asked for three of the 24 hours to train it, and asked each teammate how they move so he could fit in (*"show me how do you do that maneuver right now with practice"*). He fed the room, and set the order of work: *"one problem at a time... get out of here first."* None of it was adopted except the food. A point, for what he tried to run.
5. **Intrepid** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** The roster notes that he tries to lead and is not always the right man for the job; the attempts are visible and nothing lands. Asked for a plan on Day 56, *"I got nothing,"* and in Session 16, *"Nothing, I guess."*
   * **Session 17: 2.5 → 3.0 (+0.5).** One piece of organisation the whole team followed: *"Well, let's rest up. We're gonna have to fight in arena."*

### **5.2 Engineering & Crafting / Fabrication & Repair**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.8`
   * **All-time.** Uncontested. Logic 7 with Brilliance 4 gives an effective +11, on top of Inventor, Scientific Expertise and lab access at three sites. He runs and modifies the drone fleet and once built four new drones in three days. Session 15 was the most productive week of engineering in the campaign: a New God signal mask, an anti-teleport device that beat the Destined in play, a nanosuit upgrade, and two drones for a teammate. **Three GM-approved builds from one week of downtime.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.8.** No parts and no tools: he bombed the search. The one device he got his hands on was a power-nullification projector he tore out of a wall and started to open, until the penalty clock told him to stop. Holds.
2. **Oracle** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic 3 with Tech Reliance and Eidetic Memory, and he understands the STAR Labs systems he is built from well enough to interface with them. He has not been shown building anything. **He is not the engineer; he is part of the bill of materials.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.5.** Nothing changed.
3. **Lock-Step** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** Logic 0. He recognises when technology is valuable and cannot do anything with it himself, but he knows who to ask.
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.0.** He knew who to stop: *"Just put it in."*
4. **Intrepid** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic 0, and accurate about it: *"I don't have any tech skills."*
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.5.** *"There's a lot of stuff in this place, I think. Maybe you can make something."*
5. **Edge** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic −1.
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.5.** *"I'm not very good with Tick Savvy stuff. I will bring you stuff."* The parts list went over his head.

*Tie:* Intrepid over Edge at `0.5`, broken on Logic (0 against −1).

### **5.3 Cyber & Information Warfare & Security**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.3`
   * **All-time.** He rebuilt the drone fleet's security after the Destined breach, rotated every code, established three separate archives, and fields a communications-blackout warfare suite. Nightwing credited him by name. Session 15's circlet is information security at a level nobody on this team has operated at: it hides a New God signature from a New God. In Session 16 the drones' infrared, blind to the Session 14 thieves, found the brood.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.3.** The drones did not answer and the Green Lantern communicator could not connect. With his own eyes he found the cameras in every room (Vigilance 19) and the nullification projectors around them (Logic 25), and he looked for implants. Counter-surveillance done by hand. Holds.
2. **Oracle** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** He interfaces with STAR Labs systems and monitors media, and since Session 15 the module runs as a live network interface. Tech Reliance is on his sheet as a vulnerability, not a skill.
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.5.** Nothing to interface with in a pocket dimension.
3. **Lock-Step** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic 0. On the *security* half of this axis he is the team's single largest liability (§1.6). **Session 15 showed the *information warfare* half is his**: a broadcast set trending on command, a week-long influence operation, and a channel for every teammate.
   * **Session 17: 3.0 → 3.5 (+0.5).** **An influence operation aimed at the hosts, through their own cameras.** *"If this is a TV show, let's see if this works. They probably got secret cameras"* — and then, to the walls, a speech about how much better television it would make if they never knew whom they were fighting. Within minutes the producers gave both teams a one-minute look at each other. Half a point for working the audience he could not see.
4. **Intrepid** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic 0.
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic −1, and nothing on the sheet or in the log touches this axis.
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.5.** Nothing changed.

*Tie:* Intrepid over Edge at `0.5`, broken on Logic.

### **5.4 Field Investigation, Forensics & Intelligence Gathering**

*On-scene work, canvassing, tailing, source handling, and human intelligence.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `7.8`
   * **All-time.** A laboratory mind doing field work by remote, and doing it well: stealth drones with hyper-spectral and infrared reconnaissance, an electronic-warfare blackout suite, and the tradecraft to phone in a false noise complaint so a patrol car would occupy a front gate. In Session 15, the best remote surveillance of his campaign, with **the evidentiary status of each finding stated accurately every time.** In Session 16, the drone sweep and the deduction about the five.
   * **Session 17: 7.5 → 7.8 (+0.3).** **The best on-scene search of his file, with no drones.** He found the hidden cameras in every room, bathrooms included, on a Vigilance 19. He identified the power-nullification projectors around them on a Logic 25, and worked out that there were far more than needed to shut down a room. He thought to check the team for implants. Then he drew the session's one penalty taking a sample. Still first, by a tenth.
2. **Lock-Step** `7.7`
   * **All-time.** Elite human intelligence and nothing else. He got the nightclub location out of a mob lieutenant over Polish cake, the midnight deal location out of a captive, and the warehouse intelligence out of the Burnout Kings. People tell him things they would tell nobody else on this team. Session 15 held the tradecraft failure of his campaign: a surveillance operation broadcast to the public.
   * **Session 17: 7.2 → 7.7 (+0.5).** **The session's best intelligence came out of a loudspeaker, because he kept asking it questions.** He got who sent them (*"Minji Lin aka Firefly aka the tiny terror aka Death from Nowhere"*), why (*"elected by a representative of your world"*), and the terms of return (*"precisely at the moment and place that you left"*). He got the length of the rest, and — through a hot microphone — that there was a second team. From Earth X357 he got their mentor (*"she goes by Batman... She used to be called Raven"*), their names, and their verdict on their own adults. His hexed link to Blitzkrieg failed in a way that was itself the first clue they had left the dimension. Against: two worst-case guesses about the opposition, both wrong. **A tenth behind first.**
3. **Oracle** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** Sent to look at a museum in Session 13, he produced what a professional would: every entrance, an underground approach, an accurate security posture and an independent sighting of somebody casing the building. Vigilance 6 is the highest on the roster, and precognition is reconnaissance by another name. In Session 16 the forecast was right about the warehouse, and his briefing of the situation drew *"Ok, that told me nothing."*
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.0.** One excellent read and one bad guess. Intuition told him before the bout that he was facing *"somebody who copies powers, and has copied his already."* Earlier he had put the odds that the other team was the Destined at *"100%."* Holds.
4. **Edge** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** **The instrument is excellent and the operator is new.** Heightened Senses 2 lets him sense four times as far as normal, gives him a double edge on Vigilance checks to perceive, and lets him listen to a heartbeat for lies. Logic −1 and no investigative record. He arrived at the warehouse already briefed by Nightwing.
   * **Session 17: 5.0 → 5.5 (+0.5).** **The best epistemic discipline in the room.** He used his senses on waking to check whether anyone was watching (the log does not record the result). He reasoned from evidence about the captors (*"we wake up on beds and we don't seem to be under the same control that those kids were in"*), and he called the room's speculation what it was: *"you're doing a lot of assumptions right now."* The assumptions were wrong. He asked the hosts the question nobody else did: *"What will happen to the loser team?"*
5. **Intrepid** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** He found the Insidious nest by walking around and looking, gave Wonder Woman an accurate description of the stolen artifacts, and in Session 15 tailed a limousine across the city by air without being spotted.
