   * **Session 17: 3.0 → 3.5 (+0.5).** A deduction from a single observation: the penalty freezing Quasar-Force *"means time is a factor in this competition."* He was right before anyone saw the maze.

### **5.5 Lab Investigation, Forensics & Data Analysis**

*Bench work, document analysis, data synthesis, and evidence handling.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic 7 with Brilliance 4, and a results record nobody else approaches. He decoded containment release mechanisms, interpreted the medical report on the bane derivative, analysed the temporal artifact down to its transmission, and built the whole Lex-Corp evidence chain. In Session 14 he worked out that the mother box's *"Oh No"* was his own mother's voice. **That is forensic reasoning applied to the inside of his own skull.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** The Logic 25 study of the nullifiers — how many there were, that they were off, and that pulling one or two would change nothing — was bench reasoning done on a wall. Holds.
2. **Oracle** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** **Eidetic Memory is a serious analytical instrument**: perfect recall of every document, scene and conversation. In Session 15 the module produced the first explanation anybody on this team has had of what Lock-Step actually is — lantern-derived, linked to Oa, *"unconsciously altering time waves."*
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.0.** Nothing new.
3. **Lock-Step** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** Logic 0. He recognises when data matters and can do nothing with it.
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.0.** He did learn that Quasar-Force's father works at STAR Labs, and said it was *"information we should have known."*
4. **Intrepid** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic 0, and no engagement with the analytical side of any case.
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic −1, and no engagement with the analytical side of the case. He destroyed the one piece of physical evidence Session 16 produced, which was the right call in the moment.
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.5.** Nothing changed.

*Tie:* Intrepid over Edge at `0.5`, broken on Logic.

### **5.6 Law & Legal Skill / Ability**

*A live operational front: a cease-and-desist on record, a threatened wrongful-death suit, an open police investigation, Lex-Corp counsel engaged, a juvenile-privacy exposure, a solicitation, and a minor left cuffed in a crime syndicate's warehouse — to which Session 17 adds a statement of foreknowledge (§2.5) and an abduction across a dimensional boundary that no Earth court can reach.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** A diligent amateur, and diligence counts for a lot here. He quotes the Public Conduct and Powers-Use Standard from memory, keeps genuine chain-of-custody discipline, and walked into a police station to give a statement rather than being summoned. Against that: he argued statute with a corporate lawyer while unqualified, and in Session 15 he endorsed the burglary. **The only member of this team who understands evidence voted for the burglary.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.0.** *"Are you talking about Nightwing's rules or the rules of the sport?"* is the right distinction, asked at the right time. The foreknowledge statement is exposure, scored in 2.5, not skill. Holds.
2. **Lock-Step** `4.0`
   * **All-time.** The only member who worked out that this team needs professional lawyers and said so. Session 13 added two protective instincts — that employing minors is unlawful in most states, and that a fourteen-year-old cannot consent to agreements adults float over his head — and Session 14 a pre-acceptance questionnaire. **Knowing where the line is and paying someone to cross it is still, technically, knowing where the line is.**
   * **Session 17: 3.5 → 4.0 (+0.5).** **Terms before performance.** He refused to leave the barracks until somebody stated the rules (*"I just like to have you know the rules and what we're supposed to do said before I engage"*). He questioned the hosts on the conditions of return until he had them in a form he could hold them to (*"at the exact... spot"*). And he put the question to the announcer directly: *"Do we have to fight, or is it just like, you know, we just play sports?"* That is a contract negotiation conducted with a loudspeaker.
3. **Oracle** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** No formal knowledge and correct instincts. Demanding surrender before engaging is the legally correct posture, and he does it every time; in Session 14 he reported a loss immediately, in his own words, to the principal.
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Edge** `1.5`
   * **All-time.** No engagement with the subject. His one legal fact is a public identity that is *"not very well known."*
   * **Session 17: 1.0 → 1.5 (+0.5).** A principle of the rule of law, in his own words: *"We follow the rule when it's easy and when it's hard. Otherwise, it's not a rule, it's a mood."* That is not legal skill, and it is the instinct legal skill is built on.
5. **Intrepid** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** No engagement with the subject whatsoever.
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.0.** *"That's just exploitation."*

### **5.7 Medical & Medical-Diagnostic**

1. **Oracle** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** The team medic in every sense that matters. He triages, treats, rations and makes the hard calls; in Session 9 he deliberately under-healed a teammate to preserve reserves. He heals Quasar-Force to full before every deployment and attempted to revive the man Quasar-Force killed. In Session 16 the triage call of the fight was his: Let's Go moved to the one teammate who could get everyone out.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** **Triage by recovery mechanism.** The three members with healing factors woke on their own, and the two without them did not. He put 60 into each of those two, and kept 5 for himself.
2. **Quasar-Force** `6.5`
   * **All-time.** Not a clinician; a strong diagnostician. He interpreted the medical report on the bane derivative, identified its non-terrestrial component, proposed the screening that caught three infections, and in Session 14 diagnosed his own panic as foreign while it was happening. He remembered that *"we were able to cure the other kids,"* **the precedent that turns five infected teenagers into patients rather than casualties.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.5.** His first question on waking was the right one — *"Are we infected with parasites?"* — and his answer to it later was clever: if the arena took them, *"we may not actually have parasites on us."* Holds.
3. **Lock-Step** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Practical pharmacology first (the sedations), and in Session 14 the first therapeutic act of his career: a hex configured into healing and dosed to *"the minimum amount allowed that will wake him up."*
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.5.** Not used.
4. **Intrepid** `1.5`
   * **All-time.** Aspirin, ice cream, and a working knowledge of what hurts.
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.5.** *"Oracle definitely needs rest."*
5. **Edge** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** A healing factor that worked on him alone, and nothing for anyone else.
   * **Session 17: 1.5 → 1.0 (−0.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ The healing factor is gone. What remains is a sound instinct about recovery — food for the recovering medic first — and nothing clinical. Last on substance.

### **5.8 Stealth & Infiltration Capability**

1. **Lock-Step** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** Teleportation *is* infiltration. He goes through walls, fences, guards and shielding, and he inserts the whole team behind a defended perimeter. Against: a botched blind teleport in Session 1, a loud temperament, and since Session 15 the most recognisable face on the roster after Quasar-Force's.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.0.** He phased his hand through a handshake. In a facility with cameras in every room, nobody infiltrated anything. Holds.
2. **Quasar-Force** `7.8`
   * **All-time.** Personally the least stealthy person on the roster — he glows, hovers and has a public identity — and **the owner of the team's entire infiltration toolkit**: stealth drones with hyper-spectral vision, infrared reconnaissance, an EW blackout suite and fake-noise-complaint tradecraft. Session 15 gave him a New God signal mask.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.8.** The toolkit was out of reach, and he used its counter: the camera hunt. *Closest call* with Lock-Step at two tenths.
3. **Oracle** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Tactical gear and Vigilance 6, no stealth powers, and an Extreme Appearance that means anyone who sees him remembers him. He cased a museum for a full day without being made, which is the whole of the credit.
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.5.** ⟨Advance⟩ Agility 5 helps on the checks; Extreme Appearance does not change. He moves up a place because the newcomer moved down.
4. **Edge** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** His Session 16 role was *Precision Marksman & Spatial Infiltrator*, and the sheet supported the first half. No stealth powers, white hair and glowing eyes, a public identity, and Agility 7 as the whole case. His real contribution to this axis is the opposite capability — making it hard for anyone else to sneak.
   * **Session 17: 4.0 → 3.0 (−1.0).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ Stealth runs on Agility, and he moved all seven points out of it. The roster dropped *Infiltrator* from his role. His attacks are now *"invisible"* force — *"precise unseen cuts at range"* — which hides the attack, not the attacker.
5. **Intrepid** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** Six foot four, 301 pounds, a floor-length red cape and a metallic gold "I" on his chest. His Secret Identity protects his civilian life, not his approach.
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.0.** Nothing changed.

*⟨Note⟩ The counter-infiltration capability is still on paper.* Edge kept Heightened Senses 2 through the rebuild, so enemies still have double trouble sneaking past him. Nothing tested it in Session 17. §10.4 carries the finding as answered on paper.

### **5.9 Sensory & Environmental Awareness**

1. **Oracle** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** Vigilance 6 with a Vigilance defence of 16, Danger Sense, Precognition 1, Intuition, Charmed Life, Real Jinx and Combat Reflexes. **He literally sees things before they happen.** Session 14 showed both halves: the module let him dodge an attacker he could not see, and he did not know an astral figure was standing over a teammate. **Precognition tells you what is about to happen. It does not tell you what is in the room.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.0.** Both halves again. The module felt the abduction first (*"makes him feel weird"*). Precognition answered his question about resting accurately and uselessly (*"You'll get some rest"*), and Intuition found the copier. He also said *"my power is broken."* Holds.
2. **Edge** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** **The best innate senses on the team, at the score Exemplar II left at**: Heightened Senses 2 — four times normal range, compensation for a lost sense, a heartbeat lie-detector, a double edge to perceive, and double trouble for anyone sneaking past — with Situational Awareness and Combat Reflexes beside it. Against it, Vigilance 3. **He tells you what is in the room, which is the half Oracle's module does not.**
   * **Session 17: 8.0 → 7.5 (−0.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ Heightened Senses 2 stayed, and two supports went: Situational Awareness was traded for Battle Ready, and a point of Vigilance moved to Ego (Vigilance 2). He used the senses on waking, and the log does not record what they found. Still second.
3. **Quasar-Force** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** Vigilance 5 and a sensor suite that outperforms most innate senses: hyper-spectral drones that defeated Shroud's darkness, a technopath detected from the drift of his own drones, and tactile awareness of objects at range through telekinesis. He also has one sensory channel nobody else has: the box speaks to him.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.0.** The instruments failed and the eyes did not: a Vigilance 19 found cameras all over the facility. The channel nobody else has spoke more clearly than ever — in words — and what it said was that it could not reach anything. Holds.
4. **Lock-Step** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Vigilance 2, a Vigilance defence of 15 from Uncanny 3, and a genuinely good suspicion instinct: he identified the mentalist among five costumed villains and flagged Iron-Side's convenient absence during the Destined incursion.
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.5.** *"They probably got secret cameras"* was right. Holds.
5. **Intrepid** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** Vigilance 3, a defence of 14, and no sensory powers.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.5.** Nothing changed.

### **5.10 Social Engineering, Espionage, Grifting & Interrogation**

1. **Lock-Step** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Ego 7 and a record to match. He got the nightclub location out of a mob lieutenant over Polish cake, talked the Burnout Kings into standing aside, negotiated the Destined out of the lobby, and has produced repeated bloodless captures. **He is the only member of this team who is good with people.** Session 15 was the best social performance of the campaign, across an alien telepath, a cosmic officer, a public audience and a villain team; in Session 16 the cuffs almost worked on a mind-controlled telepath.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** He interrogated a loudspeaker, played the producers through their own cameras, and charmed both teams in the arena. He also kept a power copier from touching him and told her his powers were *"horrible."* At the ceiling this scale allows him.
2. **Edge** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** Ego 4 on the Session 16 sheet, the second-highest social ability score on the team then, and a Monster trait that gives him an edge to intimidate. His one intimidation failed against opponents whose will was not their own. With his new teammates he was tactful under a stranger's rant and direct when it mattered.
   * **Session 17: 5.0 → 6.0 (+1.0).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ **Ego 7: level with Lock-Step for the highest social ability score on the team**, and the Monster trait's edge on top of it. The record has not caught up with the ability. In Session 17 he argued a room for a day and moved it on one rule; he laughed at an opponent's name and apologised. He did no interrogating at all. **He passes Quasar-Force**, on the ability.
3. **Quasar-Force** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** Not manipulation; arguably its opposite, and it works anyway in a narrow band. Compassion, reciprocal information-sharing and transparent sincerity get wronged people talking. Against hostile professionals he gets red-herringed. Ego −2; he cannot deceive and does not try.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.5.** The sincerity worked — *"Well, we can be friends!"* got his opponent's real name — and the reciprocity cost him (1.6). Holds.
4. **Oracle** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Ego 0 and an Ego defence of 10. He speaks plainly, demands surrender and coordinates clearly, and twice he did the most effective social act in the room by naming the thing everyone was talking around. **Honest rather than persuasive.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.5.** *"You haven't been with us long enough"* is the kind of line that closes a conversation rather than winning one. Holds.
5. **Intrepid** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** Ego 0 and no social traits. The most socially deft line in his file is *"Sometimes."*
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.0.** *"I don't get it."*

### **5.11 Domestic Utility & Skills**

1. **Oracle** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** He is the household. He cooks buffet-style, adjusts the menu on request, times breakfast around who wakes when, and personally makes sure the fourteen-year-old actually eats. When the household moved to a Justice League guest suite with a robot doing the catering, he was still the one telling people to rest.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.0.** He spent the session being looked after — which the household he built did without being asked. Holds.
2. **Quasar-Force** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** Enormous utility and near-zero chores. Telekinesis is the ultimate domestic force multiplier, and his suit self-cleans, self-repairs and manages waste. He will not cook.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.0.** ⟨Partially OOC⟩ The roster now records that the suit retracts into a belt and can come off, and that he lets everyone believe it can't. Nothing domestic changed.
3. **Edge** `4.0`
   * **All-time.** In Session 16 he moved into bedroom 3, Exemplar II's old room, and nothing about his life in the tower was on record. A student with grades that were *"already bad."*
   * **Session 17: 2.5 → 4.0 (+1.5).** **The first teammate to feed the team who is not Oracle.** He found the kitchen, worked out the food replicator, and ordered burgers for everyone, *"especially Oracle since he's recovering."* It is replicator cooking, which is why it is a point and a half and not more. The instinct — notice who is down, and feed them — is the one this axis was written around. **He passes Lock-Step.**
4. **Lock-Step** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** Cocoa Puffs, Instagram Day, popcorn with red licorice for a stakeout, and a standing promise of black licorice for Quasar-Force. Genuine morale value and zero reliability. His Session 14 contribution was establishing that the Justice League's space station has Wi-Fi.
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.0.** He slept in his gladiator uniform.
5. **Intrepid** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** He makes his own oatmeal, breaks things, and offers to squeeze household objects until they fail. He knows where the library is.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.0.** First into the uniform.

### **5.12 Teaching & Mentorship**

1. **Oracle** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** He teaches by structure and by example. He runs the routine everyone else lives inside, he showed that a person on this team can say no to Lock-Step and survive it, and he is the person a younger teammate would actually approach. Session 15 made him the designated tutor for Lock-Step's unlockable powers.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.5.** ⟨Flag closed⟩ Edge's mentor tag is Barbara Gordon, not him, so there is no second tutoring role. What he taught the newcomer this session was the team's frame: *"You haven't been with us long enough,"* and *"So now you know the kind of stuff we got to put up with."* Accurate, and not the lesson the newcomer was asking for. Holds.
2. **Quasar-Force** `7.1`
   * **All-time.** He explains constantly and enthusiastically to anyone who will listen, shares intelligence unprompted, and models genuine curiosity. In Session 14 he corrected a Justice League scientist's professional manner and she changed how she spoke to the room. In Session 16 he gave the newcomer the one-line briefing Oracle could not. **He is undercut by being the youngest person present and reading younger still.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.1.** He gave the newcomer two more correct one-liners: what the ring is (*"he's a Grey Lantern heir now. Not officially yet, but he got a ring"*) and what happened to the infected (*"The ones with the eggs, we were able to remove them"*). Holds.
3. **Edge** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Black Canary's student, with Quick Learner on his sheet. In Session 16 he had nothing to teach yet and was the one doing the learning.
   * **Session 17: 2.5 → 3.5 (+1.0).** **He taught the team a rule, and the de facto leader took it.** *"We follow the rule when it's easy and when it's hard. Otherwise, it's not a rule, it's a mood"* — and Lock-Step: *"I'm hearing it... this is light work, we're following the rules."* He also tried to teach a formation, and nobody would learn it.
4. **Lock-Step** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** He established the surrender ritual as de facto team doctrine, which is teaching whether or not he meant it. Session 15 added a principle Quasar-Force took and an unprompted *"consent is everything."* In Session 16 the first lesson he gave the newcomer was that the adults are trash, alongside the most useful one: *"we can bring them back, so knock them out."*
   * **Session 17: 4.0 → 3.5 (−0.5).** **The second lesson he gave the newcomer was the errand's doctrine**: *"deaths we're not worried about right now,"* and *"we don't have that moral high ground."* The third was that the team does not rehearse — *"our maneuvers is like dancing"* — and the fourth, on Nightwing: *"you'll find out... this is you finding out... So, welcome."*

   *Tie:* Edge over Lock-Step at `3.5`, broken on uptake: Edge's lesson was taken, and Lock-Step's was argued with.
5. **Intrepid** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** Nothing to teach, and no inclination to try.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.0.** Nothing changed.

### **5.13 Empathic / Emotional Support**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.2`
   * **All-time.** **Compassion is his defining trait, and it works on people who have no reason to respond to it.** Shadow-Boxer opened up to him, Cassidy Yanks gave her real name because he was kind, and World Ender's facade collapsed under sincerity. In Session 14 he aimed it at an alien machine intelligence with PTSD, and it landed. In Session 16 he reached the teammate who never lets anyone in.
   * **Session 17: 9.4 → 9.2 (−0.2).** **The recipient he has never failed, he failed.** The box he calls Stella reached him after hours of silence. She said she could not reach the Source, *"I am still with you. Don't be afraid,"* and then *"Help."* He failed the Ego roll to comfort her, and she went back into withdrawal. Everywhere else it worked as it always does: *"Well, we can be friends!"* to the boy he was about to throw out of the arena. Still first.
2. **Oracle** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** The team's caretaker in the full sense. He cooks around individual appetites, heals people to full before deployment, notices who is not eating, and holds the space where injured people end up talking. His protectiveness toward Quasar-Force is the clearest emotional attachment anyone on this team has expressed.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.0.** He emptied himself on the two who could not get up, and hung back at the door for the youngest. Holds.
3. **Edge** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** A tactful first afternoon in Session 16. Met with a stranger's grievance about adults, he answered with care — *"I don't want to break your hope,"* *"I'm sorry to hear that"* — rather than agreement or argument.
   * **Session 17: 5.0 → 5.5 (+0.5).** He noticed the room was *"pretty down"* and fed it. He gave the frozen youngest the kindest line at the door (*"Oh, don't worry, Quasar-Force, Enter when you can"*) and gave the room hope when it had none (*"There's always a way out"*). Against: the punch, and laughing at an opponent's name.
4. **Lock-Step** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** Socially warm in a real way — the licorice, the popcorn, the video games — and attached to a willingness to override other people's autonomy that undoes most of it. Session 15 held two of the warmest acts on his file (asking consent before Quasar-Force was studied, and going back to check on the refugee). In Session 16 his welcome to the newcomer was also a lecture.
   * **Session 17: 4.5 → 5.0 (+0.5).** **The warmest decision of his file:** he sat with the frozen fourteen-year-old rather than leave him behind. *"It's okay, buddy. I want to stay too... I just don't feel comfortable leaving our youngest."* Against: telling the same boy that Guy Gardner had *"left you on red,"* and adding the apology for the three dollars in the same breath.
5. **Intrepid** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** Cheerful, agreeable, and emotionally unavailable in both directions. He is going through something and has told nobody.
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.0.** *"Yeah. Oracle definitely needs rest."*

### **5.14 Team Coordination / Interoperability**

*Working with powers, styles, and skillsets that are not your own.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** He has found a use for every teammate's kit: nudging Intrepid's strikes, placing Oracle on request, funnelling enemies toward the brawlers, handing negotiations to Lock-Step, and feeding the whole team drone reconnaissance before entry. **He adapts to other people's abilities more readily than to his own limitations.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** *"You teleport, Oracle predicts, and I figure."*
2. **Oracle** `8.8`
   * **All-time.** He interoperates with everyone by construction: he heals whoever is hurt, buffs whoever is failing, and broadcasts warnings to the whole team. Session 15's resonance gave him a sanctioned interface with Lock-Step's powers.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.8.** An assist, and the warning that kept the team from shooting the wrong Oracle. Holds.
3. **Edge** `7.8`
   * **All-time.** **Fitted in from the first round of Session 16**: his target call was built around the team's kit, his first shot was aided by all four teammates, he turned two of Intrepid's attacks into better ones, and his rear guard bought Lock-Step the turns he needed.
   * **Session 17: 7.3 → 7.8 (+0.5).** **The member who asked for this axis by name.** *"I need to form part of that and I want to see how do I need to move so we work together."* He did not get the practice. His rebuilt kit fits the team better than the old one did (4.20), and he deferred to the one-on-one format the room agreed. **He passes Lock-Step.**
4. **Lock-Step** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** He physically integrates the team better than anyone, and has socially degraded the advantage by moving people without consent. In Session 16 the stated deduction came off: he asked before moving a teammate.
   * **Session 17: 7.8 → 7.5 (−0.3).** **The team's coordination runs on improvisation, and he defended it as policy.** *"We don't really practice it just kind of you know just,"* and *"our maneuvers is like dancing... you just fill it in the moment."* It has worked — *"I think they're very successful so far"* — and it does not let a new member in. He ended the argument by asking the newcomer to explain everything he does. The maze ran on his teleports.
5. **Intrepid** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** He accepts positioning guidance from Oracle and Quasar-Force without ego, which is rarer and more valuable than it sounds in a character with this much raw power.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.0.** He took the assist and the nudge.

### **5.15 Cross-Team / Institutional Alliance Capital**

*Ability to obtain resources from, and coordinate with, outside organisations.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.7`
   * **All-time.** **Built entirely from nothing.** It covers:
     * Blüdhaven PD, and STAR Labs access through his father and in his own right.
     * Two loans of suppression cuffs.
     * Direct contact with Wonder Woman, and an open channel to a serving Green Lantern.
     * A personal sanctuary offer from a founding member of the League, a week aboard the Watchtower, and Doctor Taleria's collaboration.

     **He is the team's institutional switchboard.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.7.** Every line was dead — *"The dimension you're trying to reach is currently unavailable"* — and none was lost. ⟨Flag⟩ The roster still lists *"Exemplar's Dad's Lab"* among his lab-access tags; with Exemplar II gone, it is stale.
2. **Lock-Step** `7.2`
   * **All-time.** Extensive alliance capital, much of it in the wrong directions. The Marrow Syndicate will sit down with him, the Destined negotiate through him, the Burnout Kings stood aside for him, and three of the most significant magical figures on Earth hold positions on his future. Session 15 added the Grey Lanterns, the people of Kwa, the Destined (who now hold his words) and an audience he can move. Against: the Justice League, whose station he livestreamed.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.2.** The capital he built in the arena was across a universe: Earth X357's Titans liked him, laughed with him, and answered his questions. It does not travel home.
3. **Oracle** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** STAR Labs built his body and Nightwing takes his calls; Nightwing arranged him a private appointment with a STAR Labs board member, and in Session 14 he held the direct line to Wonder Woman in a crisis. The capital is real, narrow and largely inherited.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.0.** ⟨Retcon⟩ The Wonder Woman debrief the last edition recorded did not happen. Nothing was used, and nothing lost.
4. **Edge** `6.5`
   * **All-time.** **The newcomer arrived with a network**: Black Canary volunteered to train him, which brings the Birds of Prey, and Nightwing, operating as Batman, recruited and briefed him personally. A direct line into the Bat-family and a League-adjacent hero, held by someone who had been on the team for an afternoon.
   * **Session 17: 6.0 → 6.5 (+0.5).** ⟨Flag closed⟩ The Oracle who found him was Barbara Gordon, and the roster now carries her as a mentor. **Two principals of the Birds of Prey hold a stake in him.**
5. **Intrepid** `1.5`
   * **All-time.** Nightwing, and that is the complete list. A week inside the Justice League's headquarters produced nothing, and since Session 15 he has a social channel with a thousand followers he did not ask for.
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.5.** Nothing changed.

### **5.16 Overall Non-Combat Value / Contribution**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.3`
   * **All-time.** Most of what this team accomplishes outside a fight runs through him: engineering, laboratory forensics, the drone network, the evidence chain, the police liaison, data custody across three sites, and a telekinetic domestic multiplier. **Remove him and the Titans stop producing intelligence**, and since Session 15 they also stop being hidden from the thing hunting one of them.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.3.** With every tool gone, he still found the cameras, identified the nullifiers and solved the maze's keypad in seconds. Holds.
2. **Oracle** `8.2`
   * **All-time.** Value counts what actually gets delivered, and Oracle delivers every day: medicine, the household, awareness, perfect recall, logistics, the Nightwing channel, the STAR Labs relationship, and in Session 15 the most specific forecast of his campaign.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.2.** The two heals that put the team on its feet. Holds.
3. **Lock-Step** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** The social layer is load-bearing and invisible on a capability chart: the Marrow Syndicate détente, the Burnout Kings standing down, the negotiated surrenders, and the stipend he extracted for the whole team. **Without him this team has no diplomacy at all**, and on Kwa a species exists because of a conversation he insisted on having.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.0.** The session's intelligence came out of his questions. Holds.
4. **Edge** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** In Session 16 he made the team plan and gave it its target. Outside a fight his value was Heightened Senses 2 on paper and a network into the Bat-family and the Birds; Logic −1 capped the rest.
   * **Session 17: 4.0 → 5.0 (+1.0).** **A day off the battlefield, and he contributed more of it than anyone but the medic.** He fed the team, pushed for the formation, held the room's epistemics (*"assumptions"*), and gave the team its arena rule. He also raised the one moral question it had not asked itself.
5. **Intrepid** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** He patrols and finds things by walking around, which is not nothing. Session 14 added accurate witness testimony, and Session 15 a tail and the library.
   * **Session 17: 1.5 → 2.0 (+0.5).** The rest call and the time deduction.

### **5.17 Overall Non-Combat Power / Potency**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.3`
   * **All-time.** First place has been his on the strength of breadth at the top end. He leads engineering, lab forensics, cyber, coordination and alliance capital, with high placings almost everywhere else, and is weak only in social, where one sincere mode works, and in law, where he is a diligent amateur.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.3.** Eleven firsts across seventeen, as last revision: Leadership, Engineering, Cyber, Field Investigation, Lab Forensics, Law, Empathic Support, Coordination, Alliance Capital, and both aggregates.
2. **Oracle** `7.4`
   * **All-time.** Medical 9.5, Sensory Awareness 9.0, Domestic Utility 9.0 and Empathic Support 9.0, with Leadership above 8 — several non-combat axes at 9.0 or above, which nobody but Quasar-Force matches.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.4.** First on Medical, Sensory, Domestic and Teaching. **The breadth that once won him this section still wins him second.**
3. **Lock-Step** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** Elite in one dimension and near-absent in every technical one, and he has risen because the non-technical dimensions keep turning out to matter: human intelligence, diplomacy, the only mission-vetting proposal anyone has made, and information operations.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.5.** First on Stealth and Social Engineering, and a tenth off first on Field Investigation.
4. **Edge** `4.2`
   * **All-time.** In Session 16, no firsts: second on Sensory, third on three more, fourth on six, and last on the five Logic-driven axes where his Logic of −1 broke a tie with Intrepid. **A combat specialist's non-combat profile, with one first-rank instrument in it.**
   * **Session 17: 3.8 → 4.2 (+0.4).** Second on Sensory and now on Social Engineering, third on four more, and up on ten of the sixteen component axes (down on three, all of them the rebuild). Still a combat specialist's profile, with a second instrument in it — Ego 7 — and a day of organising on file.
5. **Intrepid** `1.8`
   * **All-time.** He patrols and finds things by walking around. At Session 15 he placed last on all seventeen non-combat axes; at Session 16, on twelve.
   * **Session 17: 1.6 → 1.8 (+0.2).** Last on thirteen of the seventeen; three of the other four are the Logic tie-breaks, and on the fourth (Medical) the newcomer fell below him. Up on three component axes, in the first session in which his calls carried.

*⟨Note⟩ The vacancy, still partly filled.* Edge is fourth on both aggregates, behind Lock-Step. **The section's third place is still Lock-Step's, and the gap Exemplar II left in the technical middle is still open**; the rebuild added a social instrument, not a technical one.

## **6\. Capability Robustness & Conditionality**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

*How well capability survives contact with adverse conditions: preparation denied, powers suppressed, countermeasures applied, support removed. Session 17 put the team in the most adverse condition it has faced, and two quieter tests sat inside it. Every outside line was cut. The facility was wired with power nullifiers, all switched off. A copy of one member turned his own kit against him. And the mother box at the centre of another member's powers reported that she cannot reach the thing that powers her.*

### **6.1 Ability to Leverage Prep-Time**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.8`
   * **All-time.** Inventor, Scientific Expertise, Logic 7 and Brilliance 4, plus access to three laboratories including an air-gapped underground physics lab. **Prep-time is the condition under which he is most dangerous**: in Session 15 he named the order of work three times and delivered three builds in six days. In Session 16 a prep window of minutes still produced a drone sweep and a deduction.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.8.** Twenty-four hours of prep, and no parts, no tools and no lab. He spent them finding the cameras and the nullifiers, and on the box. Holds — the axis scores the ability, and this was the condition that starves it.
2. **Oracle** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** **Precognition 1 is prep-time as a superpower.** Twenty-four hours of lookahead is a planning window nobody else has, and Eidetic Memory means nothing he prepares is ever forgotten. Session 13 turned a prep window into a complete entrance map; Session 15 into an actor, a probability, a collision window and a destination.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.0.** He asked the module what would happen if they rested, and got the answer the module gives when it has nothing useful: *"You'll get some rest."* He then used Intuition before the bout, which found the copier. Holds.
3. **Lock-Step** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Powerful Hex can be configured in advance, which is real preparation, and temperamentally he improvises and skips the briefing. In Session 14 he argued *for* preparation for the first time, and in Session 15 he did it on Kwa: a scout, an exit, a healer at his elbow and an opening question. In Session 16 he left the planning to Oracle.
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.5.** Both halves. He used the prep window to extract intelligence from the hosts (5.4), and refused to use it to rehearse: *"We can practice when we get back... we need a little bit more time than like a day."* Holds.
4. **Edge** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** Logic −1, and nothing on the sheet that converts time into advantage except Heightened Senses 2 given time to listen. **He was the member who asked for the plan** in Session 16, and he came to the door already briefed by Nightwing.
   * **Session 17: 2.5 → 3.0 (+0.5).** **The member who asked to use the time.** *"We have 24 hours. We can train for at least three of those 24 hours. We can come up with something. It's not so difficult as you think."* Nobody took him up on it.
5. **Intrepid** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** Nothing on his sheet converts time into advantage; he is exactly as capable after a week of planning as after ten seconds.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.0.** He used the time the way his sheet suggests: sleep.

### **6.2 Ability to Function Without Powers**

*⟨Note⟩ For the first time this axis was not hypothetical. The bunk room and, by Four-Fold's reading, the arena are wired with banks of power-nullification projectors, "WAY more modules installed than what is required to shut down someone's powers." They were off. Four-Fold read them as the hosts' answer to anyone who turned against them, and was frozen for saying so.*

1. **Lock-Step** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** Ego 7 and the best social capability on the team are not powers. Depowered, he still talks his way into the nightclub, still gets the location out of the lieutenant, and still runs the room. **Session 15 was the proof at the largest scale available**: the encounter that decided the fate of a species was a conversation.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.5.** The session's best intelligence came out of him asking a loudspeaker questions. None of it needed a power. Holds.
2. **Edge** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** On the Session 16 sheet, Agility 7 and Black Canary's *"all-out"* training made him a fast fifteen-year-old with real martial training, Ego 4 and Logic −1 underneath the powers.
   * **Session 17: 4.5 → 5.5 (+1.0).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ Melee 6 and Ego 7 are abilities, not powers, and both survive a nullifier. **Depowered, he is a trained fighter with the team's joint-highest social score.** He demonstrated the first half on a teammate: an ordinary punch, for 15. *"Damn, that hurt."*
3. **Quasar-Force** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic 7 survives suppression, and an intact Quasar-Force mind is still the best analyst in Blüdhaven. What does not survive is the body: an 84-pound fourteen-year-old with Melee −2. ⟨OOC⟩ **For him, "without powers" means without the mother box**, and Taleria could not point to where he ends and she begins.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.5.** Without powers he found the cameras with his own eyes, and he never wanted to test the rest: he pulled a nullifier out of a wall and switched it on, and nothing happened. ⟨Volatile⟩ What a nullifier field would do to a boy whose cells run on the box is unknown, and it is the worst-case version of this axis.

   *Tie:* Edge over Quasar-Force at `5.5`, broken on what survives the field: Edge's abilities are his own, and Quasar-Force's mind is partly the box's.
4. **Oracle** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** The hardest case on the axis. Vigilance 6 and Eidetic Memory persist as faculties, but **his powers are not equipment he carries; they are the body he lives in**, and the cybernetics keep him upright. "Without powers" for Oracle may not describe a survivable state.
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.5.** ⟨Advance⟩ Agility 5 is an ability and would survive. The rest of the entry stands.
5. **Intrepid** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** His mutant physiology *is* the character. Remove it and there is a sixteen-year-old with Logic 0, Agility 0, Vigilance 3 and no skills on the sheet.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.0.** Nothing changed.

### **6.3 Power Independence / Lack of Single Point of Failure**

1. **Intrepid** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** Innate mutant physiology with no device, no artifact, no patron and no external dependency of any kind. For fifteen sessions **there was nothing to confiscate, disable or negotiate for.** Session 16 found something: a power thief took Sturdy 3 off him and used it herself for two rounds.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.5.** Nothing taken. Holds.
2. **Edge** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** In Session 16, **the second-most independent kit on the roster**: a Monster origin, so his powers are what he is, and a Constructible weapon, so taking it bought an enemy very little. What kept him behind Intrepid was that his attack powers were weapon powers.
   * **Session 17: 8.0 → 8.5 (+0.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ **The weapon is gone; every power he has is now innate.** Elemental Blast, Burst and Supernova are his, with nothing to confiscate. What remains is the dependency he named himself — concentration, *"because if I don't, things could get bad"* — and an origin nobody, including him, fully understands.

   *Tie:* Intrepid over Edge at `8.5`, broken on record: Intrepid's independence has been tested and recovered; Edge's rests on a mind he says he has to work to keep steady.
3. **Oracle** `4.0`
   * **All-time.** Hardware rather than biology, which is a dependency, distributed across an integrated system rather than concentrated in one part. STAR Labs is a maintenance dependency, not an on-switch. Since Session 15 the module has a sensitivity to time phenomena, and one of his teammates has turned out to be one.
   * **Session 17: 4.5 → 4.0 (−0.5).** **The hardware's sensitivity is confirmed, and it is not only to time.** At the staging point the module *"makes him feel weird"* and put him down before the abduction reached anyone else, and in the pocket dimension he said *"my power is broken."* That is a second displacement reaction in three sessions, from a system he did not build and cannot audit.
4. **Lock-Step** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** Stacked dependencies. **The Vibranium chain whip supplies half his damage multiplier**, Protection Hex is a single power carrying his entire defensive profile, and **everything that makes him versatile and everything that makes him fragile is the same power.** Since Session 15, an outside order has a stake in what powers him.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.5.** The teleport home failed on range, not on a dependency. Holds.
5. **Quasar-Force** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** One object. It cannot be forcibly removed without killing him, and it cannot be replaced. Every power he has flows through a single Apokoliptian-modified mother box with its own damaged personality and survival drive. Both of his Iconic Items carry the same two flaws, *Worn* and *Requires Origin X-Element Mother Box*. In Session 16 the single point held against the one attack built to exploit it.
   * **Session 17: 1.0 → 0.5 (−0.5).** **The single point of failure turned out to have one of its own.** In an isolated dimension the box spelled out *"Cannot reach the Source... No connection. No response,"* panicked mid-sentence, asked for help, and withdrew. His powers still worked in the bout — a grab and a toss — and ⟨Volatile⟩ nobody knows how long the box can run him without the Source.

### **6.4 Resilience Against Countermeasures & Neutralization**

1. **Lock-Step** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** Protection Hex routes every incoming attack and effect through an Ego defence of 20, so there is no soft face, and Powerful Hex means a counter that works one round may not apply the next. Since Session 15 the Force infusion means phasing no longer makes a target immune to him. In Session 16 one perfect roll went through the Hex anyway, which is the dice, not a countermeasure.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.0.** He walked into a facility built to neutralise powers — cameras everywhere, nullifiers in every wall, freezing penalties — and his response was to work out the rules before anyone could use them on him. Holds.
2. **Quasar-Force** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** Viral catalogued his mind as unusually resilient, and Psyche's method worked twice. Session 15 narrowed the telepathic route with the circlet and answered Azimuth's search with the mask, and in Session 16 the mental countermeasure failed against him while the physical one — a strike through his damage reduction, and a bomb that ignores it — worked twice. ⟨Mechanical flag⟩ The circlet is *Worn*.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.0.** A new physical countermeasure worked on him for the first time: an elemental grab, Briar Patch's branches. The circlet stayed on. Holds.
3. **Intrepid** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** Resilient by sheer margin: the largest Health pool on the team, the highest physical defences, H-DR 3, Iron Will, Fearless, Enduring Constitution and a healing factor. **There is no clever answer to Intrepid, only a sufficient one** — and the sufficient answers have never been damage. Session 16 made it four sessions running: a power thief.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.0.** A Marvel ram before its edge, so Karma could not answer it — a sufficient answer that was, for once, damage. He got up. Holds.

   *Tie:* Quasar-Force over Intrepid at `6.0`, carried from Session 16: the same theft that took Intrepid's power failed against Quasar-Force's mind.
4. **Edge** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** In Session 16, **the answer to the counter that beat the team in Session 14**: Heightened Senses 2, plus Slow-Motion Dodge, damage reduction on both tracks and innate powers. Mental defences of 15 to 16 against it. Second on paper.
   * **Session 17: 6.2 → 5.5 (−0.7).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ The senses stayed, and so did the paper answer to obscurement. The dodge went, and his Vigilance defence dropped to 13; the first countermeasure used on him on the new sheet went through that low door, a reaction Suppressive Fire that stunned him for the round. Fourth.
5. **Oracle** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** The most completely countered member of the team. In Session 14 Ecliptica beat him with an elemental grab he owns no power to escape and an area obscurement that removed his ranged specialism, without touching his body or his soft Ego. In Session 16 the brood found a third counter: a reaction that turned his Return Fire onto a teammate.
   * **Session 17: 2.5 → 2.0 (−0.5).** **A fourth counter, and the most complete: his own kit, worn by someone else.** Facade had copied his powers before the bout. She stunned a teammate with his Suppressive Fire, and when he swung at her, she parried him with his own Point-Blank Parry into 34 damage and a stun. ⟨Advance⟩ The two advances are declared, and neither answers the grab or the soft door. **Three sessions of ⟨Flag⟩s on this entry resolve to no change, and a new way to beat him was found.**

### **6.5 Power Failure Mode Lack-of-Severity**

*What happens when it goes wrong. High = benign failure.*

1. **Oracle** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** A missed shot hits a wall, a failed heal costs Focus, and **his powers cannot kill anyone even when they work.** The structural concern dates from Session 12: the module accepts information from outside itself and renders it as its own output, and **a module whose failures look like results is benign only as long as someone remembers which roll produced them.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.0.** His failures this session were a bad guess, a lost exchange and a module that put him to sleep at a staging point. None hurt anybody but him. Holds.
2. **Intrepid** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Ground-Shaking Stomp is indiscriminate by design, and he has come within one roll of killing ordinary humans three separate times. The failure mode is manslaughter, mitigated only by the fact that everyone, including him, can see it coming.
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.5.** Not triggered.
3. **Edge** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** On the Session 16 sheet a marksman's failures were a miss and, on a Fantastic, bleeding. **The real question was the power behind the weapon**: it slices through objects by concentration, it arrived with headaches and glowing eyes, and Black Canary volunteered to train him because it is dangerous.
   * **Session 17: 3.0 → 2.5 (−0.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ **The power behind the weapon is now the weapon**, and he described its failure mode himself: *"I practice my concentration a lot because if I don't, things could get bad."* The roster's summary is *"sweeping storms of blades that carve through everything around him at once."* A failure of the area power is not a missed shot. Nobody has seen it fail.
4. **Quasar-Force** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** His powers fail loudly. Crush produced more than twice a target's maximum Health and killed a man, and commandeered by a telepath his telekinesis brought down the main containment field on a device that could compromise the stability of the universe. **His ceiling means a bad failure is not a casualty but an event.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.0.** ⟨Volatile⟩ The precursor to the worst version of this axis appeared — a box frightened, isolated and saying *"No! No! No—"* — and nothing failed. Holds.
5. **Lock-Step** `1.8`
   * **All-time.** Berserk is a failure mode with no upper bound and no off switch, and it has fired twice. Since Session 15 the diagnosis is that his powers work by *"unconsciously altering time waves to make things into reality,"* and ⟨OOC⟩ time powers are on the GM's banned list for a reason.
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.8.** No Berserk, and a sixth clean engagement. Holds.

### **6.6 Solo Operating Capability**

1. **Lock-Step** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** Self-inserting, self-extracting, nearly unhittable behind Protection Hex, and socially capable of talking his way through anything he cannot teleport past. He already operates alone by preference. In Session 16 the solo operator was alone when the enemy found him, and one roll took him from full to −24.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.5.** He chose to stay behind alone with the youngest, and he was right that it could have been a trap. Holds.
2. **Intrepid** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** A huge Health pool and fast regeneration make him extremely hard to kill alone. Logic 0, Vigilance 3 and no investigative capability mean he would never find anyone to fight.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.0.** He went through the door ahead of the team and walked the stepping puzzle alone under the orbs, until Lock-Step's step finished it. Holds.
3. **Edge** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** In Session 16, **the best solo profile on arrival after Lock-Step's**: a healing factor, innate powers, a dodge, and Heightened Senses 2 to find the fight and know who was lying in it. He did the one solo job of the fight, holding off Blitzkrieg alone.
   * **Session 17: 6.5 → 6.0 (−0.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ The two features that kept him alive alone are gone — the healing factor and the dodge. The senses and the area power that would let him finish a fight alone are new. **He can find the fight and end it, and nobody heals him afterwards.**

   *Tie:* Intrepid over Edge at `6.0`, broken on survival: alone, the member who cannot be killed outlasts the member who can find the fight.
4. **Oracle** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** Self-healing, precognitive and perceptive, with no mobility power at all and 90 Health. Sent out alone he comes back with a complete reconnaissance product; what he cannot do alone is get anywhere or escape anything.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.0.** Nothing changed.
5. **Quasar-Force** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Brilliant, mobile and made of paper: 60 Health, no healing of any kind, and sessions ending at 1, 4, −11, −12 and −30. **He is the most capable person on this team and the least able to operate without it.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.5.** Frozen at the door, he was not alone because someone chose not to leave him. Holds.

## **7\. Overall Power / Ability / Value**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

### **7.1 Versatility & Adaptability**

1. **Lock-Step** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Powerful Hex means his power list is *any power he has the rank for, prerequisites ignored*; in play it has produced Quick Phase, Teleport Blind, Teleport Together, Disrupt Person, Real Jinx, a sleep effect and healing hands. **Nobody else on this team can change what they are mid-combat.** Session 15 added four roles that use no power at all, and a fifth nobody understands yet: a Grey Lantern in training.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** In one day he was the interrogator, the producer, the diplomat, the transport and the striker. At the ceiling this scale allows him.
2. **Quasar-Force** `8.3`
   * **All-time.** One power set applied to everything — attack, defence, restraint, barriers, flight and heavy lifting — and since Session 15 roles far from telekinesis: fabricator of New God–grade hardware, research collaborator on his own biology, surveillance operator, and the team's switchboard to the League and the Corps. **His versatility comes from imagination, not options.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.3.** Counter-surveillance by eye, puzzle-solver, maze navigator and controller, with every tool he usually uses out of reach. Holds.
3. **Oracle** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** Five distinct battlefield jobs — ranged attacker, healer, reviver, morale restorer, scout and overwatch — plus field reconnaissance, a live network interface and a tutoring role. Session 14 was the counter-case: pinned and blinded, most of the jobs became unavailable at once.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.0.** Healer and forecaster; the other jobs were not asked for. Holds.
4. **Edge** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** A specialist with more range than the word suggests, on the Session 16 sheet: two damage tracks, a called shot that disarmed a phaser, a rear guard, and a sensory role nobody else on the team can fill.
   * **Session 17: 5.0 → 5.5 (+0.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ Fewer tracks and more shapes. He lost Focus damage, and gained an area attack, a free attack and a second action every round. Off the field he showed a second job: the room's quartermaster and its conscience.
5. **Intrepid** `2.3`
   * **All-time.** One job, done superbly. Session 15 gave him a second, briefly: he tailed a vehicle across the city by air.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.3.** The one job, plus walking a puzzle floor while orbs fired at him. Holds.

### **7.2 Power Mastery / Control**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Explicitly noted as very gifted at gaining precision and control, and the play backs it: he disarms individually, cuffs a sleeping man without waking him, guides a teammate's fist into a target, and in Session 16 took a telekinetic through her own force field and crushed Stote to a knockout rather than a body. **The fly-by-wire framing means his ceiling is bounded by what he thinks to try.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** Grab and toss in one action, exactly where he meant it. ⟨Volatile⟩ The experiment Taleria prescribed — telekinetic air-writing for the box to steer — finally ran, and it produced words for the first time, after hours, through a buzzing he could not concentrate past. The words were a plea. Holds.
2. **Oracle** `7.8`
   * **All-time.** He uses his module correctly every time, holds actions, and reassesses targets on precognitive input. **Knowing exactly what your instrument did and did not tell you is mastery**, and in Session 15 he reported a failed forecast honestly. In Session 16 Charmed Life turned an opening Marvel into a super-Marvel on the right target.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.8.** He asked Intuition the right question before the bout and got the right answer. Then he walked into his own power on someone else. Holds.
3. **Edge** `6.5`
   * **All-time.** **Mastery of the weapon was on record in Session 16; mastery of the power was not.** He made a called shot at a hand-held device, with trouble for the disarm, and hit; and he chose Suppressive Fire three times when Stopping Power would have done more Health damage, a throttle used on purpose.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.5.** ⟨Re-spec⟩ Now the power is the weapon, and the sheet says how he holds it: Discipline 4, the maximum, and a Signature Attack on the Blast. What he said in play is that it takes work — *"I practice my concentration a lot"* — and in his first use of it on the record he hit twice and knocked his opponent out. The throttle he used to show by choosing Focus damage he now shows by choosing how much Focus to spend, and he spent heavily. Holds.
4. **Intrepid** `4.0`
   * **All-time.** Improving visibly — he takes positioning guidance before stomping — and his own stated fear is that he cannot control it. In Session 16 the stomp went where Oracle's module pointed it and hit only an enemy.
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.0.** Nothing tested it.
5. **Lock-Step** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Chaos magic he has not mastered: two Berserk episodes he could not control, a botched blind teleport, and a hex system whose power comes from its unpredictability. In Session 14 he dosed an effect deliberately for the first time, and in Session 15 he found out he does not know what his powers are. *"So I'm like a grey lantern, but like in training."*
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.5.** A precise Phase Self through a handshake, a Protection Hex that cleared the orb puzzle, and a telepathic link that found nobody — correctly. Holds.

### **7.3 Growth Trajectory / Remaining Headroom**

*⟨Note on foreshadowing.⟩ This document treats five items as ⟨Volatile⟩ GM foreshadowing: not evidence of capability, but not nothing either. Four are the Session 15 Grey Lantern's identifications — Intrepid as Superman, Lock-Step as Green Lantern, Quasar-Force as Captain Atom, Oracle as Batman — the second of which was literally true within the hour. The fifth is Edge's legend. Session 17 added a sixth kind of item: **another universe's Teen Titans**, whose mentor *"goes by Batman"* and *"used to be called Raven,"* and whose members mirror nobody on this team. Nothing in it is scored.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.7`
   * **All-time.** The steepest curve on the team and the most consequential. The mechanical half is a published Rank 3→6 plan that ends in spacetime manipulation, the longest runway on the roster. The unmechanical half is the mother box, and Session 15 moved it further than any session before: *"When you learn, she learns."* ⟨Ruling⟩ Energy Absorption is the remaining Rank-4 survivability purchase.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.7.** **The Awakening channel produced sentences.** The box spoke in words for the first time on record: *"Quasar?... I am here... I am still with you. Don't be afraid... I will find a way."* What she said was that she cannot reach the Source. The headroom is unchanged; the dependency it rests on is scored in 6.3.
2. **Lock-Step** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** **Powerful Hex scales with rank as a blank cheque**, and three of the most significant magical figures on Earth are competing over who trains him. Session 15 added the most headroom on the board, all of it plot: a Grey Lantern succession, a secondary ring that will charge once he remembers the oath, and a diagnosis — lantern-derived, linked to Oa, *"unconsciously altering time waves."* ⟨Ruling⟩ Time Powers are banned, so whatever the ring does will arrive as GM-granted plot.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** *"Eventually, I will remember the vow, okay, guys?"* — and Intrepid's verdict on the order, typed into chat, that grey is *"the color of 'Lazy'."* Holds.
3. **Edge** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** **The newest member had the third-longest runway** in Session 16: all Rank 3.5 advances taken, Quick Learner, a League-adjacent hero training him, and behind all of it the legend — a reborn ancient weapon whose true name is lost.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.0.** ⟨Re-spec⟩ The rebuild spent the runway's first stretch. Two Rank-4 powers are already on his Rank 3→4 track (Lightning Actions and Supernova), and Discipline is at its maximum level of 4. In exchange, the legend now has a mechanical expression: an area power that fits *"cut down armies."* The roster's profile no longer hedges either: *"An ancient army-slaying cataclysm reborn."* ⟨Volatile⟩ If the legend is literal, this is still the axis it moves most. Holds.
4. **Oracle** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** STAR Labs hardware can be revised, and Precognition has obvious room to extend: growth by upgrade rather than by unfolding. Session 14 made his module a component in a League-supervised build; Session 15 added the Grey Lantern resonance. **The single highest-value purchase available to him** is any ego- or logic-based power that projects strength.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.5.** ⟨Advance⟩ ⟨Flag closed⟩ The two advances bought Agility and Accuracy, completing his Rank 3→4 track. **This entry was not understating him**, and the highest-value purchase now waits for Rank 4 at the earliest.
5. **Intrepid** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** On the sheet he is linear: bigger numbers on the same four things. **Off the sheet, Session 15 suggested a discontinuity** — a time traveller, a hive mind and a viral stream all called him Superman — and he answered with the first ambition on his file: *"Not yet anyway."* **If the GM is foreshadowing, this is the most important line in his file. If not, it is a joke, and this entry falls back to 4.5.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.0.** Nobody called him Superman. He did call a gladiator uniform *"my new uniform from going forward,"* which is not the same thing. Holds.

### **7.4 Justice League Readiness — Capability**

*Capability only. Character is 1.14.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** Asked who the smartest person in the Justice League was, Wonder Woman pointed at him. In Session 15 he did League-grade work inside the League's headquarters: three pieces of hardware in six days, one of which beat a known villain team's transport in play. **He is the only member whose capability would be League-relevant today rather than in five years.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.0.** Without a lab, he found every camera in a hostile facility and read its nullifiers on a single check. Holds.
2. **Oracle** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** Precognition and healing are precisely the two capabilities a large distributed organisation values most and fields least. Session 14 is what a vetting officer would find in the combat log: **a support specialist with no mobility, no escape and no Focus damage reduction can be switched off by a competent opponent.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.5.** ⟨Advance⟩ The advances were declared and bought none of the three things in that sentence, and a copy of him found a fourth way to switch him off. Holds.
3. **Edge** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** **A League-shaped specialist** in Session 16: the League has long carried a marksman, and his mentor knows what one looks like. Best ranged attack on the team, the only Heightened Senses, a healing factor and a dodge.
   * **Session 17: 7.0 → 7.5 (+0.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ **Area artillery with the team's best single-target line and a guaranteed first turn** is an even more League-shaped profile than a marksman's. An evaluator would want to see the Supernova used once, and an answer about what powers it.

   *Tie:* Oracle over Edge at `7.5`, broken on record.
4. **Lock-Step** `7.4`
   * **All-time.** Strategic teleportation is a League-scale logistical asset and Powerful Hex a League-scale wildcard. Session 13 closed a universe-scale incident, and on Kwa a Lantern called for the Justice League and the Titans answered in its place. What the scenario required was first contact without a fight, the capability the League prizes most and fields least.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.4.** The newcomer passes him by a tenth on the rebuild.
5. **Intrepid** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** Genuine top-tier durability and area damage at his rank. **The League has several of him, and none of them is the reason it works.** The effects that removed him from fights in Sessions 14 and 16 are ones League-tier opponents have as a matter of course.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.0.** Nothing changed.

### **7.5 Justice League Readiness — Overall**

*Character (1.14) and capability (7.4) combined, plus the situational blockers that belong to neither.*

1. **Oracle** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** First on character by a wide margin and second on capability, with no blocker attached. **He was the only member who could be put forward without a conversation about caveats first**, and in Session 14 he reported his own defeat to the sponsoring Justice Leaguer, unprompted, on the night.
   * **Session 17: 7.8 → 7.5 (−0.3).** **The caveat conversation now exists.** A sponsor would want to discuss two things before forwarding him: what he said when the newcomer asked about the errand, and *"most of the people telling us what to do don't know what they're doing."* Still first.
2. **Quasar-Force** `6.3`
   * **All-time.** He leads capability outright, sits high on character, and has been personally endorsed by the acting Batman, and he is nonetheless years away. **The blockers are situational rather than personal:** his age, an open fatality investigation, and ⟨OOC⟩ an Apokoliptian-modified New Genesis artifact bonded to his brain.
   * **Session 17: 6.6 → 6.3 (−0.3).** The character side fell half a point, on the sentence about the lab.
3. **Edge** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** In Session 16, a clean first session, a League-adjacent mentor willing to vouch for him, and capability in a role the League understands. The blockers were his age, an afternoon of record, and what the legend says he is.
   * **Session 17: 5.0 → 6.0 (+1.0).** Both halves rose: character by two points on the objection, and capability by half a point on the rebuild. **He passes Intrepid.**
4. **Intrepid** `5.3`
   * **All-time.** A clean record and top-tier durability and area damage for his rank. **Unready rather than unsuitable, and the gap is experience.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.3.** Nothing changed.
5. **Lock-Step** `1.2`
   * **All-time.** Real League-scale assets underneath a character file that would not reach a vote. **Doctor Fate has publicly declared him a danger to the world**, and since Session 15 a panel will not get past a solicited killing through a villain team. A cosmic order has vouched for him; the League has never heard of it.
   * **Session 17: 1.5 → 1.2 (−0.3).** The character side fell half a point, on the doctrine.

### **7.6 Overall Value / Contribution**

*The whole question, combat and non-combat together: what does the team lose if this person walks away tomorrow?*

1. **Oracle** `9.1`
   * **All-time.** First in combat contribution, second in non-combat contribution, first in standing and first in the pecking order, and the moral spine of a group that badly needs one. He is the only healer, the only dedicated buffer, the household and the medic. In Session 16 every recovery of the fight went through him.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.1.** Two of the five could not have got up without him. Holds.
2. **Quasar-Force** `8.9`
   * **All-time.** Almost exactly as valuable, by a completely different route: the analytical engine, the intelligence apparatus, the best control and mobility in the party, joint-highest base damage, and the widest institutional reach on the roster. **The gap to Oracle has always been the healing monopoly**, plus the fact that one strike can remove him from a fight.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.9.** The maze, the cameras and the enemy healer. Holds. *Volatile* at two tenths.
3. **Lock-Step** `8.3`
   * **All-time.** Monopolies of his own: the only person on this team who can move it strategically and the only person who is good with people. **Against:** he takes his liabilities with him, and some of what he generated may cost more than it returns. In Session 16 he was the way out.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.3.** The intelligence, the frame for the bout, and the maze teleports, against the door. Holds.
4. **Edge** `7.3`
   * **All-time.** What the team would have lost if he had walked after Session 16: the best ranged shot on the roster, the only Heightened Senses, the shot that denied the brood its objective, and the rear guard that let the extraction start.
   * **Session 17: 7.0 → 7.3 (+0.3).** Add the team's second area attack, its fastest knockout, its food, and the only voice in the room that asked whether the errand was their problem.
5. **Intrepid** `6.7`
   * **All-time.** The team's tank: the member who absorbs the damage that would kill anyone else and ends fights outright, the only real tank and the cheapest member to field. **What the team loses if he walks is the thing that stands between more fragile people and whatever hits them first.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.7.** Nothing changed.

### **7.7 Overall Power / Potency**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** The highest ceiling on the team: first in engineering, lab forensics, cyber, coordination and alliance capital, first in crowd control, grappling, personal mobility, objective creation and combat synergy, and the steepest growth curve on the roster. **What holds the power ranking below the ceiling has always been the floor.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.0.** Nothing on the sheet moved.
2. **Lock-Step** `7.7`
   * **All-time.** The highest versatility on the team by a distance, and first on social, team mobility, de-escalation, rescue, stealth and solo operation, held below first by a shallow Focus pool, the weakest power mastery on the team, and a non-combat profile that is empty outside the social column.
   * **Session 17: holds at 7.7.** Nothing on the sheet moved.
3. **Oracle** `7.4`
   * **All-time.** First on seven combat axes and on medicine, sensory awareness, domestic utility and teaching. **This axis has never measured his value** (7.6 does); it measures his power, and his power is narrow in the places a head-to-head comparison counts.
   * **Session 17: 7.3 → 7.4 (+0.1).** ⟨Advance⟩ The heavier line and two points of defence.
4. **Edge** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** In Session 16, second on combat potency, fourth on non-combat potency and third on growth: **a sharp specialist with a long runway.**
   * **Session 17: 6.5 → 7.0 (+0.5).** ⟨Re-spec⟩ Second on combat potency by a smaller margin, an area attack, and Ego 7 under the non-combat profile. Still fourth.
5. **Intrepid** `6.3`
   * **All-time.** Dominant in a narrow band, and genuinely dominant there. **He stays last here without losing anything**, because a combined power ranking also counts the seventeen non-combat axes.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.3.** Nothing changed.

**The inversion between 7.6 and 7.7 holds.** Oracle is first on value and third on power; Quasar-Force is second on value by two tenths and first on power by 1.3. Edge is fourth on both, and Intrepid last on both.

## **8\. Fame (Exposure)**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

*Exposure only — how widely known, independent of whether the opinion is good. Opinion is Section 9.*

*⟨Note⟩ **Session 17 was broadcast, to an audience in another universe.** The hosts produce *"arena ball"* for viewers who *"like to see superheroes fight alternate world versions of their same team"*, with cameras in every room and promised replays *"like Dragon Ball Z."* Lock-Step played to it on purpose (*"if we're being interdimensional famous, right?"*). On it, Quasar-Force told another universe's Titans that he will not age and where his father works, and Lock-Step gave his first name. **None of it reaches Earth**: no public, outlet, institution or community on this team's world saw any of it, and the hosts promise to return the team to the moment it left. This section measures exposure on Earth, so it holds on every axis. The interdimensional exposure is recorded in §1.6 and §10.4, where it is a discipline finding rather than a fame one.*

### **8.1 Fame — Public**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.3`
   * **All-time.** The most photographed member of the team by a wide margin. He hovered upside down outside a bank for an entire robbery in a cosmic-mural nanosuit, flew a 120-foot yacht five hundred feet into the air over a party of teenagers, and killed a man in an incident that generated protests, a threatened lawsuit and a televised expert theorising about his mental state. He is publicly identified, looks eleven, and flies everywhere. In Session 15 he was the centrepiece of the stream that went viral.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.3.** Nothing reached Earth's public.
2. **Lock-Step** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** Word-of-mouth famous, and deliberately so: hashtags, an Instagram Day, a bank rescue in front of news cameras, and Blüdhaven's teenage social scene. Since Session 15 he is a trending broadcaster with a co-producer, a house drone, a channel and a sign-off.
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.5.** He performed for the producers' cameras, which is a different audience. Holds.
3. **Oracle** `4.0`
   * **All-time.** Minimal public visibility, and Extreme Appearance means anyone who *does* see him remembers exactly what they saw. In Session 15 he appeared on the stream and got a channel.
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Intrepid** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** A Secret Identity, minimal logged visibility, and fights mostly indoors. Until Session 15 he was nearly invisible; the viral stream then introduced him with its most shareable line — *"maybe the next Superman."*
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** A public identity his own entry calls *"not very well known,"* white hair and glowing eyes, and no logged public appearance. ⟨Flag⟩ The roster's condensed entry once listed his identity as *"???"*; it now reads *Public*, which settles it.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.0.** Nothing changed.

### **8.2 Fame — Media**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.6`
   * **All-time.** The primary media-footage subject of the bank robbery, the yacht flight against the moon, and a fatality that put a named science advocate on television. **He is the most covered member of this team by a wide margin, and the coverage has been accelerating**, most recently with a week of Watchtower lab footage.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.6.** Nothing was published on Earth.
2. **Lock-Step** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** Media-native by deliberate choice. In Session 15 he stopped being only a source for the media; for a week he *was* the media. ⟨Volatile⟩ The five teenagers he captioned were the five he fought in Session 16, and the one he cuffed was left in a mob warehouse.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.0.** He was content for somebody else's show this time. Holds.
3. **Oracle** `4.2`
   * **All-time.** Minimal footage, though Extreme Appearance guarantees anything captured is memorable.
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.2.** Nothing changed.
4. **Intrepid** `3.8`
   * **All-time.** A Secret Identity, and fights where cameras aren't. Since Session 15, *"the next Superman"* is a headline somebody else has already written.
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.8.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** No media record on file.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.0.** Nothing changed.

### **8.3 Fame — Law Enforcement (Police, Criminal Justice System)**

*The full criminal-justice apparatus at every level — police, detectives, prosecutors, criminal courts, corrections, and federal enforcement bodies. Corporations, research institutions, civil courts, schools, and city government are 8.4.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** **On more criminal-justice records than the rest of the team combined.** Blüdhaven PD knows him by name; detectives hold him as a cooperating witness across the Lex-Corp case; **a criminal court holds an open death investigation with his name on it**; and ARGUS knows him because Amanda Waller tasked him personally.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** Nothing added or removed.
2. **Lock-Step** `4.6`
   * **All-time.** Until Session 15, known to this apparatus as a pattern rather than as a person — habitual breaking and entering and an accumulating truancy record. Session 15 gave the pattern a name and a publication record, and the cuffed teenager left in the warehouse in Session 16 will reach the apparatus the moment anyone finds her.
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.6.** Nothing reached the apparatus.
3. **Oracle** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Present at the same incidents and rarely the one talking to officers. Restrained suspects and no bodies is a good outcome and a quiet one.
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.5.** Nothing changed.
4. **Intrepid** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** A Secret Identity and no logged contact of any kind.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `1.5`
   * **All-time.** No record. His backstory begins with an assault near him that ended with the attacker's weapon sliced to pieces; whether that reached a police report is not on file.
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.5.** Nothing changed.

### **8.4 Fame — Other Non-Superheroic Institutions**

*Everything institutional **outside** the criminal-justice system: corporations, research bodies, civil courts, licensing and revenue systems, schools, and city government.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** Lex-Corp's legal department has him identified by name and has issued a cease-and-desist. STAR Labs knows him through his father and directly: two loans of suppression cuffs, a meeting with its Blüdhaven CFO, and Doctor Varga's interest. In Session 15 he traced a STAR Labs vehicle and parked a drone in its warded garage. **Corporate and scientific rather than judicial, and broader than anyone else's.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.0.** Nothing changed.
2. **Oracle** `6.2`
   * **All-time.** STAR Labs built his body, which makes him a permanent case file at a major research institution. School adds a district record, and a museum's staff knows the cyborg who mapped their entrances.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.2.** Nothing changed.
3. **Lock-Step** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** No employer, no registrations, no assets, a truancy record, a stipend implying a payer, and since Session 15 a platform presence and a STAR Labs employee's face on his stream.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Intrepid** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** A school district record, a Secret Identity and a social-media account he did not set up.
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.0.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** A school record with grades that were *"already bad"* before the powers arrived.
   * **Session 17: holds at 2.0.** Nothing changed.

### **8.5 Fame — Superhero Community**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** Nightwing has observed him since before he joined the team, ⟨OOC⟩ Bruce Wayne holds the dormant mother-box shell, Wonder Woman has met him several times, and Guy Gardner gave him an open information channel. Session 14 added a founding member who decided he needed protecting and a League researcher; Session 15 a week aboard the Watchtower and a Grey Lantern's verdict.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.0.** Nothing changed.
2. **Lock-Step** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** Three of the most significant magical figures on Earth have opinions about him, and in Session 14 he became memorable to the Justice League in the way nobody wants to be, wrapped in the Lasso of Truth. **Everyone in this community who has met him remembers which one he was.**
