   * **Session 17: holds at 8.0.** Nothing changed.
3. **Oracle** `6.5`
   * **All-time.** Mentor (Nightwing), a body built by STAR Labs, and the member who answers when somebody senior asks how the team is doing. In Session 14 he called Wonder Woman to report the loss, which is the interaction a senior hero remembers.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.5.** Nothing changed.
4. **Edge** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** **Known to the right people before he was known to the team**: Black Canary volunteered to train him, the Birds of Prey were the ones called when he went looking for help, and Nightwing recruited him personally.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.0.** The person who found him and called the Birds was Barbara Gordon, which the last edition already counted. Holds.
5. **Intrepid** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Mentor (Nightwing) was very nearly the complete list until Session 15, when a viral stream shot inside the Watchtower called him the next Superman. **He has never done anything this community would talk about, and it now has something to talk about.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.5.** Nothing changed.

### **8.6 Fame — Supervillain Community**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.3`
   * **All-time.** Both recognised on sight and specifically discussed. Gangers shout "Titans!" on spotting him, every captured villain has met him because he conducts the interrogations, and the Destined, Viral, Hexcaliber and Aegis-Girl have all targeted him. **In Session 16 the underworld had a written answer to him and said so**: fame, fragility, grappling.
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.3.** Nothing changed.
2. **Lock-Step** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** Recruited by Shadow-Boxer and the Red Aegis boss, the Destined's negotiating partner, on file with the Marrow Syndicate, and the subject of a bespoke temporal artifact. **As of Session 15 the underworld also knows his rate.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 8.5.** Nothing changed.
3. **Intrepid** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** Known by effect rather than by name: the one who ends fights. Since Session 15, also known by a public comparison to the one hero every villain in the world has a plan for.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Edge** `4.0`
   * **All-time.** **Introduced to the underworld in one afternoon, by the people he beat**, and two of the five walked away to tell it: *"They keep coming back up like Daisys!"*
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.0.** Nothing changed.
5. **Oracle** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Recognised and the least discussed; the villains who fight him mostly learn they can't hit him. Session 14 supplied a louder story, a goddess who drained him to 3 Focus, and in Session 16 somebody on the other side had studied him well enough to answer his Return Fire.
   * **Session 17: holds at 3.5.** Nothing changed on Earth. In another universe, a power copier studied him well enough to become him.

### **8.7 Fame — Civilian Life / Non-Superheroic Identity Exposure**

*How exposed the person behind the mask is.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Total. Endo Wenas is publicly identified, his parents are known and reachable in Slate Hill, his father's employer is known, and his family's involvement with a clinician is documented. **There is no mask to be behind.**
   * **Session 17: holds at 9.5.** He told another universe where his father works, which Earth already knew.
2. **Oracle** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** Wade Dobson is not concealed, and Extreme Appearance means concealment is not available to him anyway. What limits the score is that few people are looking.
   * **Session 17: holds at 6.0.** Nothing changed.
3. **Lock-Step** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** For fourteen sessions he did not appear to have a civilian identity at all. **Session 15 supplied both halves at once**: a name, Eric Williams, and its link to the hero, held by parties outside Earth's institutions.
   * **Session 17: holds at 5.0.** *"You could call me Eric. It's not like we'll ever meet again."* One more party outside Earth's institutions holds the first half. Holds.
4. **Edge** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Murphy King has a public identity, *"not very well known,"* and a face that is hard to mistake. A school, a family the roster does not describe, and mentors whose names are public.
   * **Session 17: holds at 4.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Intrepid** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** The only fully Secret Identity on the roster, maintained without a single logged lapse.
   * **Session 17: holds at 1.0.** Not one, even here.

## **9\. Reputation / Opinion**

**Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`**

*Session 17 happened in another universe, out of sight and reach of every party this section scores, so it holds everywhere but one cell. The one move is Edge's reputation in the superhero community, on the roster's confirmation of his second mentor. ⟨Retcon⟩ The last edition's references to a Wonder Woman debrief after the warehouse are withdrawn throughout. Three things Session 17 recorded would move this section the day they reach the people it scores, and none of them has:*

* *Oracle's view of the adults above him.*
* *Quasar-Force's statement about the lab.*
* *Lock-Step's doctrine.*

### **9.1 Reputation — Public**

1. **Oracle** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** Nothing negative attaches to him and nothing ever has. He heals people, asks for surrender first, is polite, and is visibly a teenager doing a hard job well. The viral stream of Session 15 introduced him as *"the guy that always tells us what we do wrong and tries to fix it,"* which is a teammate's joke and an accurate résumé.
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.0.** Nothing reached the public.
2. **Intrepid** `+5.5`
   * **All-time.** Low salience because of the Secret Identity, and uniformly positive within that. **Since Session 15 the public has a story to attach to him, and it is the best one available:** *"maybe the next Superman."*
   * **Session 17: holds at +5.5.** Nothing reached the public.
3. **Lock-Step** `+4.5`
   * **All-time.** Genuinely popular with the demographic that matters most for word-of-mouth, and carrying real risk. **Net positive, with a fuse in it**, and Session 15 lengthened the fuse: on the same channel that trended, he mocked five released teenagers by face.
   * **Session 17: holds at +4.5.** Nothing reached the public.
4. **Edge** `+2.0`
   * **All-time.** Public but *"not very well known,"* with nothing for or against him on record. His first public story, when it comes, could be a rescue or a legend about a monster.
   * **Session 17: holds at +2.0.** Nothing reached the public.
5. **Quasar-Force** `+1.5`
   * **All-time.** **The highest variance on the team; the ranking reflects net position, not dislike.** He is the most beloved and the most controversial figure the Titans have: a spectacular, obviously good-hearted flying child, and the boy who killed a man. **The death is still the headline. Since Session 15 it has competition.**
   * **Session 17: holds at +1.5.** Nothing reached the public.

*⟨Volatile⟩ Two stories have not surfaced*, and nothing in Session 17 changed either: the Session 14 museum theft, and three teenagers left unconscious in a mob warehouse — one in handcuffs — by a team whose public voice had mocked all five of them a week earlier.

### **9.2 Reputation — Media**

1. **Lock-Step** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** Media-native. He poses, posts and supplies quotable lines, and the handcuff ritual is a recurring bit that writes its own headline. Session 15 was a larger audience with more ammunition in it, including material a hostile outlet would want.
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.0.** Nothing published on Earth.
2. **Oracle** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** The safe, sympathetic angle: a cyborg teenager who heals people and asks criminals to surrender. There is no negative story about him available.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.0.** Nothing changed.
3. **Intrepid** `+4.5`
   * **All-time.** A classic-looking hero in a red cape, with nothing to write about and nothing bad to write. Since Session 15 the Superman line writes his headline for him.
   * **Session 17: holds at +4.5.** Nothing changed.
4. **Edge** `+1.5`
   * **All-time.** No coverage on file. The angle an outlet would find is the most striking one on the team — white hair, glowing eyes, a teenager trained by Black Canary — and the most dangerous if the word *monster* ever gets attached to it.
   * **Session 17: holds at +1.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Quasar-Force** `0.0`
   * **All-time.** **The best footage and the best controversy at once, which is the worst possible combination.** Session 15's week of Watchtower footage brought him to exactly neutral by giving every outlet a second, much better story about the same boy.
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.0.** Nothing changed.

### **9.3 Reputation — Law Enforcement (Police, Criminal Justice System)**

*Same scope as 8.3 — police, detectives, prosecutors, criminal courts, corrections, and federal enforcement, at every level.*

1. **Oracle** `+5.5`
   * **All-time.** He demands surrender before engaging, heals the people he fights, and has never generated an incident report. Officers arriving after an Oracle engagement find restrained suspects and no bodies, which is the single thing this apparatus most wants from a costumed teenager.
   * **Session 17: holds at +5.5.** Nothing reached the apparatus.
2. **Intrepid** `+2.0`
   * **All-time.** Unknown to them and carrying nothing negative.
   * **Session 17: holds at +2.0.** Nothing changed.
3. **Edge** `+1.0`
   * **All-time.** Unknown to them, no record, and a first fight in which everyone he dropped was knocked out rather than killed. Below Intrepid because his public identity gives the apparatus a name to attach anything to.
   * **Session 17: holds at +1.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Quasar-Force** `+0.5`
   * **All-time.** Working officers regard him well: statements in person, chain of custody, evidence handed over unprompted. **But the apparatus is not only its officers**: prosecutors and a criminal court hold an open death investigation, Lex-Corp's counsel is pushing a wrongful-death theory into the same system, and ARGUS holds him as a cultivated asset.
   * **Session 17: holds at +0.5.** The apparatus does not know what he said about the lab. If it learns, this axis moves.
5. **Lock-Step** `−6.0`
   * **All-time.** Habitual breaking and entering, a restrained prisoner humiliated in front of witnesses, and an assault on a minor teammate. **Every one of those is a chargeable offence rather than a bad look.** Session 15 added released juveniles broadcast by face, and out of the apparatus's sight a solicited burglary and killing. Session 16 added one of those juveniles, knocked out and left in handcuffs in a crime syndicate's warehouse.
   * **Session 17: holds at −6.0.** This axis moves when someone finds her, or the errand reports back.

### **9.4 Reputation — Other Non-Superheroic Institutions**

*Same boundary as 8.4 — corporations, research bodies, civil courts, licensing and revenue systems, schools, and city government.*

1. **Oracle** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** Institutions are built to approve of people like him. STAR Labs would regard him as a success story worth publicising, and no corporation, civil court or school has a reason to think about him twice.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.5.** Nothing changed.
2. **Intrepid** `+2.0`
   * **All-time.** Unknown, compliant, and carrying no negatives.
   * **Session 17: holds at +2.0.** Nothing changed.
3. **Edge** `0.0`
   * **All-time.** The one institution with a file on him is a school, and the file says his grades were *"already bad."*
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Quasar-Force** `−0.5`
   * **All-time.** Against him: a corporate legal department with a cease-and-desist and a wrongful-death threat. For him: STAR Labs, in its own right, and a board-level CFO who gave him a morning. **The institutions that like him are the ones with no power over him.**
   * **Session 17: holds at −0.5.** ⟨Volatile⟩ He expects the people he helped send to kill everyone in a STAR Labs facility, and his father works for STAR Labs. If that becomes known to the institution, this is the axis it lands on.
5. **Lock-Step** `−5.0`
   * **All-time.** A truancy record he is actively extending, and conduct that does not stay inside the criminal file it starts in. Session 15 gave a school, a corporation and a platform each their own reason to act.
   * **Session 17: holds at −5.0.** Nothing changed.

### **9.5 Reputation — Broad Superhero Community**

1. **Oracle** `+8.0`
   * **All-time.** He reports honestly, defers to Nightwing, refuses unethical requests from teammates, and prioritises healing over glory — exactly the profile senior heroes want to see in a teen team and recommend to each other.
   * **Session 17: holds at +8.0.** ⟨Retcon⟩ The last edition credited the team with briefing Wonder Woman the afternoon of the warehouse; it did not happen. Nothing reached the community.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** Wonder Woman pointed at him when asked who the smartest person in the Justice League was; he shares intelligence proactively; a serving Green Lantern took a personal interest; a founding member judged him worth protecting personally. ⟨OOC⟩ Against it: the fatality, and Apokoliptian-modified hardware in his skull.
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.5.** Nothing changed.
3. **Edge** `+5.5`
   * **All-time.** **Vouched for before he arrived.** Black Canary volunteered to train him, the Birds of Prey took his call for help, and Nightwing, operating as Batman, recruited him personally. The rest of the community has not met him.
   * **Session 17: +5.0 → +5.5 (+0.5).** ⟨Flag closed⟩ The roster now names his second mentor: Barbara Gordon, the Oracle who found him and called in the Birds. **Two senior figures with formal mentor roles, and a third who recruited him**, is more standing in this community than two of his teammates have after seventeen sessions.
4. **Intrepid** `+1.0`
   * **All-time.** Nobody has an opinion, and nothing negative exists to form one from.
   * **Session 17: holds at +1.0.** Nothing changed.
5. **Lock-Step** `−3.5`
   * **All-time.** The magical community is split three ways on him, with the hostile vote held by Doctor Fate, and the conventional community would object to nearly everything he does if it knew. **The first cape-adjacent order to rate him highly is one nobody else has heard of.** And the first thing the community's newest connection to this team heard from him, in Session 16, was that *"all the adults are trash."*
   * **Session 17: holds at −3.5.** The second thing the newcomer heard from him was the errand's doctrine. Black Canary's student, and now Barbara Gordon's, has two things to report. Holds until someone reports them.

### **9.6 Reputation — Broad Supervillain Community**

1. **Quasar-Force** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** **Villains like him, and it is measurable.** Shadow-Boxer respects this team because they keep their word, Cassidy Yanks volunteered her real name, and World Ender's facade collapsed under sincerity. Uncooperative professionals read him as a child to be played, and he is the one who accidentally killed a man.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.5.** Nothing changed.
2. **Lock-Step** `+5.0`
   * **All-time.** The surrender ritual is a genuine kindness in a business without much of it, and villains have cuffed themselves rather than fight him. Against it, he humiliates the ones who refuse. **Since Session 15 the underworld also knows he is a client.**
   * **Session 17: holds at +5.0.** Nothing changed.
3. **Oracle** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** He heals people who were trying to kill him minutes earlier. That gets around a jail block fast.
   * **Session 17: holds at +3.5.** Nothing changed.
4. **Intrepid** `+1.5`
   * **All-time.** Feared and respected as a physical fact, with no personal animus in either direction.
   * **Session 17: holds at +1.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `+0.5`
   * **All-time.** Barely an opinion yet, and an exasperated one: *"They keep coming back up like Daisys!"*
   * **Session 17: holds at +0.5.** Nothing changed.

### **9.7 Reputation — Iron-Side**

*Inferred from a professional educator's frame: methodical, disciplined, non-authoritarian, deliberately not appointing a leader, reporting to Nightwing daily, taking notes, with significant medical training, and willing to correct a student physically. **Iron-Side does not appear in Session 17.***

1. **Quasar-Force** `+8.5`
   * **All-time.** The ideal student from her position, and he has gone out of his way to signal it: he thanked her for teaching them, fell straight into her structured wake and bedtimes, and defended her hazard pay to Lock-Step's face. In Session 14 she took a knife out of him herself.
   * **Session 17: holds at +8.5.** Nothing changed. *"They're still good people,"* he said of the adults.
2. **Oracle** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** Compliant, structured and cooperative, and content to follow whatever education Nightwing prefers. Undemanding, reliable and easy to teach.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.5.** She has not heard what he now thinks of *"most of the people telling us what to do."*
3. **Intrepid** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** First up every morning, no friction, no complaints, and no engagement either. A perfectly fine student who leaves no impression.
   * **Session 17: holds at +3.5.** Nothing changed.
4. **Edge** `+1.0`
   * **All-time.** Not yet met in the log. The file she will open has a School obligation and grades that were *"already bad"*; the student she will meet asked for a plan and a briefing before his first fight.
   * **Session 17: holds at +1.0.** The student she will meet also asked his team for three hours of practice and was turned down. Holds.
5. **Lock-Step** `−6.5`
   * **All-time.** He argued with her at their first meeting, called her school fake to her face, accused her of doing it only for the money, and shows up last or not at all. Her calm reply — *"I am taking notes"* — remains the most quietly ominous line in the campaign. In Session 15 he asked her a question and followed the answer; in Session 16 the first thing he told the newcomer about her was the old grievance.
   * **Session 17: holds at −6.5.** Nothing changed.

### **9.8 Reputation — Nightwing**

*⟨Session 17 note⟩ **Nightwing is off screen for a fourth consecutive session.** ⟨Retcon⟩ No report reached him or anyone else from the end of Session 16, and none was possible from the pocket dimension. In Session 17 the team discussed him twice. Oracle asked the newcomer what kind of briefing Nightwing had given him — *"So now you know the kind of stuff we got to put up with"* — and Lock-Step reported it as *"you'll find out."* (⟨Note⟩ Session 16's log records that Nightwing did brief Edge on all five villains.) **Scores are carried on earlier evidence plus what Session 17 implies.***

1. **Oracle** `+8.5`
   * **All-time.** Nightwing's channel into this team, formalised in Session 12. He called about the artifact knowing exactly how Lock-Step would react; Nightwing arranged him a private appointment with a STAR Labs board member and named him alongside Quasar-Force as equal to or better than the League bench. ⟨Note⟩ *"It's no different than Nightwing"* (Session 15) was the first critical thing anyone in Oracle's file said about his mentor.
   * **Session 17: holds at +8.5.** ⟨Note⟩ The second and third critical things followed — the briefing remark, and *"most of the people telling us what to do don't know what they're doing."* Nightwing has heard neither. The channel carried nothing for a fourth session. Holds.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** Historically the primary correspondent: a proactive intelligence-sharer, evidence-disciplined, and credited by name for the security upgrades. Session 15 took half a point on implication: the boy Nightwing has watched since before he had a codename endorsed bringing in the Destined and their *"full lethal fury."*
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.0.** Nightwing would read *"the destined will kill every man and woman in that lab"* beside it. He has not.
3. **Edge** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** **Chosen by Nightwing personally**, and briefed by him on the five villains before his first fight — the only Titan he is recorded as briefing in three sessions. A recruiter's confidence is not a track record.
   * **Session 17: holds at +6.0.** The recruit he chose objected, on the day he joined, to a killing his new team had commissioned — the profile Nightwing would want to have chosen. Holds, because Nightwing does not know.
4. **Intrepid** `+1.5`
   * **All-time.** No problems and no contribution. Invited to raise concerns at a round-table convened to hear them, he had none.
   * **Session 17: holds at +1.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Lock-Step** `−5.0`
   * **All-time.** He tried to conceal a dangerous temporal artifact from Nightwing, told the room Nightwing can't be trusted, and sedated a minor teammate. The redemption path has been visible since Session 13, and Session 15 was a list of what Nightwing's professional life is organised to prevent. **In Session 16 he used Nightwing as an authority for the first time**: *"Nightwing did say we can bring them back, so knock them out."*
   * **Session 17: holds at −5.0.** He invoked the rule again, as *"the Batman stuff,"* and then the exception: *"in this situation, I agree. Yeah, but you know, I think here's exceptions."* Nightwing would read both. He has not.

### **9.9 Reputation — Batman**

*Batman is injured, unable to fight, and has time to read files. ⟨OOC⟩ He holds the dormant mother-box shell and has probably concluded that Quasar-Force will not age. Absent in Session 17; scored on what his file would contain. Another universe's Teen Titans have a mentor who also goes by Batman; she is not scored.*

1. **Oracle** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** A clean record, honest reporting, medical support, and a predictive module that is both tactically valuable and technically auditable. Session 14 added the behaviour he values above all others: a total failure reported immediately and in full. ⟨Note⟩ Since Session 15 his module resonates with a teenager's time-altering powers, which is the kind of sentence Batman reads twice.
   * **Session 17: holds at +7.0.** The page Batman would mark: a module that reacted to an interdimensional abduction before anything else on the team did.
2. **Intrepid** `+5.0`
   * **All-time.** A Secret Identity, and Batman approves. No secrets, no factions, no technology he didn't earn and no file to maintain. Boring is a compliment in this room.
   * **Session 17: holds at +5.0.** Still nothing on file.
3. **Edge** `+2.0`
   * **All-time.** Recruited under Batman's name by somebody else. Bruce Wayne, reading the file, would find a Monster-origin fifteen-year-old with a legend about cutting down armies and a power that slices through objects by concentration, plus Black Canary vouching for him. **Batman likes capabilities he can verify, and nobody can yet verify what the legend is.**
   * **Session 17: holds at +2.0.** The file now has Barbara Gordon's name on it and an area power in it, and the legend in the roster's own words. The two cancel. Holds.
4. **Quasar-Force** `+1.0`
   * **All-time.** **The most divided assessment in the campaign.** ⟨OOC⟩ Batman holds the shell and knows about the frozen ageing, so he regards Endo as one of the most dangerous unresolved variables in Blüdhaven; Endo's conduct is exactly the behaviour Batman rewards and rarely gets from teenagers. **High opinion of the boy; alarm about the situation.**
   * **Session 17: holds at +1.0.** The situation half got a page he would read twice — the box cannot reach the Source in an isolated dimension. The boy half got the sentence about the lab. Batman has neither yet.
5. **Lock-Step** `−7.5`
   * **All-time.** The file is thick and none of it is reassuring: chaos magic, Doctor Fate's declared hostility, concealment from Nightwing, the sedations, prisoner abuse, and a founding member of the League who felt the need to use a compulsion artifact on him. **In fairness, and Batman is fair:** he also talked a planet out of a war and asked Iron-Side for help. **The pattern under the misconduct is a teenager who does the right thing when he has time and outsources the wrong thing when he doesn't.**
   * **Session 17: holds at −7.5.** Session 17 is the pattern stated by its owner: *"we can either save and protect our righteousness or actually save people's lives."* Holds.

*⟨Retcon⟩ The last edition recorded that the team went back to the tower after the warehouse and briefed Wonder Woman. It did not: the team was taken from the staging point. **The League took no part in Sessions 16 or 17 and has heard nothing from either.** Edge's mentors, Black Canary and Barbara Gordon, give the League-adjacent world a line into this team that does not run through Quasar-Force.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `+8.5`
   * **All-time.** Wonder Woman, asked who the smartest person in the Justice League was, **pointed at him.** In Session 14 professional regard became institutional responsibility: personal protection, the Watchtower, the League's mother-box specialist assigned to his case. **The League has adopted his problem.** In Session 15 he was a good guest and delivered three devices.
   * **Session 17: holds at +8.5.** *"I think the only one that's making time for us is Wonder Woman,"* he said. Nothing changed.
2. **Oracle** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** A STAR Labs product with clean conduct, regarded positively without anyone having to think about it. Session 14 gave the League reasons to think: the failure call, the exact inventory of what was taken, and the remark that the apology counted.
   * **Session 17: holds at +3.5.** Nothing changed.
3. **Edge** `+3.0`
   * **All-time.** Black Canary volunteered to train him, and her opinion carries weight with the League. Nobody else there has heard of him.
   * **Session 17: holds at +3.0.** His view of the League — *"That's not how Justice League works, okay?"* — is the most favourable on the team, and the League has not heard it. Holds.
4. **Intrepid** `0.0`
   * **All-time.** Very nearly unknown to them. In Session 14 he spent an extended debrief in front of a founding member saying that nothing could have been done better. Nothing malicious and nothing disqualifying, and not what you want in the file.
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.0.** *"Yeah, let's go with that,"* to the claim that the League is busy. Nothing changed.
5. **Lock-Step** `−5.0`
   * **All-time.** If Doctor Fate has an opinion, the League will hear it, and his conduct record will not survive first contact with Superman. Session 14 happened in the League's own house: an argument with Wonder Woman, a rant, solicited souvenirs, the Lasso of Truth. Session 15 added the stream of their station. **For, and genuinely League-shaped:** a Lantern asked for the Justice League and got the Titans, and he handled it better than the League's reputation suggests it would have.
   * **Session 17: holds at −5.0.** *"The Justice League wants us to save the world. If we fail. The world's over"* — the League would recognise its own job described, and not the conclusion he drew from it. Holds.

*⟨Thin evidence⟩ Still the least-supported axis in the document. **Session 17 adds no contact**: Guy Gardner's communicator could not reach him. Volatile throughout.*

   * **All-time.** **A near-monopoly.** Guy Gardner sought him out, made him a sandwich with the ring, got angry on his behalf that nobody had told him what was fused to his nervous system, and offered Corps files on the mother box. In Session 15 Quasar-Force called him the moment the progenitor threat looked real and followed up by text — **the Session 13 instruction used exactly as given.** Guy's opinion of the boy is very high; the Corps has not formed one.

2. **Oracle** `+2.5`
   * **All-time.** Collective credit and nothing against him, which on this axis is worth second place. **He is the member whose conduct this organisation would like best if it ever looked.**

3. **Intrepid** `+1.0`
   * **All-time.** Zero contact, zero record, and a share of the collective credit for a capture Guy rated highly.

4. **Lock-Step** `+0.5`
   * **All-time.** More contact with a Green Lantern than anyone but Quasar-Force, and it ended well — *"no hard feelings"* — after he argued that heroes should be paid and then phoned a bank to drain the man's account. Session 15 added powers read as lantern-derived and linked to Oa, and a ring from a different order that works in time. **A Lantern was amused. A Guardian would not be.**
   * **Session 17: holds at +0.5.** The first apology on his file, delivered to a man who could not hear it: *"I think he's still mad about the three dollars I took from him. I don't know, maybe it's my fault. If it is, I'm sorry."*
5. **Edge** `0.0`
   * **All-time.** No contact and no record. The Corps does not know he exists.
   * **Session 17: holds at 0.0.** *"Look, you lose me when you mentioned Grey Lantern."*

## **10\. Aggregates And Cross-Cutting Synthesis**

### **10.0 Why Three Scales**

**A single 0–10 scale cannot express liability.** Intrepid's engineering is `0.5`, which is an absence. Lock-Step's standing with Iron-Side is `−6.5`, which is an active debt that is accumulating and already has consequences in motion. Flattening both to "low" loses the distinction that matters most for play.

**A single −10…+10 scale cannot express absence.** Scoring Intrepid's engineering at −9 would claim that his engineering *harms* the team, which is false. He simply isn't an engineer, and nobody expected him to be.

So the scale follows the axis. Capability and exposure are magnitudes (`0.0–10.0`). Psychology, standing and opinion have genuine negative poles (`−10.0…+10.0`). Threat exposure is only ever a cost (`0.0…−10.0`). **The aggregates normalise everything onto one consistent signed scale**, because a composite is useless if its components point in different directions.

**Recommendation:** use `−10…+10` for composites. The sign separates "contributes less than a competent peer would" from "is a net drag", and three of five PCs sit close enough to zero that an unsigned scale would hide which side they are on. A 0–10 rescale is in 10.1.6.

**Conversion.** Magnitude axes map as **(score − 5) × 2**, anchored so that 5.0 (the competent Rank 3 baseline) becomes 0. A negative there means *"this far below what a baseline peer would bring,"* not *"actively harmful."*

***⟨Note on comparability this revision⟩*** **All five members are returning, so every figure in this section is compared with Session 16 on the same five-person board.** ⟨New axis⟩ This revision adds 1.15 Heroism, so the Session 16 figures in parentheses are **restated on 102 axes**, with 1.15 at its retroactive baseline; where a published Session 16 figure is quoted, it is labelled as published. The restatement raised every Session 16 composite: Oracle +0.043, Quasar-Force +0.011, Lock-Step +0.036, Edge +0.047 and Intrepid +0.079. Two of the comparisons carry sheet changes rather than play: Edge's rebuild and Oracle's declared advances. Section 10.1.6 separates them from the session.

**Oracle leads by 0.432, against 0.427 restated.** The published history runs 0.77, 0.81, 0.77, 0.72, 0.36 and 0.39; Heroism adds between three and four hundredths to it, because Oracle is first on the new axis and Quasar-Force fourth. **On like-for-like axes the lead moved by four thousandths.** Both men fell by about four hundredths for unrelated reasons:

- **Oracle** gained on his advances (+0.027) and lost more on play (−0.064): the errand question answered by deference (which also cost him half a point of Heroism), a burnout entry, a copy that beat him, and a module that reacted to the abduction.
- **Quasar-Force** lost on play alone (−0.041): the sentence about the lab (a full point of it on Heroism), the leaks, the penalty and the box.

**Edge rises 0.367, to +0.980 — the only composite on the board to move by more than a tenth.** Of the rise, +0.315 is play, +0.032 the rebuild and +0.021 the mentor tag (10.1.6). **The rebuild was close to neutral on the composite**: it bought more than it cost in combat and potency, and paid it back in support burden, healing, stamina and risk. His range now touches −10.0 (Healing, at 0.0), and his median fell to +1.5 even as his mean rose, which is what a profile looks like when it acquires both extremes.

**Lock-Step falls 0.043, to +0.061.** Eighteen of his axes moved, four for the better and fourteen for the worse. He is still above zero, by six hundredths.

**Intrepid rises 0.073, to −0.379**, the largest gain among the veterans: eleven of his thirteen movements went up, almost all of them on the first session in which his calls carried.

#### **10.1.6 Sensitivity**

**Including the seven fame axes keeps the flip for a third revision.** Across 102 axes, with exposure treated as favourable, the composites read:

- **Quasar-Force +3.798**
- **Oracle +3.625**
- Edge +0.638
- Lock-Step +0.324
- Intrepid −0.582

**Quasar-Force leads by 0.173**, against 0.176 restated (0.21 published). Fame is worth +0.33 to Quasar-Force, +0.26 to Lock-Step, −0.20 to Intrepid, −0.27 to Oracle and −0.34 to Edge. **Unlike last revision, that is no longer enough to drop the newcomer below Lock-Step**: he is third on this treatment too.

**Rescaling to `0–10`** via `(x+10)/2` on the fame-inclusive composite preserves the order: Quasar-Force **6.90**, Oracle **6.81**, Edge **5.32**, Lock-Step **5.16**, Intrepid **4.71**.

**Excluding the risk section as well — the 87 favourable axes only — Oracle stays in front: +4.707 to +4.621.** His lead on this treatment widens from 0.070 to 0.086. **Edge is third (+1.559)**, now 0.72 clear of Lock-Step (+0.837).

**Section-weighted treatment** gives each section equal weight instead of each axis. Over the eight non-fame sections Oracle leads +3.43 to +2.68, with Edge third at +0.82. With fame included Quasar-Force leads +3.31 to +3.04. **The weighting scheme still decides the answer.**

**Sheet sensitivity.** Two sheets changed, and the movements are tagged (play, ⟨Re-spec⟩, ⟨Advance⟩, and the roster's mentor-tag clarification):

- **Edge's rebuild** moved 31 placements and was worth **+0.032** on the composite. The mentor tag moved three more, worth +0.021. **Play moved 28 placements and was worth +0.315.** Revert the rebuild and the tag and Edge is +0.927; revert the play and he is +0.665. **The newcomer's rise is what he did and said in Session 17, not what he bought.**
- **Oracle's advances** moved five placements and were worth **+0.027**; his play was worth −0.064. Without the advances he would be +3.867, and his lead 0.404.

**Fight sensitivity.** Revert all twenty-two combat axes to their Session 16 values and the composites read Oracle +3.859, Quasar-Force +3.463, Lock-Step +0.072, Edge +0.923 and Intrepid −0.379. The combat axes were worth +0.057 to Edge (all of it the rebuild) and +0.036 to Oracle, of which +0.025 is the advances and +0.011 a de-escalation call. They were worth −0.011 to Lock-Step and exactly nothing to Quasar-Force or Intrepid. **The bout itself moved no score; a one-turn fight is not evidence of much.**

**One-session sensitivity.** Session 17 moved **130 placements on the five-person board**, four of them on the new Heroism axis:

- **Oracle** 18 (6 better, 12 worse; five of the six are his advances), net **−0.037**.
- **Quasar-Force** 19 (7 better, 12 worse), net **−0.041**.
- **Lock-Step** 18 (4 better, 14 worse), net **−0.043**.
- **Edge** 62 (40 better, 22 worse; 31 re-spec, 3 roster), net **+0.367**.
- **Intrepid** 13 (11 better, 2 worse), net **+0.073**.

**All three senior members fell, and by almost the same amount.** The session that put a moral question to the room moved its three senior members down on character, each in his own way, and moved the two juniors up.

#### **10.2.7 Method Comparison**

Both composites run on the same axes. Matched:

|  | Score-based | Position-based | Agree? |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Excluding fame (95)** | **Oracle** +3.895, Quasar-Force +3.463 *(0.43)* | **Oracle** 3.86, Quasar-Force 3.48 *(0.38)* | **Yes** |
| **Including fame (102)** | **Quasar-Force** +3.798, Oracle +3.625 *(0.17)* | **Oracle** 3.80, Quasar-Force 3.59 *(0.22)* | **No — third consecutive revision** |
| **Third place (95)** | **Edge** +0.980, Lock-Step +0.061 *(0.92)* | **Edge** 2.71, Lock-Step 2.54 *(0.17)* | **Yes — first time** |
| **Third place (102)** | **Edge** +0.638, Lock-Step +0.324 | **Level**: Lock-Step 2.618, Edge 2.618 | **Not quite — a dead tie** |
| **Raw first-place count (102)** | — | **Quasar-Force** 40, Oracle 38 | **No — fourth consecutive revision** |

**Last revision's closing note set the test for third place:** *"If Edge's score-based third survives a session with deep negatives in it, the methods will agree on him; if it does not, the position composite was right."* Session 17 gave him his first deep negative — Healing at 0.0, a −10.0 on the composite scale — and **his third survived it, and the methods now agree.** The one treatment on which they do not quite agree is the one that counts his fame, and there the position composite has him exactly level with Lock-Step, at 267 position points each over 102 axes. Without Heroism it would put Lock-Step ahead by two hundredths: the new axis, where Edge is third and Lock-Step last, is what closed it.

**The re-examination, updated.** The composite splits into its native parts:

| Component | Axes | Oracle | Quasar-Force | Leader |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| Capability (magnitude, §3.6–§7) | 56 | +3.55 | **+4.36** | Quasar-Force by 0.80 *(0.81)* |
| Character, standing & reputation (valence) | 31 | **+6.79** | +5.10 | Oracle by 1.69 *(1.66)* |
| **All favourable axes (the two above)** | **87** | **+4.707** | +4.621 | **Oracle by 0.086** *(0.070)* |
| Risk (§2) | 8 | **−4.94** | −9.12 | Oracle by 4.19 *(4.31)* |

**On what they bring, Oracle's lead grew by a hundredth and a half**: the advances raised his capability as much as play lowered his character, and Quasar-Force's character fell further. **On what threatens them, the gap narrowed for the first time**, because Oracle took three risk half-points and Quasar-Force one. **0.353 of Oracle's 0.432 composite lead is still the risk section, and 0.079 is everything else**, against 0.363 and 0.064 last revision restated. Heroism more than doubles the "everything else" share; without it, the figure would be 0.037. Closing the lead through risk alone would now need Quasar-Force's risk mean to improve from −9.12 to about −4.0.

**The finding stands as relabelled two revisions ago:** "Oracle first" on the headline composite means "Oracle safer" far more than "Oracle better." Session 17 made him slightly more "better" and slightly less "safer," and the total did not move.

**Why fame stays excluded, now that it decides the answer.** Exposure has no fixed sign: it opens doors and it paints targets. Session 16 supplied the clearest case — the brood ganged up on Quasar-Force first because of his fame. Session 17 supplied a stranger one. The hosts set a power copier against the team's planner and filmed the whole thing for an audience in another universe, and that exposure appears on none of this document's fame axes. **The only treatment that puts Quasar-Force first is still the one that scores a spotlight as an asset.**

### **10.3 The Sweeps**

*Where each character's first and last places actually cluster. Position convention follows each section: on §2 first place means **most** at risk.*

| PC | Firsts | Where they are | 5ths | Where they are |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Quasar-Force** | **40** | §5:11, §8:7, §2:6, §4:5, §7:4, §9:4, §1:2, §6:1 | 6 | §6:2, §9:2, §3:1, §4:1 |
| **Oracle** | 38 | §1:12, §4:7, §3:6, §9:6, §5:4, §7:2, §6:1 | 9 | §4:5, §2:2, §6:1, §8:1 |
| **Lock-Step** | 15 | §4:4, §6:3, §2:2, §3:2, §5:2, §7:1, §9:1 | 29 | §1:14, §9:7, §3:3, §4:2, §7:2, §6:1 |
| **Edge** | 2 | §4:2 | 15 | §4:4, §5:4, §8:4, §9:2, §3:1 |
| **Intrepid** | 7 | §4:4, §1:1, §3:1, §6:1 | **43** | §5:13, §4:10, §2:6, §7:5, §3:4, §6:2, §8:2, §1:1 |

**Oracle sweeps character, for a seventh revision.** He holds twelve of the fifteen psychology axes, six of nine team internals and six of eleven reputation axes: **24 of the 35 axes that measure who a person is rather than what they can do**, the new one included. Six of the twelve psychology firsts fell by half a point this revision — Burnout, Combat Discipline, Moral Agency, Line-Holding, JL Vetting and Heroism — and he kept all six. His combat last places rose from four to five, because the newcomer's rebuild put him above Oracle on Area Damage. **He is still the best person on this team and the worst at hurting anybody.**

**Quasar-Force sweeps capability, exposure and most of the danger, and lost one last place.** He has forty firsts — eleven of the seventeen non-combat axes, all seven fame axes, five combat axes, four of seven overall axes and six of eight risk axes. He now has six last places: Physical Durability, Power Independence, Solo Operation, Support Burden, and the two public reputations. Healing left his list when Edge's rebuild took him to zero beneath him. **Four of the six are still one fact — a sixty-Health fourteen-year-old carrying an irreplaceable object with no way to heal — and Session 17 found that the object has a dependency of its own.**

**Lock-Step is the mirror image, and Session 17 moved its middle, not its edges.** His fifteen firsts are the same list as last revision, and his twenty-nine lasts are last revision's twenty-eight plus Heroism. **All fourteen psychology last places are unchanged on the restated board** — on Heroism he is last while still above zero, the only psychology axis on which that is true — and the only psychology axis he is not last on is still Moral Agency, where the newcomer has now passed him. What moved was the middle: three second places lost (Command Chair overall, Reaction and Area Damage), and five more fourths.

**Edge's distribution is still the narrowest on the board.** He has two firsts, Single-Target and Ranged, and fifteen lasts. **Six are tie-breaks**: Crowd Control and Healing on the record, Team Mobility under the standing order, and Engineering, Cyber and Lab on Logic. **Four are fame axes**, where last means least exposed. **Five are last on substance**: Trust, Rescue, Medical, and two reputations he has not had time to earn.

**Intrepid's distribution is still the most extreme on the board.** He has seven firsts — four in combat, plus Civilian Stability, Support Burden and Power Independence — having lost Single-Target to the newcomer. He has forty-three last places, up one: Law, where the newcomer passed him. ⟨Note⟩ Six of the forty-three are risk axes, and they are good news: he is the least subvertible, least targeted and least leveraged person on the roster.

### **10.4 Structural Findings**

*Team-level conclusions that fall out of the axis data rather than from any single character. Findings are carried forward with their revision history. This pass: sixteen moved, three carried, one archived, one superseded, and three are new.*

**1\. The division of labour got a sentence, and a split.** ⟨MOVED⟩ Nobody appointed anybody; Iron-Side still declines to, Nightwing was off screen for a fourth session, and no external authority could reach the team. ⟨Retcon⟩ The Wonder Woman debrief the last edition recorded did not happen. **Inside the team, Quasar-Force gave the structure its clearest statement:** *"you teleport, Oracle predicts, and I figure."* Lock-Step ceded the maze to him by name, and the newcomer called Lock-Step *"Captain."* Then the structure split at the door: Lock-Step stayed with the frozen youngest, Oracle hung back, and Intrepid and Edge walked on. **Planning to Oracle, the crisis to Lock-Step, direction to Quasar-Force — and, for the first time, a junior pair who will go without them.** *(Standing since Session 3; externally re-anchored Session 14; internally filled Session 15; divided Session 16; stated and split Session 17.)*

**2\. The tower's perimeter is half-closed, and the threat lives in its basement.** ⟨CARRIED⟩ The team was not at the tower. The anti-phasing half is still not recorded as built. The hostile progenitor is still in STAR Labs custody on the lower floors of the tower's own building, and Quasar-Force now says *"the crooked one"* at STAR Labs *"works in our building."* *(Standing since Session 10; half-closed and relocated Session 15; carried Sessions 16 and 17.)*

**3\. Quasar-Force is a single point of failure, and the single point has one of its own.** ⟨MOVED⟩ For fourteen revisions this finding was about his body: 60 Health, no healing, and a one-strike removal on record. **Session 17 was about the object.** In an isolated dimension the mother box could not reach the Source and said so in letters — *"Cannot reach the Source... No connection. No response... I will find a way. Source—no! No! No—... Help."* — and he could not comfort her. His powers still worked in the bout. ⟨Volatile⟩ How long they would without the Source, nobody knows, and a facility wired with power nullifiers is the worst place to find out. **Energy Absorption answers the body; nothing on any sheet answers the box.** *(Standing since Session 4; first mitigation Session 15; edges tested Session 16; the box's own dependency found Session 17.)*

**4\. Threats inside the trust perimeter: one masked, one named, one new unknown.** ⟨MOVED⟩

- **Left.** ⟨OOC⟩ Exemplar I is outside the perimeter with detailed knowledge of the team.
- **Masked.** Azimuth, for as long as the circlet stays on. It stayed on through an abduction.
- **Named.** The newcomer's legend. The roster now states it as fact — *"An ancient army-slaying cataclysm reborn"* — and he told the team his control takes constant work. ⟨Flag closed⟩ His mentor tag is Barbara Gordon, not a confusion inside the team.
- **New, and outside: Firefly.** Somebody on Earth — *"Minji Lin aka Firefly aka the tiny terror aka Death from Nowhere"* — was able to *"elect"* the Titans to another dimension's arena. Nobody on the team knew the name. ⟨Volatile⟩
- **Unchanged.** STAR Labs' CFO, the Destined, and the device keyed to Lock-Step's powers.

*(Standing since Session 8; rewritten Session 15; partly answered Session 16; Firefly added Session 17.)*

**5\. Oracle has been counter-built four ways, and his advances answered none of them.** ⟨MOVED⟩ The counters so far:

- **Session 14:** the grab and the drain.
- **Session 14:** obscurement.
- **Session 16:** his Return Fire redirected onto a teammate.
- **Session 17:** his own kit, worn by a power copier, which stunned a teammate with his Suppressive Fire and parried his swing with his own Point-Blank Parry.

⟨Advance⟩ The two advances are declared, Agility and Accuracy. Neither is a mental power that projects strength (the grab), Uncanny (the soft Ego door) or Focus damage reduction (the drain). **His Rank 3→4 track is now complete, so the answer cannot arrive before Rank 4.** *(New Session 14; untested Session 15; third counter Session 16; fourth counter and advances declared Session 17.)*

**6\. Cost runs opposite to capability, and the newcomer's rebuild moved him toward the expensive end.** ⟨MOVED⟩ Edge gave up his healing factor for a point of Ego, and fell from +6.0 to +4.5 on support burden: the medic put 60 Focus into him within the hour. Quasar-Force fell to −3.0 on the same heal and a penalty. **The medic now carries two members who cannot get themselves up.** The burgers are the first thing anyone but Oracle has paid back in kind. *(Standing since Session 9; anchor removed Session 15; half-restored Session 16; eroded by a rebuild Session 17.)*

**7\. Robustness runs opposite to power, and the middle sagged.** ⟨MOVED⟩ The section spread held at 1.27: Lock-Step still leads and Intrepid still trails. **The members between them fell.** Oracle went negative (+0.17 → −0.17) on a module that reacted to the abduction and a copy that beat him. Quasar-Force fell to −0.57 on the box. **Three of the five now have negative robustness means**, and the newcomer is second, on a profile that has never been tested. *(Standing since Session 11; first narrowing Session 15; widened Session 16; middle sagged Session 17.)*

**8\. One healer, now carrying two members who cannot recover on their own.** ⟨MOVED⟩ Session 16 tested the healer at full load. Session 17 tested the arithmetic. **The three members with healing factors woke on their own and the two without did not**, and the rebuild had just made Edge the second of them. Oracle spent 120 of his last 125 Focus getting them up, leaving 5 of 180 and a day of sleep, before a fight he could not rest through. Lock-Step's hex-heal was not used for a third session. **The single-healer finding now has a second dimension: the number of members who depend on him between fights, not just during them.** *(Standing since Session 7; tested at full load Session 16; dependency doubled Session 17.)*

**9\. Legal exposure is concentrated in two members, and both reached a new depth on the same subject.** ⟨MOVED⟩ **Quasar-Force reached the floor (−10.0)** on foreknowledge: he is *"very confident"* the Destined will *"kill every man and woman in that lab,"* and still defends sending them. **Lock-Step moved to −9.0** on motive: *"deaths we're not worried about right now."* **The errand has stopped being a request made in a hurry and become a position held at leisure, by two people, in front of witnesses.** *(Standing since Session 8; broadened Session 14; concentrated Session 15; accumulating Sessions 16 and 17.)*

**10\. The best pairings on this team, and the one the team refused to practise.** ⟨MOVED⟩ The Oracle–Lock-Step resonance was not used. The Session 16 pairings — Edge with Intrepid, and Edge with Oracle on Focus damage — were not tested, and the second no longer exists, because the rebuild removed Edge's Focus-damage line. **The newcomer asked the team to rehearse its pairings — *"I need to form part of that"* — and the team declined**: *"our maneuvers is like dancing... you just fill it in the moment."* The telekinetic nudge remains undeclared doctrine, and the pre-mission questionnaire is still unwritten. *(Standing since Session 6; half-closed Session 15; extended Session 16; rehearsal refused Session 17.)*

**11\. "The powers were never the problem" — resolved in the affirmative.** ⟨CLOSED, ARCHIVED⟩ The finding was about Exemplar II's sheet; §11 carries the record. *(Opened Session 9; closed Session 14; archived Session 15.)*

**12\. Research institutions hold this team's biology, and one of them is a suspect.** ⟨CARRIED, with a new datum⟩ STAR Labs built Oracle's body, wants Quasar-Force in a laboratory, holds the Breeder in custody, and its CFO signed out the car that took five teenagers into its garage. **Session 17 adds that Quasar-Force's father works for STAR Labs**, which Lock-Step, at least, did not know. The same boy expects the Destined to kill *"every man and woman in that lab."* ⟨Volatile⟩ Which lab, and whose colleagues, the log does not say. *(Standing since Session 12; STAR Labs converted from custodian to suspect Session 15; carried Session 16; the father placed Session 17.)*

**13\. The Justice League has gone from client to host to backstop — and the debrief did not happen.** ⟨MOVED⟩ ⟨Retcon⟩ The last edition's *"client to host to backstop to debrief"* loses its last step. The team was taken before it reported to anyone, and the League's one line into it — Quasar-Force's channel to Wonder Woman — could not reach a pocket dimension. ⟨Flag⟩ Her Session 14 disclosure commitment was made to Exemplar II, and whether it binds the current roster is still untested. **The newcomer's view of the League is the most favourable on the team**: *"That's not how Justice League works, okay?"* *(Standing since Session 12; converted Sessions 14 and 15; debrief withdrawn Session 17.)*

**14\. Two Justice League civilian identities spoken aloud in a compromised building.** ⟨SUPERSEDED by 16⟩ *(New Session 14; superseded Session 15.)*

**15\. The team has no answer to area denial or obscurement — and now, on paper, it has both, in one member.** ⟨MOVED⟩ **Edge kept Heightened Senses 2 through the rebuild**, so the obscurement answer is still on paper. **The rebuild added the team's second area attack**, Supernova, so area damage is no longer one person's stomp. Neither was used in Session 17. The opponents who used obscurement in Session 14 are still at large, bar Ecliptica. **Both answers are the same fifteen-year-old, on the same 90 Health, with no healing factor.** *(New Session 14; worse by departure Session 15; obscurement answered on paper Session 16; area answered on paper Session 17.)*

**16\. The team is publishing itself — now to another universe.** ⟨MOVED⟩ Nothing was published on Earth. In a facility with cameras in every room, on what the hosts called an episode, Quasar-Force and Lock-Step told the opposing team, on camera:

- that Quasar-Force will not age;
- where his father works;
- that a crooked STAR Labs figure works in the team's building;
- that Wonder Woman gives them missions;
- that they know a *"Ravenwing"*;
- and Lock-Step's first name.

**For two revisions this finding was about the team producing exposure and an enemy spending it; Session 17 is the team producing it for an audience nobody on it can name.** *(New Session 15; first priced Session 16; exported Session 17.)*

**17\. A second cosmic institution has a claim on a Titan, and it works in time.** ⟨CARRIED⟩ The ring did not charge and the oath was not remembered (*"Eventually, I will remember the vow, okay, guys?"*). Lock-Step called the Grey Lantern *"my mentor technically now,"* and Intrepid called the order lazy. ⟨Ruling⟩ Time Powers are on the GM's banned list, and whatever the ring does will arrive as plot. *(New Session 15; carried Sessions 16 and 17.)*

**18\. The team has contracted a villain team for lethal work — and the newcomer objected.** ⟨MOVED⟩ **The first objection on record came from the member who had been on the team for an afternoon**: *"if they're really killing people that's kind of our problem too right?"* It drew the errand's fullest defence yet:

- **Lock-Step:** *"deaths we're not worried about right now,"* and *"we can either save and protect our righteousness or actually save people's lives."*
- **Quasar-Force:** *"the destined will kill every man and woman in that lab, whether the progenitor is there or not,"* and *"I think we're compromising on the killing."*
- **Oracle** vouched for Lock-Step's account of the stakes and said nothing about the killing.
- **Intrepid:** *"I get what you're saying."*

The newcomer's answer became the arena's rule. **The question this document carried unscored about Oracle's silence is now scored** (1.10, 1.12, 1.14, and the new 1.15). The Destined have not reported back; if the hosts' promise holds, no time has passed for them either. *(New Session 15; unresolved Session 16; first objection Session 17.)*

**19\. The roster is five, and the newcomer rebuilt himself toward the vacancy's centre.** ⟨MOVED⟩ Of the six things §11.3 said the replacement would most usefully bring, Edge now brings two on paper (an answer to obscurement, and a second area attack), two in part (field investigation; robustness) and two not at all (a second healer — the rebuild removed his healing factor — and adult or professional standing). **He also brings things the vacancy did not describe**: the team's best single-target line, its second social instrument (Ego 7), and its loudest voice against killing. §11.3 has the detail. *(New Session 15; partly filled Session 16; rebuilt Session 17.)*

**20\. The hive wanted the team, and somebody else took it.** ⟨MOVED⟩ Session 16 ended with the brood's orders changing to *"collecting you since you proved yourselves worthy."* Minutes later, at the staging point, the team was collected — by an arena's producers, on Firefly's nomination, not by the hive as far as anyone knows. Lock-Step's hexed telepathic link to Blitzkrieg found *"her mind... not present to break into,"* which is what a link across a dimensional boundary finds. ⟨Volatile⟩ Whether the two collections are one is the session's open question. *(New Session 16; overtaken Session 17.)*

**21\. The first fight in which every member went down — and the retcon of its ending.** ⟨MOVED⟩ ⟨Retcon⟩ The last edition's account of the end of Session 16 — post-fight heals, a trip home, a debrief — is withdrawn. **What remains is starker:** the team got out of the warehouse on Let's Go and the last of Lock-Step's Focus, reached a staging point, and was taken there while two of its members were still below zero. The five teenagers are where the team left them: three unconscious in a mob warehouse, one in handcuffs, two at large. *(New Session 16; corrected Session 17.)*

**22\. The team was taken off the board.** ⟨NEW⟩ For the first time, the whole team was removed from its world:

- **Every outside line failed.** The drones, the Green Lantern communicator, the teleport and the hexed telepathy all hit the dimensional boundary.
- **The captors held power over them.** They promised a return *"precisely at the moment and place that you left,"* and enforced their rules by freezing people in place. They had banks of power nullifiers that they did not turn on.
- **The fight was a show.** The hosts set the team against another universe's Titans for a show with a thirty-second minimum. The team turned it into a sparring match, and some of it into a friendship.

**Nothing the team did in Session 17 can be seen from Earth, and everything it said is on somebody's tape.** *(New Session 17.)*

**23\. The adult deficit is now the team's consensus — Oracle included.** ⟨NEW⟩ For sixteen sessions this was Lock-Step's grievance. Quasar-Force argued with it (*"they're still good people"*), and Oracle stayed out of it. In Session 17:

- **Oracle joined it:** *"I still believe in saving people. I just think that most of the people telling us what to do don't know what they're doing,"* and, about the newcomer's briefing, *"So now you know the kind of stuff we got to put up with."*
- **Another universe's Titans confirmed it, in unison:** *"yeah, pretty useless."*
- **Lock-Step summed it up:** *"You just got to stay a kid at heart because apparently, once you grow up, you know, it's just bad."*

**The member most likely to be appointed by the adults now thinks most of them don't know what they're doing**, and the newcomer — trained by Black Canary, found by Barbara Gordon, recruited by Nightwing — is the only member still arguing the other side: *"You guys have lost a lot of hope on the adults."* ⟨Note⟩ This campaign's roster notes record that the GM has a long pattern of absent or inattentive adults; the finding is about the team's view of that pattern, not a verdict on it. *(New Session 17.)*

**24\. A newcomer in a hurry, and a team that does not rehearse.** ⟨NEW⟩ Session 17 is the first session in which a member pushed the team to change how it works:

- **A formation.** *"We can train for at least three of those 24 hours."*
- **A principle.** *"We follow the rule when it's easy and when it's hard."*
- **A method.** *"one problem at a time"*, and *"you're doing a lot of assumptions."*

He won the principle and lost the formation. The argument ended with *"I give up, I completely give up"* and *"I can't believe that guy saved my life."* **The team has improvised successfully for seventeen sessions — *"I think they're very successful so far"* — and has never had a member who wanted it to stop.** Whether the formation gets trained when they get home, and who wins that argument, is a Session 18 question. *(New Session 17.)*

##

## **11\. Departed — Exemplar II (Archive and Vacancy Analysis)**

*Exemplar II's player left the campaign between Sessions 14 and 15. In the fiction he was left out of the Grey Lantern's transport; on Day 46 he "returns to earth, with only a quick goodbye to the team," and from Day 53 he "is not present" at the tower. The roster lists him under Departed PCs. **He is not ranked in this revision.** His replacement, Edge, arrived in Session 16 and rebuilt himself before Session 17; this section keeps Exemplar II's final standing as the document last measured it and measures the vacancy against the replacement as he now stands.*

### **11.1 Final Standing (frozen at Session 14)**

| Measure | Figure | Place (of five) |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| Composite (94 axes) | **+1.49** | 3rd |
| Median / SD / range | +2.5 / 4.04 / −8.0 → +8.5 | — |
| Fame-inclusive composite / 0–10 rescale | +1.64 / 5.82 | 3rd |
| Combat / Non-Combat / Robustness / All-Capability | 4.71 / 6.49 / **6.25** / 5.70 | 5th / 3rd / **1st** / 4th |
| Character / Team Standing / Reputation / Combined | +3.89 / +5.18 / +3.23 / +3.86 | 3rd / 2nd / 3rd / 3rd |
| Risk Load | −6.06 | 3rd most exposed |
| Raw position (101 axes) | 6 firsts, 15 lasts, average 3.21 | — |
| Polarity-adjusted position composite | 2.71 (median 3.0) | 3rd |

**His six first places:** Civilian Life Stability (`+7.0`), Low Operational Support Burden (`+8.5`), Field Investigation (`8.8`), Law (`7.0`), Function Without Powers (`8.0`), and Law-Enforcement Reputation (`+6.0`). Four of the six were versions of one fact: **he was the only adult with a job.**

**His final sheet.** He had 150 Health, 90 Focus, H-DR 2 and F-DR 2. His abilities were Melee 7, Agility 0, Resilience 5, Vigilance 3, Ego −2 and Logic 6, and his defences 19/19/17/15/18/18. He carried two Iconic Items, both flawed *Custom Built Parts for the Exemplar II Project* and *Human (David's DNA) use only*:

- **AI Cybernetic Implants & Sensory Bionics:** AllSpeak, Heightened Senses 2, Microscopic Awareness, Uncanny 2.
- **Torso Cybernetic Implantation:** Mighty 3, Speed Run 1, Venom Bash, Venom Burst.

The roster's Rank 3→4 plan ends at its fourth line: *"{Player left the game}."*

**His arc in this document.** He joined in Session 3 and went through four sheets in eight sessions. For three revisions this document described a generalist who bought capability and never used it. **His last session on the team was the best any character had had in the campaign**, and he played it on the sheet he already had:

- **Azimuth.** He saw the astral figure nobody else could, sketched it, and put a name on a New God missing for thousands of years.
- **Ecliptica.** He found the drone footage that identified her.
- **The casualty.** He moved the unconscious Quasar-Force out from under the figure.
- **Wonder Woman.** He told a founding member of the Justice League that she had failed a team of minors, and got *"Consider me admonished"* and a disclosure commitment in return.

Session 14 moved him on sixty-one axes, the record until Lock-Step's seventy-two in Session 15; Edge's sixty-two in Session 17 passes it, thirty-one of them on a rebuild. ⟨OOC⟩ The clone subplot — a father who intends to overwrite him — is unresolved. So is the one instrument that would have answered it for him: Microscopic Awareness, never once demonstrated on screen.

**A precedent worth noting.** Exemplar II was the campaign's serial re-specifier: four sheets in eight sessions, each rebuild moving him further from the character the document had just scored. Edge's rebuild before his second session is the first re-spec on the team since Exemplar II's, and it ran the other way. Exemplar II's re-specs moved him toward generalist capability he rarely used. **Edge's moved him from a precision role into the two gaps this section has listed since Session 15: area damage and a second social instrument.**

### **11.2 The Vacancy, Axis by Axis**

*What the team had in him at Session 14, who covered it after he left, who covered it once Edge arrived, and who covers it now that Edge has rebuilt himself.*

| Capability | Exemplar II (S14) | Covered at S16 | Covered now (S17) | What the rebuild changed |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Field Investigation** §5.4 | **8.8** (1st) | Quasar-Force 7.5 | Quasar-Force 7.8, Lock-Step 7.7 | Little. Edge (5.5) had the best epistemics in the room and his senses' results are unlogged; Lock-Step's human intelligence is now within a tenth of first. |
| **Sensory Awareness** §5.9 / counter-stealth | 8.0 (2nd) | Oracle 9.0, Edge 8.0 | Oracle 9.0, **Edge 7.5** | Heightened Senses 2 survived the rebuild; Situational Awareness did not. Untested. |
| **Law** §5.6 | **7.0** (1st) | Quasar-Force 5.0 | Quasar-Force 5.0 | Nothing. Edge (1.5) stated the principle, not the law. |
| **Alliance Capital** §5.15 | 9.0 (2nd) | Lock-Step 7.2 | Lock-Step 7.2 | A new line, not a replacement: Edge (6.5) now names Barbara Gordon beside Black Canary. |
| **Engineering / Lab / Cyber** §5.2–5.5 | 7.0 / 7.5 / 5.0 (2nd on all three) | Oracle 4.5 / 7.0 / 4.5 | Oracle 4.5 / 7.0 / 4.5 | Nothing. Edge has Logic −1 (0.5 on all three). |
| **Low Support Burden** §3.5 | **+8.5** (1st) | Intrepid +6.5, Edge +6.0 | Intrepid +6.5, Edge +4.5 | **Backwards.** The rebuild removed his healing factor, and the medic paid for it within the hour. |
| **Civilian Stability** §1.7 | **+7.0** (1st) | Intrepid +6.0 | Intrepid +6.0 | Nothing. |

| **Robustness index** §6 | **6.25** (1st) | Lock-Step 5.30, Edge 5.12 | Lock-Step 5.30, Edge 5.17 | Little. Still second; he traded a healing factor and a dodge for innate attacks and a dependency on concentration. |
| **Area Damage** §4.11 | 5.0 (2nd) | Lock-Step 3.0 | **Edge 8.5** | **Filled, on paper.** Supernova (Force): every enemy within ten spaces. Unused. |
| **Physical Durability** §4.1 | 7.8 (2nd) | Edge 6.2 | Oracle 6.3, Edge 5.5 | Backwards. Sturdy 3 does not replace a healing factor and a dodge. |
| **Law-Enforcement Reputation** §9.3 | **+6.0** (1st) | Oracle +5.5 | Oracle +5.5 | Nothing. Edge (+1.0) is unknown to the police. |

**What left that the team is better off without** is unchanged: his OPSEC record (§1.6 `−1.0` at Session 14), whose exposure did not leave with him, and his risk load, including a patron who was also his adversary.

###

### **11.3 What the Replacement Actually Brought**

*Two revisions ago this section ranked six vacancies by how much each one cost the four who remained, and said the replacement would be scored on arrival against that list. Session 16 was the arrival; Session 17 is the rebuild.*

1. **An answer to obscurement and invisibility** (finding 15). **Yes, on paper — kept through the rebuild.** Heightened Senses 2 is the power that saw through the Vapors of Galator and tracked an invisible assassin in Session 14. Nothing has tested it.
2. **A second healer** (finding 8). **No, and the rebuild made it worse.** He sold his healing factor for a point of Ego, so the team now has two members who cannot recover on their own and still one person who can heal them.
3. **Field investigation in person.** **Partly.** The instrument is excellent and the operator has Logic −1. Session 17 showed the habit an investigator needs — *"you're doing a lot of assumptions"* — and nothing yet of the method.
4. **Adult or professional standing** — law, contracts and police relations. **No.** He is fifteen. He now has two adult professional mentors, and a conduct file a panel would like (§1.14), which is a connection and a character rather than standing.
5. **Robustness**: a member who functions when powers, preparation or support are removed. **Partly.** Still second on the index (5.17); depowered he is a trained fighter with Ego 7, and powered he depends on a concentration he says he has to work at.
6. **A second area attack or real crowd control.** **Yes on area, on paper (Session 17 rebuild); no on control.** Supernova (Force) gives the team its second area attack, with a ten-space reach and wording that spares allies. He has no grab and no barrier, and ties fourth on Crowd Control.

**What nobody asked for, and he brought anyway:**

- **The team's best single-target line** (§4.10, first, taken from Intrepid): Ego ×7+7 with Smash-style scaling, two edges, and a second blast each round through Lightning Actions.
- **A guaranteed first turn** in every fight (Lightning Actions), the second-best reaction profile on the team.
- **Ego 7**, level with Lock-Step for the highest social ability score on the team, and a Monster trait that intimidates.
- **The team's conscience on the Destined errand**, on the day he joined (§1.10, §1.15, finding 18).
- **Food**, which on this team had only ever come from one person (§5.11).

Gone with the rebuild: the best ranged *precision* on the team (the called shot that destroyed the device), the second Suppressive Fire line, and the only dodge reaction on the roster.

**The verdict, revised: two of six on paper, two in part and two not at all — and a profile that now covers the vacancy's two largest combat gaps and none of its adulthood.** He replaced Exemplar II's senses and his area attack, and none of his adulthood. He is now third on both composites (+0.98 by score, 2.71 by position), second on combat value and potency, and fourth on non-combat potency.

### **11.4 Loose Ends He Leaves Behind**

- **The Azimuth file.** He produced the only worked identification from Justice League material Wonder Woman handed him. The log does not record whether the team kept the file.
- **Wonder Woman's disclosure commitment** — *"every piece of available information I have, even if it is just suspicion"* — was made to him, in front of four witnesses. ⟨Retcon⟩ The last edition thought the team had briefed her after Session 16, which would have been the first test of whether the commitment binds the current roster. It did not happen, so the question is still untested (finding 13).
- **Exemplar I.** ⟨OOC⟩ He intends to overwrite his son, he knows this team, and in Session 10 he hacked a phone in seconds. He is outside the perimeter, and the roster's own backstory note anticipates him becoming *"an enemy of David and the group."*
- **Quasar-Force's lab access.** The roster still lists *"Exemplar's Dad's Lab,"* and it should be treated as stale.
- **Bedroom 3** is occupied again, by Edge, who has not been recorded sleeping in it: his first rest as a Titan was in a captor's bunk room, in another universe.
- **⟨OOC⟩ Precedent.** This campaign has lost three player characters before him: Robin was kidnapped, tortured and placed at the centre of a sacrifice ritual; Magius was abducted, and the abduction turned out to be staged; Up-Tempo quit silently. The GM has turned departures into plot before, and the father is the obvious hook if the GM wants one. ⟨Volatile⟩

##

## **12\. Closing note on this revision.**

**Session 17 had no sheet changes among the veterans except Oracle's two declared advances, one rebuilt newcomer, a day of argument, and the shortest fight on record.** The team was taken from a staging point minutes after the warehouse — ⟨Retcon⟩ not from home, after a debrief, as the last edition said — and woke in a pocket dimension where every outside line was dead. Its hosts set it against another universe's Teen Titans for a show. The team turned the show into a sparring match, the newcomer knocked out his opponent in one turn and sat down, and the session paused at the start of Round 2.

**The standings did not move, and the reasons did.** Oracle's composite lead held at 0.43 — 0.432 against 0.427 restated with Heroism (0.394 as published) — while both leaders fell by about four hundredths:

- **Oracle** answered the errand question with deference, entered his first burnout item, and lost to a copy of himself.
- **Quasar-Force** said the sentence about the lab, leaked his life to another universe, and could not comfort the box.

**Lock-Step fell by the same amount**, on the door, the doctrine and the formation he would not rehearse. **The two juniors rose: Intrepid on the first session in which his calls carried, and Edge by 0.37, to +0.98, 86% of it on what he did and said rather than on his rebuild.** The score and position composites now agree that Edge is third.

**One axis was added: 1.15 Heroism** — risk taken or cost paid for someone else, toward the public, teammates or enemies. Its order is the same at the retroactive Session 16 baseline and after Session 17: Oracle, Intrepid, Edge, Quasar-Force, Lock-Step. Four of its five scores moved. **The three members who defended the errand or vouched for its author fell; the one who objected rose.** Intrepid held: the ram he took and the traps he walked balanced an *"I get what you're saying."* It is the only psychology axis on which Lock-Step is last and still positive, because nobody on the team has gone into more danger for more people, and nobody else has commissioned a killing.

**Last revision asked Session 17 to test ten things:**

1. **The three in the warehouse, and the 24-hour clock.** Not tested, and frozen: if the hosts keep their promise, the team returns to the moment it left, with the three where they were left and the clock where it stopped. Lock-Step's link to Blitzkrieg found no mind to reach, because she was a universe away.
2. **The hive's collection order.** Overtaken. The team was collected minutes later — by an arena's producers, on Firefly's nomination. Whether the hive and Firefly are connected is the session's open question (finding 20).
3. **The Destined errand.** Not resolved, and for the first time **objected to**: by the newcomer, on the day he joined, which drew the errand's fullest defence yet from Lock-Step and Quasar-Force. **It also settled Oracle's voice**: he vouched for the errand's author and said nothing about the killing. Now scored in 1.10, 1.12, 1.14 and the new 1.15.
4. **Oracle's advances.** **Declared**: Agility 4 → 5 and Accuracy 1 → 2. Neither is the escape power or Uncanny, so §4.2, §4.5, §6.4 and §7.3 resolve to no change, and finding 5 gained a fourth counter instead.
5. **Edge's senses against obscurement.** Not tested. The senses survived the rebuild.
6. **Edge's legend, and the mentor tag.** **The tag is settled**: Barbara Gordon. **The legend is no longer hedged by the roster** — *"An ancient army-slaying cataclysm reborn"* — and he said what keeping it down costs: *"I practice my concentration a lot because if I don't, things could get bad."* What the monster is, and whether it can be woken, is still unanswered.
7. **Quasar-Force's floor.** Not tested; he took no Health damage. The thing tested instead was the box (finding 3).
8. **The healing backup.** Not used, for a third session. The healer emptied himself before the bout instead, and the rebuild added a second member who depends on him (finding 8).
9. **Nightwing's first report.** Impossible from a pocket dimension, and ⟨Retcon⟩ the one report the last edition thought had gone to Wonder Woman did not. When it comes, it has to cover:
   * Day 56;
   * the warehouse;
   * the recruit Nightwing briefed without telling the team;
   * an abduction to another universe;
   * and what two of his longest-serving students now say about the adults who tell them what to do.
10. **The flip and the third place.** The fame-inclusive score composite favours Quasar-Force for a third revision (by 0.17) while the position composite favours Oracle, and the raw first-place count has disagreed with the headline for four. **Third place survived its first deep negative** — Healing at 0.0 — and the methods now agree on Edge, except on the fame-inclusive treatment, where the position composite has Edge and Lock-Step dead level; without Heroism it would put Lock-Step ahead by two hundredths.

**What Session 18 should test:**

1. **The bout.** It paused at the start of Round 2:
   * Quasar-Force is in Briar Patch's branches, and Lock-Step is frozen.
   * Oracle and Edge are coming off stuns, and Intrepid is up after a 72-point ram.
   * Four-Fold is at 1 Health, and Pink-Shift is in the staging room.

   The win condition and *"what will happen to the loser team"* are unknown. Whether the team ends a staged fight without hurting anyone badly — or the hosts decide thirty seconds was not enough — decides §4.17, §4.18 and finding 22.
2. **The nullifiers.** They were off. If the hosts turn them on, §6.2 stops being hypothetical, and what happens to a boy whose cells run on a mother box is the worst-case version of it.
3. **The box.** *"I will find a way. Source—"* — whether the isolation affects Quasar-Force's powers, whether the box recovers when they return, and whether she can be comforted. §1.2, §2.6, §6.3 and finding 3 all wait on it.
4. **Edge's Supernova.** The team's second area attack has not been thrown. Its first use decides whether §4.11's 8.5 was right, and whether *"carve through everything around him"* is a description or a warning (§4.16, §6.5).
5. **Firefly, and the way home.** Who Minji Lin is, how she could *"elect"* the Titans, and whether the hosts keep their promise to return the team to the moment it left.
6. **The Destined errand.** Still the largest single swing in the document, for Lock-Step and now for Quasar-Force, and with a newcomer on record against it. If the Destined have killed the lab's staff by the time the team returns, §1 (Heroism among it), §2 and §9 move for both.
7. **The formation.** Edge asked for one and was refused. Whether it gets trained at home — and who wins the argument when it is asked again — decides §3.2, §5.14 and finding 24.
8. **Oracle's verdict on the adults.** *"Most of the people telling us what to do don't know what they're doing"* is one sentence. If it becomes a stance, §1.3, §3.6 and §9.7–9.10 move; if he says it to Nightwing, it moves differently.
9. **The leaks.** Another universe's Titans now know that Quasar-Force will not age, where his father works, and Lock-Step's first name, and so does whoever was behind the cameras. If any of it travels home, finding 16 becomes a §8 matter.
10. **The flip, and third place.** If the fame-inclusive flip holds again, the two methods disagree on first for a fourth revision. On third, Edge leads Lock-Step by 0.92 on score and 0.17 on position: it would take a session of deep negatives for the newcomer to lose it, and he has just had his first.
